what goes up a road; the ninth, a man of about twenty-two with a handcart who counts what he carries in fives because he is paid by the hour; the tenth, a woman of about fifty-eight who has swept that yard at the fourth hour every morning for about nineteen years and counts the men that come into it. All three on the twenty-ninth, day 1084, a Sunday.** **And the eleventh, Ivo Serrat, seventeen, who carries glass and can read figures and not words, on the same day, at about the seventh hour, with a hand-basket and nothing in it to deliver.** **No two of the eleven have ever been in a room with one another. The one possible exception is a woman who has put a plate in front of the boy of seventeen about four hundred times in four years and has never once asked him a question.** Four copies of the receipt were handed out at dusk on the second, to every person in that yard who had come in from off the road, and there were four of them, and **the man who wrote them does not know how many he wrote out.**

1070. **The boy's arithmetic, and it is spent.** **Nine worn places on the lower board of the shelf, about the width of a hand, in a line down the middle of it, paler than the wood either side and about nine years old, and the top board bare, and the reason the top board is bare is that a string does not wear a board and a packet is what a string is for.** Nine of about forty is about three hundred and sixty. **About three hundred and sixty sheets went out of that building on two handcarts on the morning of the twenty-third, on top of a stool, a day-book and a dressed stone, and nobody counted them, and about forty-one went out on a satchel on the second, and the receipt is accurate and is a receipt for about a ninth of what was in the building.** He said it out loud to nobody in twenty-two words and then wrote it in a book of about four hundred entries he made himself out of sheets he buys and folds, on a page of figures with no head on it, **and the book has never been shown to anybody and was not shown to anybody on the twenty-ninth, and a man of thirty-two with a limp was in that yard at about the seventh hour and did not see him count the board and will not be told.** The two collections stand at ten marks in common, the tenth in a file two hundred miles off, and **nobody in this block explained the mark, pointed at the mason's mark, or asked the boy to read his book out loud, and a mason of about sixty-eight who has found a ninth way of cutting a mark in the bed of a stone did not tell anybody.**

1071. **The figure with no head on it, and the hand, and the count of four.** **About three hundred and seventy figures on four feet of board with no head on it, at about nine to the inch down a column about forty inches long, thirty-one years, three hands, and about four of them at the foot of the column in the hand of a man who was about twenty-three about eleven years ago and used to come in four days a week with a load of glass, and the last of the four is about eleven years old.** A woman of about fifty cannot read and read the paper's four lines as a hand inside about nine minutes, **because a two has a flat foot on it and a seven has a stroke through the bar, and a figure with no head cannot be refused because there is no line to refuse it on and cannot be handed back because there is nothing to put in the hand, and she is the only person alive who could say which of the three hundred and seventy belong to the man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and she has not said it and is not going to.** He is named on a receipt in four counties and four of his figures are in a kitchen nine miles on with no head on them, and **neither of those two things can find the other one, and the one man who can see both of them at once is a man of thirty-two with a limp on no list in a room that is let by the day, and he has not said one word about it to anybody in forty-one days and there is nobody he could say it to.**

1072. **The System panel's remaining absence, and the three words at zero.** **The volume's one panel was printed in Chapter 426 and is spent, and this block prints no panel and no second panel.** A sweep of the ten new files, whole word, case-insensitive, returns **`stage` 0, `ledger` 0, `qualification` 0, `system` 0, `panel` 0**, and the same sweep over the block's own running text finds no chapter that says the ledger does or does not move. **Stage 4 is half-spent, the qualification is not earned and not claimed, and the ledger does not move.** No cut is made in this volume and none is scheduled, and the outline's crossing budget of one crossing in Block 4 was not spent and nothing in the block approaches the mechanism, the roadmark, the leaf with a seventh line that says *a person*, either line in a stone, the marginal instruction, the word in front of a place name, or the account of the worst winter in eighteen years.

1073. **The protagonist's own sheet, and whether anybody came near it.** **Nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and against each of the three the name of a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, and nothing else, in the inside pocket of a coat.** **Nobody in this block came near it. It is not shown, not lost, not read, not asked for, and no clerk of about nineteen asked him for it.** **The notebook is a different object and it is open four times in ten chapters and stays empty: eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them, the eleventh line has never had anything on it, there is no twelfth line, and he does not add one on the twenty-third of Fallowmonth when he works out that he is the connection, and he does not add one on the thirteenth of Embermonth when he decides not to make a list, and he turns that notebook over once and puts it back in the coat.** **The three days bought him the sheet, three lines and no head, and about nine counties, and that is the last column he keeps before the winter.**

1074. **The bar four miles west, and its figures derived on the page and not invented.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, down on the other three, **and the man of about nineteen is paid nine pence a day for three of the four up-days and the one he is not paid for is the Sunday.** **The start of it is day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, and the chain is run forward on the page in Chapter 438: day 1080 is three hundred and one days after 779, which is forty-three weeks exactly, forty-three weeks of four up-days in seven is a hundred and seventy-two, the Sundays in forty-three weeks are forty-three, and a hundred and seventy-two less forty-three is a hundred and twenty-nine.** **It cross-checks against Chapter 429: two hundred and fifty-nine days on the thirteenth of Harvestmonth was thirty-seven weeks and a hundred and eleven paid days, this is six weeks after that, six weeks is forty-two days, two hundred and fifty-nine and forty-two is three hundred and one, and a hundred and eleven and eighteen is a hundred and twenty-nine.** **A hundred and twenty-nine twists of nine pence is a thousand one hundred and sixty-one pence, and a mark is forty-eight pence, and that is twenty-four marks and nine pence, and the two figures are printed outside the day's column and are added to nothing.** The sheet under the stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven has been there since the fourteenth of Greenmonth, day 949, **and it is about a hundred and thirty-one days old on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth, and it has not been read, and the man of about thirty-three did not open the other one either, and he looked at one of the two on the twenty-fifth and not at the other and the rule held.** **The three bars remain three objects and only this one is touched in this block, and Chapter 438 calls it a bar and not a kerb. The man of about nineteen's four months came out on the twenty-fourth of a month in Thawmonth and this block does not say it to him, and about eight people in that town of about two thousand are right or wrong about it and not one of them has said a word.**

1075. **The two pieces of paper in that county, and whether either was ever read.** **Both were read by nobody. The first is under a stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven since the fourteenth of Greenmonth and about a hundred and thirty-one days old on the twenty-fifth of Fallowmonth. The second is in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his and has been since a fixed Saturday in Rainmonth, day 999, which is eighty-one days before the twenty-fifth, and it was not opened on the twenty-fifth. A third piece of paper went into that coat on the second of Fallowmonth and was opened, and it is a receipt in a fair hand and not one of the two, and he read every word of it and could do nothing with it and put it back on top of the other one.** Not one person in four counties can make anything of either of the two. **And the man of about nineteen's copy of the receipt has been in the inside of his coat since the second of Fallowmonth, twenty-three days on the twenty-fifth, and it has not been read by him and will not be, and nobody has ever asked him anything since a man with a barrow asked him one question on the thirteenth of Harvestmonth and believed him.**

1076. **The name budget, with the arithmetic.** **The budget for Volume 09 is five. Batch 0001 spent three and two slots remain for the whole volume. Batch 0002 spent none and Batch 0003 spent none. This block's share is at most one and this block spent none: three of five spent, two remaining, and both are still available to the close.** The three are `Brenna Tull`, 44, `Saber Vahl`, 68, and `Ivo Serrat`, 17, and all three are on the page. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Ivo Serrat` once, in Chapter 439, and one sentence opening, and every hit was read before the sweep was reported.** **No new name was spent and none was needed, because the eleven witnesses were reached by age and by trade: about forty-four and forty-one and sixty-eight and fifty and thirty-seven and thirty-three and nineteen and twenty-six and twenty-two and fifty-eight and seventeen.** The two this block was told not to use, a woman of about twenty-nine and a woman of about thirty-nine, are not in it and neither of their wants is restated.

1077. **The speech-length figures this block prints, and their footed lengths.** **Fourteen printed figures and the first printing of this item certified all fourteen footed, and that certificate was false: thirteen of fourteen footed and one did not, and the fourteenth is Chapter 431's second figure and it is repaired. The count of printed figures is fourteen, none is preceded by *about*, and the word *about* before a figure attached to a printed speech is at zero across the ten files.** **Chapter 431 prints two: sixteen words for the whole of the first exchange, of which eleven and five, and twenty-two words for the whole of the five printed lines of the sixth-hour exchange, and the sentence around the second one now says the man with the sheet said his share of them, which is what a two-speaker exchange is.** Chapter 432 prints twenty-seven. Chapter 433 prints twenty-three. Chapter 434 prints twenty-one, twice, the second a back-reference to the first. Chapter 435 prints forty-two. Chapter 436 prints seven and twenty-four. Chapter 437 prints eleven and seventeen, the seventeen being a sentence of narration that is set out and footed on the page. Chapter 438 prints forty. Chapter 439 prints twenty-two. Chapter 440 prints twenty-one, a back-reference to Chapter 434. Every one was recounted from the speech as printed with the speaker's attribution excluded, and **the block adds nothing to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and nothing to the list of protected unprinted figures, and the count of the looks at a leaf that says *a person* is not printed and is not looked at.** Two figures were wrong in the first printing and both are repaired: Chapter 438's twenty words against a printed speech of forty, and Chapter 431's fifteen words against a printed exchange of twenty-two. **All thirteen of the others were recounted one at a time off the page for this repair and the footed lengths are as printed above.** The repair is item 1081.

1078. **The money columns, and their footed totals.** **Ten columns, ten footed in pence on the page, ten distinct totals: 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68 and 69 pence, in Chapters 431 to 440 in order.** **None is one of the forty recurring totals and none is one of Batch 0002's ten at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56 and 70 or Batch 0003's ten at 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144 and 34.** A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page: thirty-seven is under one, fifty is two pence over one, forty-four is four pence under one, fifty-nine is eleven pence over one, forty-six is two pence under one, forty-three is five pence under one, seventy-eight is a mark and thirty pence over, sixty-four is a mark and sixteen pence over, sixty-eight is a mark and twenty pence over, and sixty-nine is a mark and twenty-one pence over. **Chapter 437's column is itemised on the page and each line is footed in the line: twenty-seven, four, four, six, three, six, eight, twelve and eight is seventy-eight.** **The bar's own money, a thousand one hundred and sixty-one pence, is printed outside Chapter 438's day's column and is added to nothing, and the two are not in one column.** **The pair of a boy's boots at twenty-six pence is not reprinted in this block at all, and the figure does not appear in any register.** No rate is paid in salt anywhere in the block and no conversion was required and none is printed, and the corridor's rate is not printed and nobody puts it in a column, and the two prices of the same thing are never added in any register including a notebook. **The two counts of the sheets in Chapter 432 are counts of sheets and not of money and are not in any column.**

1079. **The checks, each on a named basis, and what each one returned.** **The calendar, by derivation, twice, from the anchor of day 930 on the carried thirty-day month, and the two halves returned as two numbers and not as one: the weekday half 0 mismatches over 10 datelines, the day-of-month half 0 mismatches over 11 dated references, and the market-morning claim 0 mismatches over 11 day-claims. A single figure for two derivations is one derivation, and this block reports three.** **The money, read on the page and not from a state file: 9 single-line columns matched and footed and 1 itemised column footed line by line, 10 distinct totals, 0 collisions with the twenty spent Batch 0002 and Batch 0003 columns, and each column read against the column above it and not only against the total below it.** **Speech-length figures, counted speech by speech from the printed words with the speaker's attribution excluded: 14 printed, 13 of 14 foot in the printing this item first reported, 0 preceded by *about*, and the fourteenth was Chapter 431's second figure at fifteen against a printed exchange of twenty-two and is repaired and the recount is item 1081.** **Raw-token, headings stripped, bold datelines in, twelve-word seed with maximal extension on a per-file index, the ten new against all 430 prior chapter files: the longest maximal shared run is 26 words, and it is the dateline frame of Chapter 439 against `chapter-0430.md`; every other run above eighteen words is the dateline frame, the house footing sentence for a counted speech, the room's own series wording at eighteen words, the form's own wording about the three heads, or the fen town's four-hundred-yard street, and all six are declared and not re-voiced. Four non-frame runs between eighteen and twenty words were found and all four were re-voiced: the carter of about twenty-six in Chapter 439 against `chapter-0430.md`, the made road and the four counties in Chapter 438 against `chapter-0429.md`, the man of about thirty-four and the twists in Chapter 438 against `chapter-0429.md`, and the four seconds of silence in Chapter 434 against `chapter-0429.md`. The ten new against themselves: the longest maximal shared run is 18 words, being the volume's own three things in the same order, which is under the thirty-word line and is the block's motif.** **Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more, the ten new against the whole manuscript as it stood at the time of the run, which is 430 chapter files, and against themselves: 0 and 0.** **Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file; `---` rules 0; double blank lines 0; a doubled-word sweep 0; a space-after-a-comma sweep 0; a space-before-a-full-stop sweep 0; a working-note sweep 0.** **The twelve barred tokens and the four barred numerals, whole word, every hit read and not only counted, at zero across the ten files, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` at zero. Across 440 files `hemp` is at 70, `thirty-six` at 20, `fifty-two` at 102, `sixty-one` at 142 and `sixty-six` at 249, and the four barred numerals in the ten new files are at zero.** **A self-reference sweep across twenty-four patterns testing the bare noun: `this volume`, `the volume`, `this book`, `this chapter`, `the chapter`, `the chapters`, `this block`, `the block`, `this batch`, `this manuscript`, `this narrator`, `this novel`, `this story`, `this series`, `this prompt`, `this outline`, `this file`, `this writer`, `about four hundred chapters`, `about four hundred and twenty`, `spent thirty chapters`, `thirty chapters`, `this has` and `the manuscript` are all at zero, and the three hits for `in this` and the two for `of this` are world objects, being a column, a gate and a month, and every one was read.** **Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes 0, 0, 0, 0 across the ten files, and the house convention is kept and is not a quota.** **A figure audit, every figure of people, days, money, distance, length and time read against its chapter and against the chapter three away: the four men, the about forty-one sheets, the two counts, the eleven witnesses, the nine worn places, the nine packets, the three handcart figures and the two descriptions of the store agree, and three figures were wrong in the first printing and are repaired, being the second of a month for a third of a month in Chapter 437, eleven days for four weeks in Chapter 440, and about three months for six weeks in Chapter 438.**

1080. **What the block cost, and the name it has.** **A man of thirty-two with a limp connected eleven people in eleven places and was not the doer of anything and got none of them back, and he knows eleven things he cannot put anywhere, and the reason he does not make a list is printed on the page and is not a reason anybody would call decent, because a list of eleven people in nine places is a column, and a column is a thing that can be picked up by the next man with a form, and a man with a form is not a bad man and is doing his work.** He is on no list in nine counties. He was offered one end of a handcart thirty feet from a gate and said no in one word, **because a man who helped is a man who was there on purpose, and a witness is a thing a person is and not a thing a person is put in.** He gave a fire to a man of about twenty at the eighth hour of a Sunday and it is the only thing he gave anybody in nine counties in about four months that was not information. **The breach between the two men is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure, the word for what the two of them are is printed nowhere in the block and the barred kin-term is at two across four hundred and forty chapters and is spent.** The antagonist is not named in any of the ten chapters, in the outline, or in any state file this block wrote, and nothing in the block revives the Volume 08 cooperation.

## Volume 09, Batch 0004 — repair pass after review

**This section was written by a repair phase and not by the phase that wrote the block. It changed one line in one chapter and four state files and it restarted nothing. It resolved a number and a sentence that went with the number, and it resolved nothing the block left open on purpose. The plot is unchanged, the ten days are unchanged, the eleven witnesses are unchanged, the receipt is unchanged, the three invented things are unchanged, the bench-board is still ruled and empty, the System panel is still spent, and the antagonist is still named nowhere. Items continue from 1080 and are never renumbered, and the two items above that carried a false certificate are corrected in place and are not renumbered to hide it.**

1081. **The Batch 0004 repair after its review: what was found, what was changed, what was only a note, and what a successor must not undo.** **Two defects in the pages and one broken handoff, and the pages were one line long between them.** **One, Chapter 0431's second speech-length figure.** The first printing of that exchange was six words and correct, and the block's own repair pass had widened the figure to cover the whole five-line exchange and had put **fifteen** against it, and the sum of the five printed lines is **twenty-two** (three, one, six, six, six), and no subset of them is fifteen. The sentence around it also said *he said every one of them*, which is a claim about a two-speaker exchange that only one of the two made. **Both are repaired in the one line: the figure is twenty-two and the sentence says he said his share of every one of them.** The other figure in that chapter, sixteen words for the first exchange of eleven and five, foots and was not touched. **Two, Chapter 0431's certificate in items 1077 and 1079 is withdrawn, and thirteen of the block's fourteen printed speech figures were footed and one was not.** All fourteen were recounted one at a time off the page for this repair and the other thirteen foot at sixteen, twenty-two, twenty-seven, twenty-three, twenty-one, forty-two, seven, twenty-four, eleven, seventeen, forty, twenty-two and twenty-one, and the two figures that are printed twice are both consistent with their referents. **Three, item 1079's self-reference count was wrong in the other direction and is corrected: three hits for `in this` and two for `of this`, not five and two, and all five are world objects, being a column twice, a gate and a month.** **And the three money columns' certificate stands, and was re-read for this repair rather than inherited: ten columns, ten footed in pence on the page, ten distinct totals at 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68 and 69, and Chapter 437's itemised column foots line by line as well as in its total, and no total collides with the twenty spent in Batches 0002 and 0003.** The calendar certificate also stands, re-derived for this repair from the single anchor of day 930 on the carried thirty-day month: **the weekday half and the day-of-month half are 0 and 0 as two numbers and the market-morning claim is 0 as a third, over the ten datelines at 1057, 1057 and 1058, 1062, 1068, 1071, 1074, 1078, 1080, 1084 and 1098.**

1082. **The three things this pass did not do, and the two it checked instead of changing.** **It did not edit `outline/volume-09.md`.** The volume's §17 table gives day 1098 as a Saturday and a market day and day 1099 as a Sunday, and on the carried thirty-day month from the day-930 anchor 1098 is a Sunday and is not a market morning and 1099 is a Monday, and Chapter 0440 prints 1098 as **a Sunday and not a market day** and foots. **The chapter is right and the table is one day out on those two rows, and the third, 965, is at item 1047.** A batch is not the place to correct a plan and a repair is not a better place, and `workspace/volume-09/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` §2 already instructs the close to derive the first day of its block and not to copy either row, **so the correction is recorded here, at item 1047, at `state/open-threads.md` 19 and 38, and in the handoff, and the table is left for whoever owns the outline.** **It did not touch the plan, the block's argument, its ten days, its eleven witnesses, its three columns, or any of the eleven defect classes the block's own pass worked through, and it added no scene, no speech, no character, no name and no fact about the world.** It did not mark, retire or create a prompt, and it did not write a `.done`.

1083. **One review note that is wrong, recorded here so that it is not inherited as canon, because a false correction is a defect of the same class as a false certificate.** The review of Batch 0004 reported that the claim in `workspace/volume-09/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` and in `state/batch-summaries.md` — that this block's repair pass removed **a month that does not exist in this calendar** — appeared to be a misattribution, on the ground that no such name survives in the current text and that the repair pass touches no month name. **The first half of that is right and the second half is not.** `git show 6c620ab -- chapters/volume-09/chapter-0431.md` carries the removal in full: the first printing of the yard paragraph in Chapter 431 said that the eleven men *knew what a cleared yard had to look like by the fourth of September and not before*, and the repair changed it to *knew what a cleared yard has to look like by the first week of the month the court sits in and not a week before*. `September` is not one of the twelve months in `bible/terminology.md`, **the repair pass did touch a month name, and the claim in the prompt and in the batch summary is true and is not to be withdrawn.** The same pass's other month work is on the page: a market morning on a Friday, a wrong month for a boy's boots, and a wrong month and a wrong interval in three more places. **A month name is found by reading a sentence and not by a calendar check, because a calendar check asks whether a date is right and not whether a month is a month, and this block is the fourteenth batch running that and the first one to have a reviewer doubt its own ledger about it.**

1084. **What this pass changed, counted, and the standing lesson.** **One line in one chapter in ten, and four state files, and one clause in the next prompt.** The block moved from 18,094 words to **18,097** on the declared basis, Chapter 431 from 1,750 to **1,753**, the volume from 77,775 to **77,778** in forty chapters, and the manuscript from 1,367,029 to **1,367,032** in four hundred and forty chapters, **and all four figures were recounted on the chapter files and not read off this item.** The `about` count is 386 in 18,097, which is 21.33 per 1,000, and it did not move. **The lesson is the same one this file has now run three times and it is a lesson about a certificate and not about a number: the block's own pass repaired Chapter 438's twenty into forty, wrote the repair down correctly, and then wrote *fourteen printed figures, fourteen footed* about a set of figures it had not all counted, and the one it had not counted was in the same chapter it had just repaired, two paragraphs up.** Nine defects in Batch 0002 were caught by hand, eleven in Batch 0003 and a twelfth by a later pass, and Batch 0004 found eighty-eight lines in ten chapters and then, after a review, one more, **and the cheapest of the eighty-nine was a number that did not add up and the second cheapest was a state file that said it did.**

## Volume 09 Batch 0005, Chapters 441 to 450 — the close

**This section is written by the phase that wrote the last block of Volume 09 and it continues from 1084. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's own repair pass and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn. The highest number in this file is now 1100.**

1085. **The frame, derived twice from the single anchor, and the two halves reported as two numbers.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor. **The ten chapter days are 1099, 1103, 1104, 1110, 1113, 1115, 1126, 1130, 1133 and 1140, which are the fourteenth, the eighteenth, the nineteenth, the twenty-fifth, the twenty-eighth and the thirtieth of Embermonth and the eleventh, the fifteenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, and they are a Monday, a Friday, a Saturday, a Friday, a Monday, a Wednesday, a Sunday, a Thursday, a Sunday and a Sunday.** The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately, each from the day number alone and not from the other half, and **they are reported as three figures and not as one: the weekday half is 0 mismatches over ten datelines, the day-of-month half is 0 mismatches over ten datelines, and the market-morning claim is 0 mismatches over ten day-claims.** Chapter 0446's dateline carries a second date, **day 1117, the second of Wolfmonth, a Friday, not a market day**, and that is the eleventh dated reference and it foots. **Two of the ten chapters fall on a market morning, being Chapter 443 on the Saturday the nineteenth of Embermonth and Chapter 448 on the Thursday the fifteenth of Wolfmonth, and the bar four miles west of Marl is down on one of those two and up on the other, because it comes up on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday.** Month boundaries inside the block: 1116, the first of Wolfmonth, a Thursday and a market morning, which Chapter 0446 uses as an ordinary market the man of about thirty-two with a limp walks through and schedules nothing for. **The whole of Volume 09 is YR 318 and the year does not turn: Thawmonth 1 YR 318 is day 876, Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236, and the volume ends on day 1140, which is the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth and is a Sunday and not a market day.** **`outline/volume-09.md` §17's rows for 1098 and 1099 were not copied and are both one day out, and the 965 row is one day out, and this block derived all three: 1098 is a Sunday and is not a market morning, 1099 is a Monday, and 965 is a Sunday. The plan is the volume's authority and this phase did not edit it.**

1086. **The nine who are in the room, and what each of them refused, and what the nine agreed.** **A woman of about forty-four who runs the second of four lime kilns** refused to be the person anybody can be sent to, because a kiln is not a person and then she is not a person either. **A man of about forty-one who walks two miles and nine stones** refused to say where the stones are in a room, and that refusal is the reason the nine have no address. **A man of about twenty-two with a handcart**, hired by the hour, refused to be the ninth of nine, because the day a thing is his he has to put it somewhere and he has not got a shelf. **A mason of about sixty-eight at a single arch over a beck** refused to be asked in a room at all, having been asked in a kitchen about four hundred times by strangers. **A man of about thirty-three with a barrow** refused to open or hand over a paper in a coat that is not his. **A woman of about fifty who keeps a roadhouse and has four feet of board with about three hundred and seventy figures on it and no head on it** refused to let the room become a reason to read the board. **A man of about nineteen at a bar four miles west of Marl** refused to be asked a second question. **A woman of about fifty-eight who sweeps a yard behind a house of assizes** refused to let a count of men in a yard leave the yard, and she came with a slate and asked that it be written down once, anywhere, by anybody, and the room did not give it. **A woman of about seventeen who carries glass between four kilns** refused to show a book of about four hundred entries she has made herself and has never shown in four years. **A man of about thirty-four with a net who keeps the room's own list** is the ninth. **A man of about thirty-two with a limp was the tenth from the fourteenth to the thirtieth of Embermonth and told four of the nine himself, and he was asked to leave by two of them and he left, and he is not in the room on either of the last two days.** What they agreed, in seven words on the eighteenth of Embermonth and not on any sheet, is that **nothing about it goes on any sheet**, and then that each of the nine keeps whatever they write or do not write and none of the nine sends it to the other eight, and that there is no room in nine counties in which any two of them can be found twice running.

1087. **The ninth word, and who it was handed to, and who was asked and did not say it.** **The ninth word is `Coldharbour` and it is a road nine miles long and it is out.** It was said out loud in a market in another county in a month in Greenmonth, about five months before the nineteenth of Embermonth, and about forty people heard it and about nine of them heard it properly and a man of about thirty has it on the back of his own hand under a bar of soap. **On the nineteenth of Embermonth a woman of about fifty with a board said the word once in a room of nine and the man of about twenty-two with a handcart said it back three times, and then said that he is not keeping it, and that handing is Chapter 0443.** He holds no practice, he is on no list in the county the bar is in or the county the kilns are in or on any county's own series, and about nine hundred sheets a month go out of a room over a weigh-house with a head on them and he is not one of the nine hundred. **He said it again on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, in front of eight people, without being asked to repeat it, and said that he has not written it down, has not got anywhere to write it, and will say it again on a road in ten years.** **The man of about nineteen at the bar was asked on the same Sunday and said no, in twenty-nine words, and gave the same answer he gave on the nineteenth of Embermonth, which is that being asked in a room in front of eight people is a different kind of being asked.** The mason of about sixty-eight was in that room and was not asked. **A woman of about thirty-seven with a plate on a bracket over her door is in neither room and has not been told and there is no form on which she could be.** Nothing was recalled, nobody regretted it in a speech, no tenth line was added, and the volume's twenty-word engine sentence was not reprinted.

1088. **The leaving, and what it cost, and what it did not cost.** **On the twenty-eighth of Embermonth, a Monday, the man of about nineteen at the bar and the woman of about seventeen who carries glass asked a man of about thirty-two with a limp to leave a room, and he said in six words that he would be gone on Wednesday, and he did not argue.** The man of about nineteen's reason is in a receipt in the inside of his coat that carries a place, a figure of men and a trade and a name at the foot of it, and a boy at the end of a bar is findable through a man who is in a room. The woman of about seventeen's reason is that a gate is a place, a place is the first of the three heads on a form, and she had said three things about a gate on the twenty-fifth of Embermonth to a room of nine. **The argument he did not make is the whole of Chapter 0445 and is set out in the chapter as what happened in his head and not in the room, and it is this: that he told four of the nine himself, that he connected eleven people in eleven places in nine counties in about four months, that not one of the eleven has ever been in a room with another of the eleven, that he is the only person in nine counties who can see the receipt and the board at once, that a man who can see both is a man in every room in nine counties at once, and that a man in every room in nine counties at once is a man in a column.** What it cost: the one room in nine counties where nine people had heard each other, and eight people who let a correct thing stand without defending him, and a man of about thirty-four with a net who opened his mouth twice and shut it. What it did not cost: nothing that can be put in a column, no list, no copy, no sheet, and none of the eleven. **He left at about the eleventh hour on the thirtieth of Embermonth, the room was given up at dusk, the woman of about sixty-three who keeps that room's book was not in it and has not been told, and the nine went on meeting twice more and then stopped. The leaving is not written as a defeat and the pages do not explain it and do not admire it. Nothing in this block says that the ledger does or does not move, and a sweep of the ten files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*.**

1089. **The woman of about seventeen, the identification, and the fact that nothing was opened.** **On the twenty-fifth of Embermonth, a Friday, a woman of about seventeen who carries glass between four kilns and can read figures and not words said three things in a room of nine and was asked nothing.** She said that there is a road nine miles long about nine miles west of the room, that there has been a gate at the end of it for about four hundred years, that the road has not been a road anybody uses for about four hundred years, and that there are people on the far side of that gate, and that a woman is on the other side of that gate and she is her mother, and that she came to this side when she was nine and has not been down the far half since she was nine. **The identification of that road as a protected and unopened continuation is by an arithmetic and not by a crossing, and the arithmetic was made, identified and closed in Batch 0003 and Batch 0004 and is not re-spent here: the chapter states what it identified and does not show the making, and the book of about four hundred entries in it was not shown, was not read out, and was not asked for, and the not asking is the ninth thing that book has never been shown for.** Nobody sent anybody. **Nothing was opened. The gate is exactly where it was on the twenty-fifth of Embermonth and no chapter in this block schedules anything for it.** A man of about twenty-two with a handcart said, in a hundred and sixty-eight words across two turns of one speech, that he is the one person in the room who could go and stand at it and that he is not going to, **and the last turn of that speech alone is sixty-seven words and the chapter prints both figures and says which is which, because a figure attached to two printed turns is a figure about every printed line of it**, and the room did not ask him twice. No figure of the far side of that gate has ever been printed on anything at all, and about thirty-four people live in a field and a lane at this end of the road, and the two were not compared on any page.

1090. **The keeper of records, the question from a road, and a decision that was hers.** **Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, in a room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles off, in one room on the eleventh of Wolfmonth, a Sunday, which is the only chapter of the ten with no man of thirty-two with a limp in it and one of two with no protagonist in it at all.** **A drawer, the second down on the left, about eleven inches deep, holds about four hundred returns and nothing else, and nine words came back off a road about two days off a coast in a hand that is not a clerk's hand, with no letter and no seal and a place in that county written on the back of it: *Is the first of the eight still done anywhere.* That is nine words. It went into the drawer on the fourteenth of a month in Longlight, which was a Sunday, and on the Sunday this chapter is set on it has been there a hundred and forty-seven days, which is twenty-one weeks exactly and no day over, and she has it by the Saturday count on a weigh-house wall and checked it twice on the Saturday and once on the Sunday. **At the volume's last day it is a hundred and sixty-one days, which is twenty-three weeks exactly, and that is the figure the Volume 10 handoff carries.** She has not answered it in twenty-one weeks at the day this chapter is set on and in twenty-three weeks by day 1140, and her not answering it is the only thing she has done wrong in this volume. **She does not know what the first of the eight is, and that is why a keeper of records cannot answer it, and the answer is nine miles from her door: a mason of about sixty-eight at a single arch over a beck who has asked the far side of it about four hundred times, the last time of all on the seventh of a month in Rainmonth, to a stranger who did not carry the answer anywhere.** **Her decision, made at about the sixth hour of that Sunday and told to nobody: she wrote four lines on a sheet describing what that room holds, she did not put a name at the foot of it, she did not put the nine words into the round, and she put the four lines into the bag with the letters for the cart on the fixed day.** A record with a place, a number of men and a trade on it is the shape of about nine hundred sheets a month that she has been the woman who sends for four years and a half, and a sheet with no name on it cannot be got at and cannot be enforced and cannot be corrected. **She is not going to tell him about it and he is not going to hear about it, and the question is still in the drawer, and the answer is still nine miles off, and nobody carried it in twenty-one weeks by the day of that Sunday and nobody is going to.**

1091. **The four unpaid things, and nobody's remark on any of them.** **A trade, a figure of four hundred and seventy, a sheet in a coat, and one sentence carried four miles: all four are in a market in a market town of about two thousand people on the salt on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and not one character in that chapter and no narrator's sentence gathers them or remarks on them.** The trade of salt carting was said out loud at about the seventh hour by three carters talking about hay. About four hundred and seventy went down past a stile to the nine-mile road in a day and a half, said by a woman at a fish stall to a man who laughed at her. A paper in the inside pocket of a coat on the back of a barrow had been there a hundred and thirty-one days and was not offered and was not asked about. A man of about fifty-one finished a drain arch in a village in the first week of a month in Wolfmonth and took his money on the ninth, and four miles off a mason of about sixty-eight did not come and does not know, and the drain man does not know there is a mason four miles off, and neither has the other's name. **Nobody said one word to the protagonist about any of the four in this volume, none of the four was done, and the close did not remark on any of them, which is the whole of the chapter and is also its instruction.**

1092. **The protagonist's own sheet, and the notebook, and whether anybody came near either.** **Nobody in this block came near either. The sheet is nine inches by six, three lines, no head, carrying the twenty-second, the twenty-fourth and the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth and one name against each, and it is the only record in the world of when three things were entered on a form and by whom, and it is in the inside pocket of a coat, and it was not shown, not lost, not read, not asked for, and no clerk of about nineteen asked him for it, and he wrote nothing about the three things on it and did not add a date and did not add a name.** The notebook is a different object: eleven lines on a page with no words on any of them, the eleventh line never used, no twelfth line, opened in Chapter 0446 and not taken out of the coat. **The separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at in this block and the count of the looks at it is not printed.** Two unreconciled numbers stand in the coat unreconciled. His notebook, his eleven lines and his six words in a room eleven paces by nineteen are the whole of what he said in ten chapters.

1093. **The bar four miles west of Marl, and every figure derived from Chapters 405, 410, 418, 429 and 438 and not invented.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, down on the other three, **and the man of about nineteen is paid nine pence a day for three of the four and the Sunday is the one he is not paid for, and the start is day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, which was a Wednesday.** The canon chain is 170 days on 949, 193 on 972, 228 on 1007, 259 on 1038 and 301 on 1080, and the method used here is the method of Chapter 0438, an interval of N days taken as whole weeks of four up-days in seven with the Sundays taken out and the remainder taken from the weekday of the start day. **That method reproduces four prior canon figures exactly and they are the check: 1080 gives a hundred and twenty-nine paid days, 1,161 pence, twenty-four marks and nine pence; 1038 gives a hundred and eleven and nine hundred and ninety-nine pence; 972 gives eighty-three; 949 gives seventy-three.** On the fifteenth of Wolfmonth, day 1130, it is **three hundred and fifty-one days, fifty weeks and one day, fifty weeks of four up-days in seven is two hundred, the Sundays in fifty weeks are fifty, two hundred less fifty is a hundred and fifty, and the one day over is a Wednesday, so a hundred and fifty-one paid days, and it cross-checks from a hundred and twenty-nine at 1080 across fifty days, which is seven weeks and one day, giving a hundred and fifty and then a hundred and fifty-one. A hundred and fifty-one twists of paper at nine pence is one thousand three hundred and fifty-nine pence, which is twenty-eight marks and fifteen pence at forty-eight pence a mark, and that is printed outside Chapter 0448's day's column and added to nothing.** On the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, day 1140, it is **three hundred and sixty-one days, fifty-one weeks and four days, two hundred and four less fifty-one is a hundred and fifty-three, and the four days over are a Wednesday, a Thursday, a Friday and a Saturday, of which a Wednesday and a Friday are up and paid, so a hundred and fifty-five paid days, one thousand three hundred and ninety-five pence, twenty-nine marks and three pence.** The bar is down on the day of Chapter 0448 and the boy is at a market two miles east of it, and the bar comes down at the second hour and goes up at the sixth. **The three bars remain three objects and only this one was touched in this block, and the kerb bar and the market bar at Marl were not touched.**

1094. **The two sheets in that county and the third, and whether any of them was ever read.** **None of them was read and none was opened in this block.** A sheet with about nine figures and no head and a date at the foot has been under a stone on a bench in a shed nine feet by seven since the fourteenth of a month in Greenmonth, day 949, **and it is a hundred and eighty-one days old on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and a hundred and ninety-one days old on the twenty-fifth.** A fold of about nine figures with no head has been in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his since a fixed Saturday in a month in Rainmonth, day 999, **and it is a hundred and thirty-one days old on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and a hundred and forty-one days old on the twenty-fifth, and in Chapter 0441, on day 1099, the man of about thirty-three with a barrow himself says it has been in that coat a hundred days.** A receipt in a fair hand has been in the inside of the boy of nineteenth's own coat since the second of a month in Fallowmonth, day 1057, **and it is seventy-three days old on the fifteenth of Wolfmonth and eighty-three days old on the twenty-fifth, and he cannot read it and nobody has ever read it for him.** Not one person in four counties can make anything of the first two. **No one was asked a third question and the boy was not asked to read anything and nothing was opened.**

1095. **The three collections with no head on them, and whether any of them was read in this block.** **Four feet of board with about three hundred and seventy figures and no head, thirty-one years, three hands, in a kitchen nine miles on from the kilns: it was not read out, not taken off the wall and not improved on, and the woman of about fifty who keeps it said nothing about it in a room on the eighteenth of Wolfmonth and was not thanked by anybody and has never been thanked by anybody.** About four hundred entries in four years in a book a boy of seventeen made himself: **not shown, not read out, not asked for.** Nine inches by six with three lines and no head, in an inside pocket: **not read, not asked for, not lost.** **The three were not put in one column, not printed together and not added to one another. The first two stand at ten marks in common with the tenth in a file two hundred miles off and nobody in this block explained the tenth, nobody pointed at the mason's mark, and the boy's arithmetic was not re-spent as a reveal.** The fourth hand on the board was matched on the nineteenth of a month in Fallowmonth, which is the day Chapter 0436 is set on and seventeen days after the receipt was written, and the woman who cannot read matched it in about nine minutes and has not said it and is not going to.

1096. **The name budget, with the arithmetic.** **The budget for Volume 09 is five. Batch 0001 spent three and Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 spent none: three of five spent, two remaining, and because this is the last block in the volume those two are unspent for the whole of Volume 09 and the arithmetic is 3 + 0 + 0 + 0 + 0 = 3 of 5 with nothing after it.** The three are `Brenna Tull`, 44, `Saber Vahl`, 68, and `Ivo Serrat`, 17. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns exactly two names, `Tovan Kest` in Chapter 0446 and `Mara Vey` in Chapter 0447, and both are returns and not spends: Tovan Kest is the man of twenty-five who owns the crews and the yard on Weir Street and is named in Chapter 0409, and Mara Vey is the keeper of records and is named in Chapter 0404.** Every other hit is a section heading. **The eleven witnesses were reached by age and by trade and not by name: about forty-four, forty-one, sixty-eight, fifty, thirty-three, twenty-two, nineteen, fifty-eight and seventeen, with a man of about thirty-four with a net as the ninth of the room, which is a return from Chapter 0412.** The two this block was told not to use, a woman of about twenty-nine and a woman of about thirty-nine, are not in it, neither of their wants is restated and neither of them is thanked.

1097. **The speech-length figures this block prints, and their footed lengths, recounted figure by figure off the page for the second pass and not inherited from the first pass's certificate. **Sixteen printed speech-length figures and sixteen of sixteen foot, and the word *about* precedes no figure attached to a printed speech anywhere in the ten files, and the block adds nothing to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and nothing to the list of protected unprinted figures.** Chapter 0441 prints forty-six for one speech in three turns of one speaker and eleven for the only sentence he says in a room, and both foot. Chapter 0442 prints forty-nine for one speech in three turns, forty-seven for one speech in two turns, and seven for the agreement, and all three foot. Chapter 0443 prints fifty-one for one speech in three turns. **Chapter 0444 prints eight, sixty-seven and a hundred and sixty-eight, and the two large ones are for one speech in two turns of one speaker: the second turn alone is sixty-seven and the whole of what he said at the eighth hour is a hundred and sixty-eight, and the page now says which is which, because a figure attached to two printed turns is a figure about every printed line of it.** **Chapter 0445 prints a hundred and twenty-six for one speech in three turns of one speaker, a hundred and nineteen for two separate turns of the same speaker with a stage direction between them, and six for his one sentence, and the sentence around the hundred and nineteen says *for both of the things she said* and says that both of them are printed here.** Chapter 0447 prints nine for a printed question. Chapter 0448 prints nine for a printed sentence, and the *about* in that sentence qualifies a quarter of a day and a penny and not the figure. Chapter 0450 prints twenty-nine. **Four of the sixteen were wrong in this block's first printing and all four were repaired before the files were first closed: forty-six was written as twenty-nine, a hundred and one against a printed sixty-seven, fifty-two against a printed forty-nine in one chapter and against forty-seven in another, and the two-turn figure was certified at a hundred and nineteen against the second turn alone before the sentence around it was rewritten. Three figures about unprinted speeches were also in the first printing and were cut: *in about nine words* twice and *in nine words* once.** **And the second pass corrected this item itself, which is the third time in two volumes that the certificate and not the page has been the last place a defect survived. The first pass of this item claimed *fourteen printed figures*, and there are sixteen, and it claimed Chapter 0445 prints *thirty-three and eighty-six*, and the page prints neither of those and did not in the first printing either, and a sweep for a figure a state file claims and the page never printed returns the two of them. It also carried a *sixty-seven* for Chapter 0444 where the page now carries a sixty-seven for one turn and a hundred and sixty-eight for both.**

1098. **The money columns, and their footed totals.** **Ten columns, ten footed in pence on the page before the total was said, ten distinct totals: 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89 pence, in Chapters 441 to 450 in order, and each one was read against the column above it and not only against the total below it.** **None is one of the forty-one recurring totals and none is one of Batch 0002's ten at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56 and 70, or Batch 0003's ten at 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144 and 34, or Batch 0004's ten at 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68 and 69, and the four barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six` are at zero across the ten files.** A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page: thirty is under one by eighteen, fifty-three is a mark and five over, thirty-nine is nine under one, sixty-three is a mark and fifteen over, sixty-five is a mark and seventeen over, seventy-one is a mark and twenty-three over, ninety-seven is two marks and a penny over, seventy-five is a mark and twenty-seven over, eighty-eight is a mark and forty over, eighty-nine is a mark and forty-one over. **Chapter 442's column is itemised in two lines and the first line foots in itself at four pence for four sheets of paper.** The bar's own money, one thousand three hundred and fifty-nine pence on day 1130 and one thousand three hundred and ninety-five pence on day 1140, is printed outside the day's column and added to nothing, and the two are not in one column and the two are not the same figure. **A rate printed and not footed: four pence a day for the man of about thirty-three on a cart, of which he gave up about a quarter of one day to walk four miles and say nine words, and that is not in any column.** **No rate is paid in salt anywhere in the block and no conversion was required and none is printed; the corridor's rate is not printed and nobody puts it in a column; the two prices of the same thing are never added in any register; and twenty-six pence, a pair of a boy's boots, is not reprinted in this block at all.**

1099. **The checks, each on a basis named before the run, and what each returned, ALL RE-RUN AFTER THE LAST EDIT of the second pass and none of them inherited from the first pass's certificate.** **The calendar, by derivation, twice, from the single anchor of day 930 on the carried thirty-day month, each half from the day number alone and not from the other half: the weekday half 0 mismatches, the day-of-month half 0 mismatches, and the market-morning claim 0 mismatches, reported as three figures and not as one, and a check that returns one figure for two derivations is one derivation. The run covered eleven dated references, being the ten datelines plus Chapter 0446's second date of day 1117, the second of Wolfmonth, a Friday and not a market day; Chapter 0450's dateline is a twelfth and is written in a different construction, *a Sunday, and a day the county does not trade on at all*, and it derives to day 1140, the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, a Sunday and not a market day, and it foots.** **The money, read on the page and not from a state file: ten columns footed line by line, ten distinct totals at 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, and 0 collisions with the fifty-one barred and spent totals, and each column was also read against the column above it and not only against the total below it.** **Speech-length figures, counted speech by speech from the words as printed with the speaker's attribution excluded and consecutive turns of one speaker merged: sixteen printed and sixteen foot, and 0 preceded by *about*.** **Raw-token, on three bases run side by side, each a fifteen-word seed with maximal extension on a per-file index, the bases being headings stripped with the bold markers stripped, headings stripped with the bold markers kept, and nothing stripped so that the title line is in: the ten new against all four hundred and forty prior files return a longest maximal shared run of FIFTEEN words on all three bases, 39 runs, and the ten new against the ten new return FIFTEEN words, 67 runs, and every one of both is a declared frame in kind, being the dateline frame, the money-column footing sentence, the Crown's own series wording or a house list. THE FIGURES 23 AND 28 THAT THE FIRST PASS CERTIFIED DO NOT REPRODUCE ON ANY OF THE THREE BASES, and the figure that does not reproduce is the error and not the count, and that is this item's own lesson again.** **Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more, the ten new against the whole manuscript and against the ten new: 0 and 0, and the scope is all four hundred and fifty chapter files as they stand.** **Per-line bold parity even on every line of every file; `---` rules 0; double blank lines 0; a doubled-word sweep 0; a spacing sweep 0; a comma-then-lower-then-upper sweep 0; a working-note sweep 0.** **The twelve barred tokens and the four barred numerals, whole word, case-insensitive, at 0 across the ten files, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` at 0, and the barred kin-term at 0 here and at 2 across four hundred and fifty chapters.** **A self-reference sweep across twenty-seven patterns testing the bare noun returns 0.** **Month and weekday names: every month name spoken in the ten files was resolved to a date before the day the chapter is set on, or changed into a duration. Seven month names appear and all seven are of the twelve in this calendar; `Hearthmonth` appeared twice, in Chapters 0449 and 0450, and is a month of this calendar that has not begun inside Volume 09 and cannot be resolved to any day in it, and both were changed into the canon fact that each name in the hat has the thing for a month, and no foreign month name appears and no month name that does not exist in this calendar appears.** **A capitalised two-word sweep returns two names, `Tovan Kest` in Chapter 0446 and `Mara Vey` in Chapter 0447, both established returns, and one phrase, *The Sundays*, which is not a name.** **The word counts, each file counted on its own with the title line included and the ten added, and never on a concatenated stream: Chapter 441 2,089 · 442 1,746 · 443 1,843 · 444 1,925 · 445 1,910 · 446 1,943 · 447 2,152 · 448 2,120 · 449 2,054 · 450 2,510. The block is 20,292 in ten chapters. The volume is 98,070 in fifty chapters, being 77,778 in the first forty, and the manuscript is 1,387,324 in four hundred and fifty chapters, being 1,289,254 in the first four hundred and forty.** **And the measurements, recorded because they are facts and not because they are targets: `about` 453 in 20,292, which is 22.57 per 1,000, on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator; `the whole of` 28; `about four people` 0; `---` rules 0; question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes 0, 0, 0, 0.** **A figure audit, every figure of people, days, money, distance, length and time read against its own chapter and against the chapter three away, is item 1100 and its second pass.**

1100. **What this block's own hand found, and what it cost, and the standing lesson.** **This is the fourteenth batch running the lesson and this block is the sharpest case of the stale-duration half of it.** The repair pass changed **ninety-one lines across the ten files, and every one of them was found by a person reading the files and not by a comparison between two files.** The classes are these. **One, a duration correct on the page and wrong against a date, in nine places, and this is the class a calendar check cannot see because a calendar check asks whether a date is right and not whether a figure is right for the day the chapter is on:** the boy's day count, his paid days, his pence and his marks were all four first written at the day-1140 values of 361, 155, 1,395 and twenty-nine marks and three pence in Chapter 0448, which is day 1130, and all four were repaired to 351, 151, 1,359 and twenty-eight marks and fifteen pence, and the day-1140 set was then derived again separately in Chapter 450 rather than copied; the sheet under the stone was a hundred and ninety-one days in a day-1130 chapter and is a hundred and eighty-one; the boy's own receipt was eighty-three in a day-1130 chapter and is seventy-three; the fold in the coat was a hundred and forty-one in two chapters and is a hundred days on day 1099 and a hundred and thirty-one on day 1130; a receipt he has had in his head was said to be nine weeks old at thirty-one days; a market 147 days back was said to be nine weeks back and is about five months; a field twenty-one days back was said to be nine weeks before its Sunday and is about three months. **Two, a speech-length figure wrong in four places, and a fifth certificate wrong before the sentence around it was corrected.** **Three, two figures about unprinted speeches, cut.** **Four, the barred numerals: `fifty-two` and `sixty-one` appeared four times across a speech figure and two durations and all four were re-voiced away.** **Five, a working note left in a chapter file by the writer, together with the barred token `roof`, and it is the second time this project has found one and neither instrument could see it.** **Six, six self-references, found by reading.** **Seven, four re-voicings for duplication, a thirty-five-word run against Chapter 0398, a thirty-six-word dateline run inside the block, and the volume's own argument sentence printed twice in the block.** **Eight, a woman of about thirty-four where the canon has a woman of about thirty-seven, in two chapters.** **Nine, a count of meetings wrong in two chapters, eleven where there were eight and five where there were seven.** **Ten, a sentence in Chapter 0449 that said eight of nine people said nothing about a receipt when five of them had spoken.** **Eleven, two grammar slips and one month of a meeting that was a fortnight and not a month.** **What the block cost: nine people who met eight times and then agreed not to meet again; a man of about thirty-two with a limp asked out of the only room in nine counties where anybody had heard anybody, who left in six words and did not argue and whose argument the chapter sets out and the room never hears; a woman of about fifty-eight with a slate who asked for one number to be written down somewhere that is not her and was refused and is the one it cost most and was thanked by nobody; a woman of about fifty who has four figures in one hand on a board with no head on it and who was not thanked in a room on the eighteenth of Wolfmonth and has never been thanked by anybody; four sheets of paper with nothing on any of them; a gate at the end of nine miles of road that is exactly where it was; and a boy of about nineteen who went home nine miles and was at his bar at the second hour on the next morning.** **And the standing lesson, which is now fourteen batches running and is the fourteenth running of it: the cheapest of the ninety-one lines was a figure that was right for a different day, and the second cheapest was a state-file-shaped sentence in a chapter, and the ninth was a writer's own note in the middle of a paragraph, and not one of the three is visible to any instrument this project owns, and the certificate in this item is the second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale.**

1101. **The second pass over the close, run by a phase that inherited the ten files and the five state files from the phase that wrote them, and what it found, and the lesson it adds.** **Nineteen lines across seven of the ten files, and not one of the nineteen was visible to a comparison between two files and not one was visible to a calendar check, and fourteen of the nineteen were found by a person reading the files.** The classes are these. **One, a duration correct for a different day, in three places, and it is the same class the first pass called its cheapest and it happened again after the first pass closed: Chapter 0447, which is day 1126, printed *twenty-three weeks* in three places for a paper that went into the drawer on day 979, and the true figure on that day is a hundred and forty-seven days, which is twenty-one weeks exactly, and the page now derives it; the twenty-three weeks figure is the day-1140 figure of a hundred and sixty-one days and it is still correct as the Volume 10 handoff figure and is carried as such; the boy of about nineteen at the bar in Chapter 0445, which is day 1113, was printed at about three hundred and forty days standing at one end of nine feet of oak, and 1113 less the start at 779 is three hundred and thirty-four, and it is repaired; and Chapter 0441 put the matching of the fourth hand on the board in a month in Greenmonth, which is day 945, seventeen weeks before the receipt existed, and Chapter 0436 is the chapter that prints the nine minutes and it is the nineteenth of a month in Fallowmonth, and the date is repaired.** **Two, two figures attached to a printed speech that did not foot or could not be read, in two chapters: Chapter 0444 printed *sixty-seven* for a speech in two turns of one speaker, and the second turn alone is sixty-seven and the whole is a hundred and sixty-eight, and the page now prints both and says which is which; and Chapter 0445's sentence said *the number of it is not printed anywhere and will not be* in the same breath as printing it, which is a self-contradiction a reader finds and no sweep finds, and the sentence now says that both of the two speeches are printed here and that nothing about the number is on any board in nine counties.** **Three, a figure about an unprinted speech, in one place, and it was also wrong against the world: Chapter 0447 said the mason asked the far side of it *in nine words*, and the first of the eight is four words long and is printed at Chapter 0416, and the figure is cut and the page says the first of the eight is four words long instead.** **Four, three gender slips, in three chapters, where a woman of about fifty-eight with a slate is a man twice and a woman of about forty-four at a kiln is a man once, and all three are a reader's eye and not an instrument's.** **Five, one month name that cannot be resolved to a day, in two chapters: *a month in Hearthmonth* in Chapters 0449 and 0450, and Hearthmonth is a month of this calendar that begins at day 1206 and so has not begun inside Volume 09 at all, and a month name that cannot be resolved to a day is a month name you change into a duration, and both are changed into the canon fact that each name in the hat has the thing for a month.** **Six, two sentences that did not parse: Chapter 0444's *She did not say how many of the eight the other seven are*, and its *the number of people who know it is nine because there are nine of them in a room and one of them is the tenth*, which put the tenth inside the nine.** **Seven, one contradiction of place in Chapter 0449, which said the hundred-and-forty-year-old book was on a shelf behind the door and in a room four miles off at the same time, and the keeper is four miles off and not the book.** **Eight, one grammar slip in Chapter 0445 and one stale count of meetings, which is the class the first pass also found twice: Chapter 0441 said the room had said a thing out loud once a month for about two months, which makes nine meetings in all, and the three counts the close actually carries are five at day 1113, seven at day 1133 and eight at day 1140, and there is one meeting before the block and the sentence now says so.** **Nine, one count that was right on the page and wrong against a date three chapters later is item one's first case, and one that was wrong against Chapter 0416 six chapters earlier is item one's third case, and a duration is the figure most likely to be right for a different day and it is now the third time in two volumes.** **Ten, and the tenth is the lesson: three of the nineteen were in the state files and not in the chapters. This item's own predecessor, 1097, certified *fourteen printed speech-length figures* where the page prints sixteen, and certified that Chapter 0445 prints *thirty-three and eighty-six*, and the page printed neither of those in the first printing or the second. 1099 certified a longest shared run of 23 words against the prior files and 28 among the ten new, and those two figures do not reproduce on any of the three bases this pass ran them on, and the true figures are 15 and 15, and the figure that does not reproduce is the error and not the count. 1090, 1095, 1089, 1097 and 1099 have been rewritten off the pages and the word counts in 1099, in this file, in `state/current.md`, in `state/chapter-summaries.md`, in `state/batch-summaries.md` and in `state/open-threads.md` have all been recounted on the files and not carried.** **The block moved from 20,224 to 20,292 words on the declared basis, the volume from 98,002 to 98,070 in fifty chapters, and the manuscript from 1,387,256 to 1,387,324 in four hundred and fifty chapters, and all three were recounted on the chapter files and not read off any item.** **And the standing lesson is now fifteen batches running and it is the same one in a new dress: the third time in two volumes, and the second time in one volume, the last place a defect survived was a certificate, because a state file that reports a sweep is a second measurement of the prose and the second measurement is the one that goes stale, and this time the certificate was wrong about a count of figures on the page, wrong about two figures the page never printed, and wrong about a duplication measurement twice. Nothing is quarantined, nothing is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn.

1102. **The third pass over the close, run by the phase that repaired the review findings on 1101's repair, three lines across three of the ten files, and what it is.** **A reader found these three and no instrument this project owns found any of them, and one of the three is a regression the second pass itself introduced over a clause that was correct before it touched it.** The classes are these. **One, a figure that is correct for a different day, and this time the second pass wrote the wrong one and the page had it right: Chapter 0450, which is day 1140, printed the boy's start as *fifty-one weeks and four days and one day over a year*, and 51 × 7 + 4 is 361 and 365 − 361 is 4, so 361 days is four days short of a year and *one day over* would need fifty-two weeks and a day, and the clause the second pass replaced, which read *four days short of a full year*, was correct and is restored. Every other link in that sentence is right at 361 and was left alone: 204 up-days, 51 Sundays, 153, 155, 1,395 pence and 29 marks 3 pence, and the four remainder days are printed as a Wednesday, a Thursday, a Friday and a Saturday, which is also correct, because the method at 1093 takes the remainder block from the weekday of the start day and the start day, 779, is a Wednesday, and that method reproduces the four prior canon figures at 949, 972, 1038 and 1080 and the two at 1130 and 1140 exactly, and it was re-run for this item and all six reproduce. A pass that has corrected a right clause because it looks like the wrong one is worse than a pass that misses one, because the first is not recoverable by a reader who trusts the file.** **Two, a repair that does not parse and that contradicts the count the volume carries, and this is item 1101's own class eight turning out to have been recorded as fixed when the sentence it produced is broken: Chapter 0441 said the room *has said a thing out loud on purpose once a month and has said it once before this*, which has no main clause, splices a present perfect into a past-perfect frame, and reads as a claim that this is the second meeting of the nine on a chapter that is the first of the eight. The sentence now says the room *had said a thing out loud on purpose once before this*, which is the count 1101 recorded as the intent, being one occasion before the block, and the two counts the close carries, five at day 1113 and eight at day 1140, are untouched and still foot against 0441, 0442, 0443, 0444, 0445, 0446, 0449 and 0450.** **Three, two referents with no antecedent in the chapter they are used in, in one sentence the second pass edited and did not finish: Chapter 0444 said *the ninth word and this one are in the same mouth in the same week*, and the only ninth in that chapter is the ninth thing a book has never been shown for, which is a different ninth, and *this one* had no antecedent for the *one*. The sentence now says a name that is not a practice went into that mouth on the nineteenth of this month and that speech came out of it on the twenty-fifth, which is six days. The name is not printed, being the ninth word and out, the handing is Chapter 0443, 1110 less 1104 is six, and the old claim of one week was wrong by one week-boundary and is not repaired into a week.** **The block moved from 20,292 to 20,303 words on the declared basis, the volume from 98,070 to 98,081 in fifty chapters, and the manuscript from 1,387,324 to 1,387,335 in four hundred and fifty chapters, all three recounted on the chapter files and not read off any item, and the `about` figure of 453 does not move and its rate is 22.85 per 1,000 against a denominator that is eleven words larger.** **And the lesson is the same one for the third time in one volume and it is now about a repair and not about a check: the cheapest defect in each of the three passes was a figure that was correct for a different day, and the second of the three was a figure that was correct for the day it was on and was changed to one that was not, and a certificate is a last place a defect survives and a repair log is the same kind of thing and is not a safer one.** Nothing is quarantined, nothing is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn.**

## Volume 09 Close — items 1103 to 1112, and the highest number in this file is now 1112

**This section is written by the phase that wrote the close record at `outline/volumes/volume-09-close.md`, and it continues from 1102. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this close's own repairs and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn.**

1103. **The frame, re-derived from the single anchor, and three figures that moved because the page moved.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor. Day 931 is the volume's first day and day 1140 the last, two hundred and nine days elapsed and two hundred and ten inclusive, five blocks of forty-two days inclusive, and **the block span and the volume span agree exactly, which no earlier volume's did. All fifty datelines foot on month, day of month, weekday and market flag with zero mismatches, and the two half-derivations were run separately and returned 0 and 0 and a market claim of 0 as three figures and not as one.** The volume carries 60 market mornings at 12 a block, 30 Saturdays and 30 Thursdays, and 13 of the 50 chapters fall on one. **As this close received them the block figures were 22,937 / 19,774 / 16,970 / 18,097 / 20,303 at 98,081, and the manuscript 1,387,335, and all of those reproduce; after this close's repairs they are 22,935 / 19,774 / 16,970 / 18,097 / 20,303 at 98,079 and the manuscript 1,387,333, and the two words are accounted for to the word at `volume-09-close.md` §11.1.**

1104. **The plan's three wrong calendar rows, and the fifty pages that are right.** `outline/volume-09.md` §17 gives day 965 as a Wednesday when it is a Sunday, and gives 1098 as a Saturday and a market day and 1099 as a Sunday when 1098 is a Sunday and not a market morning and 1099 is a Monday. **Chapters 0440 and 0441 print the correct ones, and this close did not edit the plan, because a close is not a better place than a batch to correct a plan.** The rest of §17 reproduces, **including its own Block 1 market list of twelve, which is the first printed day list in this project that is correct to the digit, and including its claim of sixty market mornings at twelve a block with thirty Saturdays.** The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived from the day number and not from one another and are reported as two figures and never as one, because a check that returns one figure for two derivations is one derivation.

1105. **Six repairs in five chapters, in four classes, and the two that moved the volume's word count.** A speech opened and never closed at `chapter-0404.md` line 33, repaired by adding a closing curly quote, **and the per-line bold-parity sweep returned clean on it because the bold was closed and the quotation mark was not, which is the Volume 08 close's §2.13 defect with the two halves swapped.** A doubled opening curly quote at `chapter-0405.md` line 49, the only one in fifty chapters. A speech-length figure of *about nine words* against a printed speech of twenty-seven at `chapter-0402.md` line 50, and the same against a printed speech of fifty-nine at `chapter-0405.md` line 33; **both broke `outline/volume-09.md` §15 item 15, which forbids the word *about* before a figure attached to a speech the page prints, and between them they removed the two whitespace tokens that moved the volume's totals.** A speech-length figure of fifty-nine against a printed speech of seventy-one at `chapter-0409.md` line 19, in a chapter that foots its fifty-seven at line 61 exactly. A fold of paper at thirty-eight days against an anchored start at day 999 in a chapter set on day 1038, where it is thirty-nine, at `chapter-0429.md` lines 27 and 43. **The cheapest defect in this close and the cheapest defect in each of the three passes before it are the same class, and the one before it ran the class backwards.**
