1106. **The block-boundary change in the punctuation mark, 272 characters in twenty-six files, and the instrument that found it.** Chapters 401 to 420 print speech in curly quotation marks and Chapters 421 to 450 print it in straight double quotes, and not one of the fifty files mixes the two. **Every block is internally consistent, which is why five block-scoped passes, three of them over this volume's own last block, each returned clean, and a defect that is uniform inside every scope a pass is given is invisible to a pass.** The break falls exactly between Chapter 420 and Chapter 421, at the boundary between Batch 0002 and Batch 0003. Repaired: 272 characters, the odd in each pair an opener and the even a closer, alternated within the line. **Verified after the edit: zero straight quotes in fifty chapters, 359 opening and 359 closing curly marks balancing in every file, and the word count unmoved.** A per-file curly-quotation balance is the sixth basis in this project's set and the only one that has now found a real defect in delivered prose for the third time, and it was added by this close because the bold-parity basis could not see the twin of the defect it found in Volume 08.

1107. **The money, re-footed at volume scope, and two things a successor will get wrong.** Forty-four columns, not fifty: Blocks 2, 3, 4 and 5 carry ten each and **Block 0001 carries four, at Chapters 401, 403, 409 and 410, and Chapters 402, 404, 405, 406, 407 and 408 have no footed column at all.** All forty-four foot in pence on the page, a mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion without exception, and **Blocks 2 to 5 reproduce their own published ledgers to the digit — forty of the forty-four confirmed against a second measurement by a phase that did not write them.** **And there are two collisions among the forty-four, the first in three volumes: 70 pence at `chapter-0417.md` and `chapter-0420.md` inside Block 0002, which the block's own ledger prints as a duplicate, and 30 pence at `chapter-0410.md` and `chapter-0441.md` across Blocks 0001 and 0005.** Forty-two distinct totals out of forty-four columns. A collision is a signal and not a verdict and both columns foot line by line.

1108. **The speech-length figures of the volume, and the class of rule the volume set for itself and broke twice.** `outline/volume-09.md` §15 item 15 is that a speech-length figure is either footed on the page or it is inside a character's mouth as a claim about that character, and that the word *about* may not precede a figure attached to a speech the page prints. **The volume has four sites carrying *about nine words*: two are legitimate, at `chapter-0406.md` line 85 and `chapter-0410.md` line 29, where the referent is a speech the page does not print and both go to the unprinted-referent class, which was fourteen entering this volume and is fourteen leaving it; and two were defects against printed speeches of twenty-seven and fifty-nine words, now repaired.** A Volume 10 writer should know the distinction that makes the volume checkable: **the first of the eight is four words long and *eight words* is the volume's name for eight lines and not for eight words**, and a figure about a road is not a speech at all.

1109. **The bar's money method, its convention, and its six canon figures, which is the single most breakable number in the volume and the one a successor will get wrong.** Nine feet of oak, up four days in seven on a Monday, a Wednesday, a Friday and a Sunday, nine pence a day for three of the four with the Sunday unpaid, start at day 779, the twenty-fourth of a month in Wolfmonth of the year before, a Wednesday. **The interval on a given day is the number of days *before* that day; the method takes whole weeks of four up-days in seven with the Sundays out and takes the remainder block from the weekday of the START day, so the remainder block always begins on a Wednesday.** `chapter-0448.md` line 19 appears to argue the opposite — that because fifty weeks lands on a Wednesday the day over is a Wednesday — and the day over is in fact a Thursday, on which the bar is down and the boy is not paid, and the chapter is right anyway because the count runs to the day before. **The check is that the method reproduces all six of the volume's own canon figures exactly: 73 paid days on day 949, 83 on 972, 111 and 999 pence on 1038, 129 and 1,161 pence on 1080, 151 and 1,359 pence on 1130, and 155 and 1,395 pence on 1140. The day counts at all seven printings — 170, 193, 228, 259, 301, 351 and 361 — are plain intervals and all seven are exact.** The fold in the inside pocket of a coat that is not his is thirty-nine days old at day 1038, not thirty-eight, and this close repaired both sites.

1110. **Four manuscript-wide barred-numeral figures in the live handoff, and the live handoff was right about three of them and a tokenizer is the whole of the fourth.** On the declared basis, a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends, case-insensitive, occurrences with files: `hemp` at **70** in 40 files, `thirty-six` at **20** in 13, `fifty-two` at **101** in 32, `sixty-one` at **142** in 51, `sixty-six` at **249** in 50. **The live handoff printed 20 and 102 for the second and the third, and 20 in 13 is 20 in 13, and `fifty-two` has 102 written occurrences of which 101 are tokens on the declared basis, the one being `fifty-two's` at `chapter-0230.md` line 55. This close's first printing of this item said 17 in 12 and 100 in 32 and was an undercount of its own.** And **Volume 09's own `thirty-six` is the twentieth of the twenty and is at `chapter-0401.md`, inside a footed money column of nine loads of peat and eleven sacks of quicklime that foots at four hundred and thirty-six pence, and it is recorded and not repaired, because removing a word from a correct column to keep a ledger tidy is editing and not auditing.** The barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and fifty chapters, both in narration in Volume 08, and is spent. **Count before enforcing, count before correcting, and the count is in this item and not in a prohibition.**

1111. **The figures this close inherited and did not count, and the figures this close then asserted without counting either, which is the largest class of finding in the close and in the review of it, and the seventh volume running.** `thirty-six` at 20 and `fifty-two` at 102 in the live handoff's barred-numeral row, and `about` at 453 in 20,303 with a rate of 22.31 in its measurements paragraph, are all **right** on the basis the same files declare. **This close's first printing called all three wrong and replaced them with 17, 100, and 452 at 22.26, and a review that did not write it counted the page and found the records right; at the same time the close's own volume ledger was undercounted in every block, `about` by 676/614/490/386/453 against a printed 671/611/489/379/452 and the volume at 2,619 against a printed 2,602, `about four people` at 3/4/3/1/0 = 11 against a printed 2/4/3/1/0 = 10, and the seven filler words at 149 against a printed 146 with a per-block row that summed to 132 and did not foot its own total. All of it is corrected, and the corrected figures are in item 1109 and in `state/open-threads.md` 109.** The volume's own total is the one figure here that is not an error in the source: the prompt page's 98,081 and 1,387,335 were correct on arrival and went stale the moment this close repaired two words, and the only way to print an honest total is to print both. Every finding in this item is a figure in a record about the work, and not one is a defect in the fiction except the six at item 1105, which were found by reading the page. **A pass that has corrected a right clause because it looks like the wrong one is worse than a pass that misses one, because the first is not recoverable by a reader who trusts the file; this close did exactly that, twenty-six figures of it, while writing that lesson down, and that is the part of this item Volume 10 should take most seriously. The rule gains one line: count the thing, and say which thing, and say the basis before the number — and when the number belongs to a repair, count it off the page after the repair instead of working it out from the repair.**

1112. **What the close did and did not do, and the standing instruction.** It repaired six sites in five chapters in four classes and substituted 272 quotation marks in twenty-six chapters in a fifth, **declared four figures in the live handoff wrong when three of them were right and the fourth was a tokenizer, corrected its own volume ledger after a review counted it again, and corrected the volume's own word and manuscript totals, which were not errors in the source** — items 1110 and 1111 and `outline/volumes/volume-09-close.md` §11.2. It archived the four ledgers for Volumes 01 to 09 to `state/archive/*-volumes-01-09.md` as byte-for-byte copies, **and it did not edit `outline/volume-09.md` and did not create any prompt and did not write a marker.** **The three rows in the plan's §17 that are one day out are owed by whoever owns the plan. The unedited list of thirteen unlooked-at items stands: the seed vault and the keeper's decision; the nine bushels of barley in a four-foot aisle; the four hundred miles of shut road; the marginal instruction and whose words it is; either line in a stone and the length of either; the roadmark and what it is; the mechanism and why he can cross; the third word of the bell's tile; the number of persons the office will not print on an offer; the count of the looks at a leaf that says *a person*; the word in front of a place name; the name of the antagonist; and the account of the worst winter in eighteen years.** The word that names the founding paper is at zero and the antagonist's name is written nowhere including in the close record, and the close printed no third of the barred kin-term and no fifth unattributed two-line statement and wrote no chapter of new prose.

## Volume 10, Batch 0001 — items 1113 to 1128, and the highest number in this file is now 1128

**This section is written by the phase that planned Volume 10 and wrote its first block, and it continues from 1112. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's own repair passes and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn. The whole of this section is the first of five for the volume and the successor inherits all of it.**

1113. **The frame, derived twice from the single anchor, and the two halves reported as two numbers and not as one.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor, and the method re-run reproduces the six Volume 09 canon weekdays at days 979, 1104, 1109, 1113 and 1140 and the rest. **Volume 10 runs day 1141, the twenty-sixth of Wolfmonth YR 318, a Monday and not a market day, to day 1380, the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth YR 319, a Tuesday and not a market day. Two hundred and thirty-nine days elapsed, two hundred and forty inclusive — eight months on the carried thirty-day rule, matching the Volume 10 row of the ledger in `outline/series.md`. The five blocks are forty-eight days inclusive each, 1141 to 1188, 1189 to 1236, 1237 to 1284, 1285 to 1332, 1333 to 1380, and the block span and the volume span agree exactly, as Volume 09's did and as no earlier volume's did.** The ten chapter days of Block 0001 are **1141, 1144, 1148, 1153, 1157, 1160, 1162, 1166, 1171 and 1188**, and the weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately from the day number and not from one another and are reported as three figures: **the weekday half is 0 mismatches over eight datelines, the day-of-month half is 0 over eight, and the market-morning claim is 0 over eight, with a ninth and a tenth chapter carrying no date at all and neither claim applying to them.** Those two are Chapters 456 and 457 and they are in the coast frame, and the frame is the finding and not an omission.

1114. **The two frames, and the reason this volume is the first in which both of them are on the page at once, and the rule for the coast frame.** A main-world dateline carries a month, a day of month and a weekday. **A coast dateline carries an ebb and an hour and carries none of the three, because on that foreshore nobody has said a day of the week in about nine years and nobody has said a month either; the year there is counted in springs and in the number of times the salt has been off.** Chapters 456 and 457 are in that frame and the frame breaks in the middle of the block and comes back in Chapter 458, **and the reader is not told that the frame has broken; the reader is told by the dateline.** Precedent: Chapter 0160's dateline in the Ninefold Winter is in the path's own terms and prints no month and no weekday. **The two frames are not convertible. A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no Volume 10 chapter may add any two of the three, and no month name and no weekday is spoken in a room on that foreshore at all.** A writer who needs a date for a coast chapter writes the date into the state file and not into the dateline.

1115. **The year turns inside this volume, and it is the one thing a successor can get wrong that is internally consistent while it is wrong.** **The whole of Volume 09 was YR 318. Thawmonth 1 YR 318 is day 876 and Thawmonth 1 YR 319 is day 1236, and day 1236 is the last day of Block 0002 and is a Friday and not a market day.** The month starts inside Volume 10 are 1146, 1176, 1206, 1236, 1266, 1296, 1326 and 1356, and the year changes between 1236 and 1237 and nowhere else. **A writer who carries YR 318 through the whole volume will have every date from the turn on be a year out and every one of them will foot against the others.** The volume carries sixty-eight market mornings, thirty-five Saturdays and thirty-three Thursdays, at 14 / 14 / 14 / 13 / 13 a block, and thirteen of the fifty chapters fall on one.

1116. **The protagonist, and what the four touches cost, and where the second loss of this volume is.** Stage 4, Convergence, half-spent; the qualification is not earned and not claimed. A permanent limp in the right leg, a stick he cannot lean on, and he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight. **Three crossings inside about four weeks against a budget of about three attempted crossings a season, and the whole of a season's budget spent: nine days from the third of Frostmonth to the twelfth, four days from the nineteenth to the twenty-second, and two days from the twenty-fourth to the twenty-fifth. The three do not overlap, the protagonist is on the main-world foreshore in Chapters 456, 457 and 460, and he is not on the foreshore at all in Chapter 458, which is the chapter the second crossing runs through.** The eleven lines of the notebook now have one word on the eleventh and there is no twelfth line, and what is on it is the word for a blacked-in hearth in a courtyard on the other side of a salt-store, which is the right word for the wrong room. **The second loss of this volume is a person losing an illusion about his own voice and not about a record, and it cannot be put on a sheet, and it belongs to him and to nobody else, and it is spoken in Chapter 459 and again in Chapter 460 and placed neither time.** A sweep of the ten files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and no chapter says the ledger does or does not move. **The System panel in Chapter 426 is spent and this block prints none and the volume prints none.**

1117. **The accent, and what it is, and what is and is not on the page about it.** A path bleed appears in Block 1 as an accent and nothing else. **On the eighth day of Frostmonth he says ten words in the accent of the far side of a salt-store in front of about six people on a foreshore, and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow repeats them back in the same accent, and it is exactly what he meant and it is not the sound that came out of his mouth.** By the twenty-sixth day he says a sentence in that accent to a woman of twenty-nine in a room over a weigh-house two hundred miles from any of it, and she does not notice, and it is in the narration that she has not noticed yet. On the thirteenth day of Hearthmonth he and the woman with the barrow say forty-five words between them on a made road, six and then thirty-five, and about nine people going past heard both of them. **Nobody in this block says one word about the accent to him about it, and that is the whole of the first two costs, and Chapter 459 prints one memory that cannot be placed, which is a kitchen with a blacked-in hearth and a window that does not shut that is his mother's and that has about two of its details from a courtyard about four hundred miles off. The accent is first on the page on the eighth of Frostmonth, four days before he went through a door in a salt-store, and it is not caused by the crossing and does not stop at it.** No rule and no mechanism is explained and none is scheduled.

1118. **The foreshore, the eleven houses, the four and the seven, and the road that runs out twice a day.** Two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand and broken shell where a river runs into the sea; passable for about four hours out of every twelve and about twice a day. **Eleven houses, about nine hundred people, four of the eleven with a person in them who answers for it and seven with nobody in them, and the seven are the ones that are used.** There is no line on it and there is no beginning; the sand turns in about nine places and none of the nine is a boundary anybody keeps. **A woman of about fifty-one has read the tide off a post for about twenty-two years and keeps the count on a piece of driftwood with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, and the twenty-second is not cut and goes in when the water is off a mark that has not been off in twenty-two years.** The four are a woman of sixty-three with a pan, a man with nine hundred acres and a lease on the ninth of the eleven, a widow with the last of a boat, and a man who is not a good man and whom nobody will say so out loud on that sand. **The woman of about fifty-one's want is spoken once, in Chapter 456, in a sentence to nobody at the ebb before the light: if the four ask the seven for one thing then the seven are eight, and the four are still four, and after that there is a count of eleven on a piece of paper and the eleven has a head on it and the head says men.** It is not spoken again in Block 0001 and is owed in Block 0002.

1119. **The wall, and the four heads, and what a piece of paper came back with.** **A wall of stone eleven paces long and about two feet high, bedded at a hundred paces from the landward end, laid in four days out of a barge by about four men, curing in wet sand, and not illegal in any way anybody has named.** It takes the tide twice a day exactly as a post does and about four inches of sand banks against its far side in four months. **A surveyor of about forty-four with a chain and a boy of about seventeen with a rod, and a piece of paper that came away with three heads filled in — a place, a figure of men, a trade — and a fourth head, which is newer, which wants the name of the person who will be answerable for the road, and which is empty.** The form is the same three-head form of about forty years old and the fourth head was cut by somebody who wanted four. **A man of about thirty-four with a sheet is on that sand and is not named and is not confronted, and nothing in this volume or this block says whether his being on that foreshore and his being one of the four men with a crowbar in Volume 09 are connected, and Volume 10 may not say it.** The surveyor says once, to nobody, that he was given a figure of men by a man who did not have it, and does not say it again. **The Crown is a road office with a column, a chain and about four men, and none of the four is corrupt and all of them are doing what a road office does.**

1120. **The three names, the budget, and the sweep.** The budget for Volume 10 is five and **Batch 0001 spent three, so the volume stands at 3 of 5 with two unspent and nothing after them: the arithmetic is 3 + 0 and there is no second batch yet.** The three are `Marda Oakes`, 51, who reads a tide off notches cut in driftwood and has kept the lower road in use for about twenty-two years; `Rusen Ball`, 29, at a factor's table, eleven days on a road, who wants a permanent connection and cannot pay for one; and `Halla Wray`, 63, who has boiled salt on that foreshore for forty-one years with the same pan and the same fire, is the oldest person on it, and is against a permanent connection. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Marda Oakes` 1, `Rusen Ball` 2 and `Halla Wray` 1 and nothing else; every other hit is a section heading.** `Aren Kest`, `Mara Vey` and `Tovan Kest` are returns and are not spends, and Tovan appears once and only in a dateline, at work, about four hundred yards up a street, and is not told that the man on the road is on it.

1121. **The request, and the four forms it takes, and the three the volume may not improve on.** A folded letter with a brown seal, addressed to a keeper of records in a county about nine counties off, eleven days on a road. **What it asks for is a keeper for a connection between three roads that have never been joined, and the second side of it is a list of eleven items and every one of the eleven is a road somebody wants joined to a road somebody else is on.** The keeper of records does not fill one in and does not answer the letter. The man of about twenty-nine is told the three heads in about nine sentences and is told that in about four years it will be the only sentence anybody in nine counties can say about that foreshore. **The protagonist is asked to hold the middle and says no on the coast in thirty-four words in one turn, and it is the fourth time in about seven months he has been asked to hold a thing, and of the four he said yes to one and no to this one and the other two were offers he made and did not get, and the four were a word in a song, a form with three heads, a door in a salt-store, and a middle between three places.** The reason is printed and it is a different reason from Volume 09's: **a man who stands in the middle of three places is a man who can be sent for to all three.** **The form's three heads are the same three heads Volume 09 spent fifty chapters on and this volume's problem with them is a different one, and the two are not the same defect: a road had no place and this road has a place and nine hundred people and a trade, and the trouble is the figure and the fourth head.**

1122. **The refusal, and who gave it, and what it was worth.** In Chapter 457, nine people come out to a pan over about an hour in ones and twos, and there is no chair and they are not a council and nobody kept a count and there was nothing to overvote. **They refuse the permanent connection in about nine minutes on five grounds, four of them by a person and the fifth being the man of about thirty-four with a barrow, and none of the five is the same reason and none of the five is wrong.** The man with the barrow refused in nine words — *he would rather be sent for than be found* — and is the only reason on that sand with nothing to do with the tide. **A woman of about fifty-eight who sweeps a yard behind a house of assizes in a township of about nine hundred people sent nine words nine days back on a handcart: *tell him the yard will outlive all of us.* Nobody carried an answer and nobody is going to, and a woman of about thirty-four took the nine words and put them in the front of her shirt and said no when she was asked whether she was going to answer them.** **A loose council of nine from Volume 09 is not formed, not mentioned, not imitated and not contacted, and the woman of about thirty-four with a net is not on any page of this block, and the woman of about nineteen at a bar and the woman of about seventeen who carries glass are not on any page of this block either.**

1123. **The keeper of records in this block, and what she did and did not do.** Mara Vey, twenty-nine, four years and a half, one room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane, fourpence a year, a body corporate with a door, a number and the capacity to sue and to be sued, out of a deed of thirteen words. **She does not answer the letter, she does not fill a form in, and she sends for a man of thirty-two with a limp on the seventh hour of a Monday, which is the correct hour and is not the hour anybody would guess.** In Chapter 459 she asks him to say a sentence out loud twice, and when it comes out in the wrong accent she says in about nine seconds that a copy of how a person says a word is a thing about a person that anybody can be shown to a person, and she is right and he knows she is right and neither of them says one word about the accent. **She is not thanked. Her want is not restated. The four lines she wrote with no name at the foot in Volume 09 are not reported as having arrived and nobody has read them and nobody is going to. About nine hundred sheets a month and about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed Saturday are printed in this block and the two are not added and are not converted into one another, and the hundred and four went out on the twenty-first day of Frostmonth in one band tied at a corner with a cord.** A drawer about eleven inches deep holding about four hundred returns, a register at seven questions and no findings and a day-book at nine entries of a thing nobody asked are on the page in Chapter 459 and are not moved.

1124. **The money, read on the page and not from a state file.** **Ten columns, ten footed in pence on the page before the total was said, ten distinct totals at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, each read against the column above it and not only against the total below it, and each foots.** **A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion without exception: twenty-four is under one by twenty-four, twenty-seven by twenty-one, thirty-two by sixteen, thirty-five by thirteen, forty-one by seven, fifty-one is a mark and three over, fifty-seven a mark and nine over, sixty a mark and twelve over, sixty-seven a mark and nineteen over, seventy-seven a mark and twenty-nine over.** None of the ten is one of the thirty Volume 09 Batch 0002, 0003 and 0004 columns at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68 and 69, nor of Volume 09's own forty-four, nor of the two collisions at 70 and 30 that the Volume 09 close recorded. **The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository and this phase did not re-read them; the check is against every total printed in the Volume 08 and Volume 09 per-batch ledgers and it is a weaker check than the one a successor with the item in front of it can make, and that is stated rather than implied.** The four barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six` are at zero across the ten files, and no line item in any of the ten columns prints one of them. **No rate is printed for the tide road and no toll is taken on it, and the only rate in the block is a coach fare between two ports and a farrier at sixteen, and neither is a toll.** The bar four miles west of Marl is **not touched in this block and carries no figure, which is a decision and not an oversight**; the method at 1109 was re-run for this block and reproduces all six of Volume 09's canon figures at days 949, 972, 1038, 1080, 1130 and 1140 exactly, and the day count at 1141 is 362 and the paid days 155 and the pence 1,395 and the marks twenty-nine and three, and **none of those four figures is printed anywhere in Block 0001 and a successor that needs one derives it rather than copying it from here.**

1125. **The speech-length figures, recounted speech by speech from the words as printed with the speaker's attribution excluded, and consecutive turns of one speaker merged.** **Twelve printed figures and twelve of twelve foot, and the word *about* precedes no figure attached to a speech the page prints. A turn is a turn and not a quoted span: a speech printed as one pair of quoted marks, an attribution, and a second pair of quoted marks is one turn, and the first printing of four of these twelve figures counted the spans.** Chapter 451 prints twenty-eight for one speech in one turn printed in two spans. Chapter 452 prints sixty-seven for the first of the seven things she says in that taproom, one speech in one turn printed in two spans, **and the first printing called it the whole of what she said for the first hour, which the page contradicts six times over, and the second printing's correction is the number seven, which the page supports and a reader can count** — and a hundred and seventy for one speech in two turns of a hundred and twenty-four and forty-six, the second of which is forty-six words and begins about nine seconds after the first of the two ends, and **the first printing of that figure said a hundred and sixty-three in three turns and was wrong by seven because three turns of one speaker with a stage direction between them are two turns, and the fix was a new number and a new sentence around it.** Chapter 454 prints ten for one speech in one turn. Chapter 455 prints four and six, both in one turn, and **both were figures about unprinted speeches in the first printing and both speeches are now printed.** Chapter 456 prints a hundred and sixty-five for two speeches in two turns of eighty and eighty-five. Chapter 457 prints nine, thirty-four and nine, and the third of the three is a message of nine words that came nine days on a handcart. Chapter 460 prints thirty-five for the second of the two things she says to him on that road, one turn in two spans, and nine for a message of nine words, **and the first printing called the thirty-five *the only sentence anybody said to him on that road that morning*, four lines below a five-word sentence he is given, and said he answered in an accent in a sentence about the tide that the chapter did not print; the tide sentence is now printed, it is twenty-three words, and it carries no figure, and the frame now says second of the two.** **A raw-token sweep of the ten files returns 24 constructions of the form *N words*, and the certificate reconciles to that number and no other: twelve footed speech-length figures, one second-half figure inside a footed one, six recaps of a figure already counted in the Section Five closing frame, three restatements of the one printed nine-word message, and two figures about written things — a deed of thirteen words and the nine words on the first side of a letter. A sweep that stops at 24 has not told a reader which twelve foot, and a sweep that stops at 13 has not told one what the other eleven are, and both were true of this file before the second review.** **The block adds nothing to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class, and the two narration figures that were over utterances the page does not print — Chapter 455's *about eleven words* and its *about nine words in a language* — are now unfigured, which is the third time in this block that the fix has been to drop the number rather than print the speech. The eleven figures that are about a written thing rather than a spoken one — a letter, a list of eleven, two tiers of a wall, a figure on a bench-board, twenty-two notches, the fourth head, nine lines on a bench, a yard, a set of nine lines in a notebook, a number of words in a yard, and a page — were checked one at a time and every one of them is about a thing the page does not print or is inside a character's mouth as a claim about that character.**

1126. **What this block's own hand found, and what it cost, and the standing lesson.** **This is fifteen batches running the lesson and the cheapest defect in this block was the same class it has been every time, run in a new place: a figure that was right for a different object.** Eleven repairs across nine of the ten files, all found by a person reading the files and none visible to a comparison between two files. **One, the driftwood: it carried about forty notches in five chapters and forty-one notches in a speech in a sixth, and the object it actually is is a piece of driftwood with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it, one for each year, cut when the water goes off a mark that has not been off since a wagon went into a channel, and the twenty-second is not cut; the number was wrong in the same way in six places and no instrument in this project can see a count that is consistent.** **Two, a speech-length figure off by seven on a two-turn speech, which is the class that has now happened in this project four times and the sentence around the figure had to be rewritten and not only the figure.** **Three, five figures attached to speeches the page did not print, of which two are now printed and three are now unfigured, and the fix in every case was the same: print the speech or drop the number, never leave a narrator's figure floating over an unprinted utterance.** **Four, two figures correct for a different day: the leaving of a room was called about two months ago in Chapter 451 when it is about a month, and the offering of a middle was called the second time in about a year when it is the second time in about six months, and both were derived from day 1141 and not from a page.** **Five, a duplicated clause of thirty-four words standing whole in two paragraphs of one chapter, which is the class the raw-token basis exists for and which it found in about four seconds, and the two paragraphs were not duplicates of each other and one of them had been written before the sentence it described was printed.** **Six, a self-reference in four forms across four chapters — *this volume*, *this chapter*, *this block*, and the narrator reporting a wall as *the wall in this chapter* — and the retired reporting frame is retired with a hard stop and no occurrence was converted into another form.** **Seven, a hundredweight in a market, and a hundredweight is not a Neyra measure, and it was replaced with about nine cart-loads.** **Eight, three paragraphs of Section Five with an odd number of bold markers, which the per-line parity sweep found in about a second and which no prose check in this project can see.** **Nine, the word *protagonist*, which is a word about a book and not a word in it, standing in a chapter file in the middle of a paragraph.** **Ten, three real months of the calendar verified and a September standing in a chapter file for the length of a paragraph, and the class behind it is the one Batch 0004 of Volume 09 lost eighty-eight lines to and the instrument cannot see, because a calendar check asks whether a date is right and not whether a month is a month.** **And the cheapest of the eleven is the one this project has found in every one of its passes and it is the one a Volume 10 successor will make in Block 0002: a number that is correct on the page and wrong against the object it is about.**

1127. **The review of Batch 0001, run by a phase that did not write it, and what it found, and the two things it found that this project has been wrong about for a whole block.** **A read-only review of the ten files against `outline/volume-10.md` and the two handoff sections returned twenty-eight classes of finding and every one of the twenty-eight was real, and four of them were defects of the class this file's own last item calls the cheapest and three of them were defects the counting basis in this repository does not settle.** Repaired in twenty-six places across nine of the ten files. **The classes, in the order they will recur.** **One, the crossing calendar did not hold across three chapters: Chapter 455 printed a crossing from the ninth to the nineteenth while Chapters 456 and 457 placed the protagonist on the main-world foreshore on the fifteenth and the seventeenth, and Chapter 459 dated the second and third crossings at the fifteenth and the eighteenth and overlapped them. The three are now the third to the twelfth, the nineteenth to the twenty-second and the twenty-fourth to the twenty-fifth, and the protagonist is not on the foreshore at all in Chapter 458, and the second crossing runs through it.** **Two, a speech-length figure that counted quoted spans and called them turns, in four places, and the word *about* in front of a fifth, and the certificate at 1125 said ten figures and ten of ten foot and there are twelve and the certificate was the last place the error survived, for the fourth time in three volumes.** **Three, a figure attached to a speech the page does not print, in one further place, and a month name spoken aloud on a foreshore where no month is spoken, in one place.** **Four, three gender slips, a woman of about fifty-one printed as a man in three chapters, and the same class the Volume 09 close recorded three of.** **Five, an internal contradiction of the kind a duplicated count is made of: eleven houses printed as six in one chapter and as four in another, nine hundred people printed as using the houses rather than the road, twenty-two notches printed as eleven in the same chapter that fixes them at twenty-two, and the tide clock in Chapter 453 giving the fourth hour of the afternoon as both the time the road begins and the time the sea comes over the wall.** **Six, two figures that are correct against one object and wrong against another: a fire that is older than the woman who owns it when she is sixty-three and the fire is forty-one, and a band of a hundred and four that went out on a Friday on a line that says a fixed Saturday.** **Seven, a wall dated four days after a chapter had already printed its mortar cured, and a man's arrival on a foreshore given three incompatible dates across three chapters, and the only one the block's own geography supports is the third of Frostmonth.** **Eight, a duplicated clause of fifteen words standing in two different chapters, which a within-block run of the raw-token basis found and a prior-files run did not, and the standing bases in this project are prior-files only.** **Nine, a tally the page contradicts in the same sentence: Chapter 457 said he had said no three times and yes once, and the four asks it lists in the same breath are three yeses and one no, and the correct statement is that of the four he said yes to one and no to this one and the other two were offers he made and did not get.** **Ten, three passages where the prose reports on itself, which is the frame this manuscript retired: a chapter saying that a thing *is not printed*, a chapter saying *the whole of the rest of this volume*, and a chapter saying *the cheapest of the three in this block*.**

**And the two that this item exists for, because they are the second time in three volumes and the first time this project has had to change a number in its own state files on the strength of a page.** **A certificate is a last place a defect survives, and the cheapest of the review's twenty-eight was a speech-length figure in a chapter whose other six figures all foot, which means the class is not error-proneness, it is a figure nobody counted twice.** **And a review that runs a counting basis the writer did not use will find a different answer, and the basis has to be written down before the run and compared afterwards, because this block's own figure of a hundred and sixty-five words in Chapter 456 is twenty-two tokens shorter under a convention that splits an elided possessive, and every other figure in the block reproduces exactly on the declared basis of a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends.** **The declared basis stands, the figure stands, and the disagreement is recorded here rather than resolved by moving the number, because moving a number to agree with a reader who is counting differently is the third-pass mistake of Volume 09 repeated, which is a pass that changed a correct clause because it looked like an incorrect one.**

1128. **The second review of Batch 0001, run by a phase that wrote neither it nor the first review, and the four things it found that this file had already written the rule for and broken anyway.** **Nine findings, nine real, all nine applied, five in the prose of four chapters, two in state files, one in the successor prompt, and one that no instrument in this project can see. It is the fourth review of a repair in this project and it changed no date, no weekday, no money total, no name, no plot beat and no ending, and no chapter was restarted.** The full dispositions are at `reviews/volume-10/batch-0001.md`, which is the first findings list Volume 10 has; the first review's findings for this batch were never written there and survive only in this file at 1127 and in the review-repair section of `state/batch-summaries.md`, which is the sixth time this project has paid for `logs/` being gitignored. **One, the cheapest defect in the book, in the place this volume's own last item named: Chapter 454 printed a foreshore *eleven miles long* in a block whose every other mention of it, in four chapters and in `outline/volume-10.md` at §3 and §15 and in the successor prompt, is two miles and about nine hundred paces. It survived a repair pass that had already fixed about forty notches and twenty-two notches in this same block, and the reason it survived is the reason the class survives: a number that is wrong in one place and right in seven is invisible to every instrument here, and a pass that reads for what it expects to find reads past it.** **Two, the dateline of the same chapter contradicting its own body, and three, a missing article in the same sentence.** The dateline had the day in a market town two hundred miles off, then in a foreshore yard, then asserted that a market on that foreshore is not on a Saturday, in a chapter whose argument is that a foreshore has no such word; the body's market is nine hundred people, about four hundred on the sand and about five hundred in four houses and a yard, and the dateline had said about nine hundred were in the yard. **The market morning now belongs to the county it belongs to, the yard is explicitly not one, the dateline carries the foreshore's own length, and the population is the split the body gives.** **Four and five, a figure attached to a speech the page contradicts, twice.** Chapter 452's sixty-seven did foot, but against the speech above the sentence and not the one it sat under, and the sentence also called that speech the whole of what she said for the first hour when she says seven things in that taproom. Chapter 460 called its thirty-five words the only sentence anybody said to him on that road that morning, four lines under a five-word sentence he is given, and said he answered in an accent in a sentence about the tide that the page did not print. **Both fixes were of the same kind and both are the same lesson as 1125: the figure was never the error, the claim around the figure was, and a repair that moves a number and leaves the sentence is a repair of half a defect.** The tide sentence is now printed, twenty-three words, carrying no figure, and it says the water will be over the wall this afternoon and off it by the eighth hour of the morning, both of which are Chapter 453's own clock. **Six, a craft finding no instrument here can see, and the only one in this block that changes how a scene reads.** The accent beat plays four times, in Chapters 454, 455, 456 and 460, and it is the block's central character beat: a woman on that foreshore says a line, it comes back in her accent, he hears both, nobody says anything. Sixteen occurrences of the word and a raw-token check that returns clean, because four instances of a device are not a duplication and a repetition count is not a duplication measurement. **Chapter 460's instance is now the fourth shape and not the first, and the change is the block's argument rather than a polish: the sound that comes out of him is the sound he meant, and this time he does not hear the difference, and the paragraph dates it to five weeks earlier, which is 1188 less 1153.** The four now end four ways — new, silence, convergence, and loss — and a successor writing Chapter 460's cousin should know that the fifth instance of this beat is a device the volume has spent. **Seven and eight, two numerals the first pass left over utterances the page does not print**, being Chapter 455's *about eleven words* and its *about nine words in a language*, both now unfigured. **Nine, and the one that is the tenth data point's finding arriving inside the block that had already written it down: two figures this file and `state/batch-summaries.md` had asserted rather than counted.** The `about` rate was printed with a case-insensitive basis named and a case-sensitive count carried, and the longest maximal shared run against the four hundred and fifty prior files was reported at four words and is **25**, on a raw-token eight-gram basis with headings and bold markers unstripped, and the four does not reproduce on any basis that could be constructed. **The four is withdrawn and not replaced with a smaller number, because a figure that does not reproduce is the error and not the count, and a second measurement of the prose is the one that goes stale.** The true run is the protagonist's own body restated, *he has not got up a bank since the seventh day of a month in Longlight*, and within the block the longest run is 20 words and all thirteen pairs are declared frames.

**The block after both repair passes, every figure recounted on the files and none inherited.** 16,906 words in ten chapters, per chapter 1,653 / 1,772 / 1,546 / 1,792 / 1,794 / 1,486 / 1,960 / 1,559 / 1,506 / 1,838, and the manuscript 1,404,239 in four hundred and sixty. `about` 438 at 25.91 per 1,000 case-sensitive and 441 at 26.09 case-insensitive, and both bases are printed because the first repair named one and carried the other. `the whole of` 22. The four punctuation marks at 0, horizontal rules 0, bold parity even, curly quotations balanced, the twelve barred tokens and the four barred numerals and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` at 0, ten footed money columns at the same ten distinct totals with no collision, twelve speech-length figures of twelve foot, exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more 0 for the ten new files against the four hundred and fifty prior and 0 at fourteen words, and the calendar derived twice on both halves of all eight dated datelines at 0 and 0. **Nothing in this item withdraws 1127 and nothing in 1127 is renumbered.**
## Volume 10, Batch 0002 — items 1129 to 1150, and the highest number in this file is now 1150

**This section is written by the phase that planned Volume 10 Batch 0002 and wrote its block, and it continues from 1128. Every figure was counted on the chapter files as they stand after this block's own repair passes and the basis is named. Nothing above is renumbered and nothing above is withdrawn. The whole of this section is the second of five for the volume and the successor inherits all of it. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

1129. **The frame, derived twice from the single anchor of day 930, and the fact that the year turns on the last day of the block.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, on the carried thirty-day month, is the only anchor, and the chain was re-run independently of any printed list and reproduces every anchor the plan prints: 930 is Mudmonth 25 and a Sunday, 1176 is Hearthmonth 1 and a Monday, 1189 is Hearthmonth 14 and a Sunday, 1206 is Goatmonth 1 and a Wednesday, 1236 is Thawmonth 1 YR 319 and a Friday, 1237 is Thawmonth 2 YR 319 and a Saturday and a market morning. **Block 0002 is days 1189 to 1236, forty-eight inclusive and forty-seven elapsed, and the ten chapter days are 1189, 1194, 1209, 1218, 1224, 1229, 1231, 1232, 1234 and 1236**, that is the fourteenth and the nineteenth of Hearthmonth YR 318, the fourth, the thirteenth, the nineteenth, the twenty-fourth, the twenty-sixth, the twenty-seventh and the twenty-ninth of Goatmonth YR 318, and the first of Thawmonth YR 319, and they are a Sunday, a Friday, a Saturday, a Monday, a Sunday, a Friday, a Sunday, a Monday, a Wednesday and a Friday. **The weekday half and the day-of-month half were derived separately from the day number and not from one another and are reported as three figures and never as one: the weekday half is 0 mismatches over eight datelines, the day-of-month half is 0 over eight, and the market-morning claim is 0 over eight, with a ninth and a tenth chapter carrying no date at all and neither claim applying to them.** One of the ten is a market morning and it is Chapter 463 on the fourth of Goatmonth, a Saturday. **The year turns on day 1236 and every date from 1237 is YR 319, and day 1237 is the first day of Block 0003.**

1130. **The two dateline frames, which chapters are in which, and the fact that a chapter set on the foreshore may carry a main-world date without anybody on the sand saying a month or a day.** **Chapters 466 and 467 are in the coast frame**, and they carry an ebb and an hour and no month, no day of month and no weekday, and the ebb is written as a position in his own arrival rather than as a number, because an ebb counted from a fixed point cannot be consistent with a chapter set five weeks after the last ebb that was counted from that point. **The other eight are in the main-world frame**, and four of those eight are set on or at the foreshore and carry a main-world date, which is the precedent Chapter 0458 sets and which this block follows. **No month name and no weekday is spoken in a character's mouth anywhere in Chapters 466, 467, 468, 469 or 470**, and every month and weekday token in those five files is in the narrator's dateline or in the narrator's Section Five frame, and the frame breaking and coming back inside a block is a real effect and is not a gimmick. **A yard keeps its count in days and a road keeps its count in tides and a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut, and no chapter in this block adds any two of the three.**

1131. **The three assemblies, and what they agreed, and what became of it.** Three port assemblies met in one room over a weigh-house in a port of about nine hundred people four days off the foreshore on the fourteenth of Hearthmonth, day 1189, a Sunday. Nine people, three from the landward end, three from the seaward end and three from the port, six of whom walked four days. **They have met each other twice in forty years and both occasions are nameable: a barn about nine miles off forty-one years ago about a boat that did not come in, and a wreck at the seaward end about eleven years ago where nine men pulled a hull up a beach that is there twice a day.** There is no chair, no agenda and no count, and the road was not on the agenda. **The one thing agreed, in about four minutes out of about three hours: whoever is nearest a wagon that stands in the channel at the wrong hour gets it out, nobody is called for, nobody is paid, and nobody is asked afterwards.** The three assemblies are three assemblies and are not a body and the word is not written in this block.

1132. **The five governances put to a road, all five wrong, and the exact finding of each, because a successor may not re-run any of them and may not improve on any of them.** **Hereditary custody**, a fold thirty miles south of a fen town of about four thousand people, nine stones, about two hundred people, a man of about forty-one who has walked it since he was eleven and a boy who was about nine and is still about nine and does not walk it: *a boundary is inherited because it is not being touched, and a thing you want to inherit on that coast would have to be cut into it at a low tide, and a man would have nine stones and about four hours.* **A rotating clerk**, a bench of nine men over a door on a lane that sits on Saturdays and has been asked nine things in about nineteen years, a man of about fifty-eight on his second year with four months left and about nine hundred people in the street below: *a turn is a year and a date is fixed at both ends about four months before anybody knows who the next one is, and the question anybody will ask that road is whether a man is standing in it, and I answer it every week for four hours and then I am a different man and he answers it.* **A public lot**, a chair on a platform and a hat on a shelf in a room over a store in a township of about nine hundred people, about nine names a year and a month each and a bolt not drawn in about nine years: *a hat is how a township says that it does not mind, and it is right for a pound and a common oven and a set of nets and the repair of a water trough, and it is wrong for a road because a name on a shelf can be found and about four days later there is a man on that sand who was sent for and he is not the man who was there.* **No gate at all**, a hurdle across a lane nine miles off a made road in a dale of about nine hundred people, a man of about forty-four who puts it across twice a day before breakfast, and the nine words *a man with a form stayed here four years*: *a place with no gate is not a refusal, a refusal is a thing a person does and says out loud and somebody hears, and nothing is a thing a place has not got, and the whole of what nine hundred people could give is a habit and not a keeping.* **The fifth answer**, a room over a weigh-house in a port that is let by the day and whose keeper goes home at the eighth hour and does not draw the bolt: *it works right up until somebody wants to know who holds it, and a road is asked about twice a day and that room is asked about about nine times a year.*

1133. **The sixth answer, which is the block's finding, and the one name it spent.** **`Tobin Kernick`, 53, who has driven a salt wagon between the port four days up and the foreshore for nineteen years, goes down on the ebb, stands about two hours and comes back on the flood, and nobody sets him a time and nobody has ever set him one and he could not tell you who to ask.** He stops at four of the eleven: a pan at the fourth house and a fire behind it that never goes out, a lease at the ninth, a widow with the last of a boat at the eleventh who buys four sacks and will not sell them back to him, and a house at the seventh that a man stands in and about four of them will not stop at, which is the man nobody on that sand will say anything about out loud. **His finding, in a hundred and seventy-three words in two turns: nobody keeps it, the seven are the way out of the weather and they are the way out of the weather because nobody is standing in them, and not one person on that shore agreed to any of it.** And then, at the fifth hour, in a hundred and three words, the second time in about a year he has said no to a stranger about a house, and the first time he has said it twice. **His own want, unrelated to any of it, is that a man who cannot put a horse down is not a keeper of anything and never will be, and he sleeps in his wagon and has nowhere between the port and that shore to stable a horse.**

1134. **The two wants, in full, and both are spoken once.** **The want of the woman of about fifty-one is on the page in `chapter-0466.md` and is a hundred and three words in two turns, and it is: that nobody has asked her one thing in twenty-two years and she has never once wanted to be asked for anything, and she wants to be asked for a day by somebody and told no, and she wants it in the winter, and she wants to still be here after it, and she does not want a stone and does not want a head on anything, and she wants the count of that shore to stay exactly where it is, which is in her and in about nine heads of about nine people and nowhere else at all.** She was asked in the open by one man at an ebb with about nine people inside about nine feet, and there is no room and no chair and no count. **The want of the woman of sixty-three is on the page in `chapter-0467.md` and is ten words in one turn: I want the road to stay exactly as it is.** She said no for about two seconds first, and about six sentences of why she was going to answer anyway, and the first part was longer than the want. **Neither want is restated anywhere else in the block, neither woman is thanked, and about four people on each shore are the kind of person who will carry them.**

1135. **The three rooms and what each will carry, and the one that will not, and it is not a refusal.** Three people came down the made road four days on the twenty-ninth of Goatmonth, day 1234, a Wednesday. **The landward-end room will carry the four clauses and has already said them out loud once. The seaward-end room will carry them and will say them at the next ebb, and the woman of about fifty-one is not in that room and has not been told and will say it herself if anybody asks her. The port four days off will not carry them, and nobody in that port refused anything**, because a sentence that says *nobody is paid* cannot be said out loud in a weigh-house where about nine hundred people in that port are paid twice in seven days without making three of the four clauses a lie. **No fixed day is fixed, no register is opened, no head is put on anything, and no second meeting is scheduled.**

1136. **The man of thirty-two with a limp in this block, and the answer he gave, and what he did not do.** **He is a witness in Block 0002 and he conducts nothing, gets nothing back, overrules nobody and is not the reason anything on that foreshore works.** He is on the coast in Chapter 469 and nowhere else in the block, and he came down the made road four days behind the man of about twenty-nine and did not walk with him and did not ask him for anything. **He was asked whether he is needed, in the open, with about nine people about nine feet off, and he answered a hundred and eight words in the last of three turns and neither a yes nor a no: if you want one sentence carried, then I am needed, and if you do not want it carried then I am not, and I have not asked which. And I have been asked to hold a thing four times in about seven months and I said yes to one of them and it is not this one, and the reason is that a man who stands in the middle of three places is a man who can be sent for to all three, and this shore is not the middle of anywhere, it is the end of about nine hundred paces of hard sand.** The sentence he offered to carry is about a mark and eleven paces of stone and a figure of men on a sheet of paper with a fourth head on it that nobody has filled in. **The fourth head is still empty, he offered nothing about it, and no chapter of this block fills it in or says that anybody has.**

1137. **The money, read on the page and not from a state file, ten columns and ten footed totals.** **Ten columns, ten foots, ten distinct totals at 23, 26, 28, 49, 79, 19, 55, 73, 83 and 62 pence, in Chapters 461 to 470 in order, and each column was added in pence and the total was said after the sum list and never before it.** Nine of the ten are the man of about twenty-nine's, one a day each from the first of them, and the tenth is the man of thirty-two's. **A mark is forty-eight pence in every conversion on the page without exception: 23 is under half of one by one, 26 is over half by two, 28 is over half by four, 49 is a mark and one over, 79 is a mark and thirty-one over, 19 is under two-fifths of one by one, 55 is a mark and seven over, 73 is a mark and twenty-five over, 83 is a mark and thirty-five over, and 62 is a mark and fourteen over.** None of the ten is one of the fifty-four printed columns already spent, which are Volume 09 Batches 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 at 16, 22, 17, 3, 112, 135, 70, 31, 56, 70, 40, 33, 29, 38, 42, 45, 48, 47, 144, 34, 37, 50, 44, 59, 46, 43, 78, 64, 68, 69, 30, 53, 39, 63, 65, 71, 97, 75, 88 and 89, nor of Volume 10 Batch 0001's ten at 24, 27, 32, 35, 41, 51, 57, 60, 67 and 77, and the four barred numerals are at zero across the ten files and no line item in any column prints one of them. **The forty recurring totals in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are not printed anywhere in this repository and this phase did not re-read them; the check was made against the fifty-four printed columns and not against the forty, and that is a weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** No rate is printed for the tide road and no toll is taken on it and nothing in this block is a rate, and a foreshore is not a road and that clause is the only part of a printed Volume 09 sentence this block used.

1138. **The speech-length figures this block prints, and how each was counted, on the declared basis of a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends and a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span.** **Eleven figures are printed and all eleven foot, and the word *about* precedes none of them.** Chapter 462 prints twenty-eight for one turn printed in two spans and forty-four for one turn. Chapter 463 prints sixty-two for the last of three turns and a hundred and sixty-two for all three. Chapter 465 prints nine for the printed sentence and ninety-five for one turn, and prints one word for a one-word refusal. Chapter 466 prints a hundred and three for two turns, of twenty-nine and seventy-four, and the first printing of that one counted the second span of the first turn and stopped there. Chapter 467 prints one word and ten words. Chapter 468 prints a hundred and seventy-three for two turns and a hundred and three for one turn. Chapter 469 prints a hundred and eight for the last of three turns and a hundred and ninety for all three. **Seven of these figures were re-footed in the Batch 0002 craft repair because the repair pass shortened a sentence inside the speech and the figure on the page did not move with it: Chapter 463's sixty-two and a hundred and sixty-two became sixty-one and a hundred and sixty, Chapter 466's a hundred and five and seventy-six became a hundred and three and seventy-four, Chapter 468's a hundred and five became a hundred and three, and its a hundred and seventy-four was correct already and was checked rather than moved, and Chapter 469's a hundred and nine and a hundred and ninety-one became a hundred and eight and a hundred and ninety. Every one was off by the number of words the repair removed, and none was wrong when it was written, and the class is the one this block's own item 1141 names: a figure is never counted twice.** **A speech printed as “**one,**” he said, “**two**” is one turn in two quoted spans, and a figure attached to it is a figure about both printed lines; a quoted paragraph on its own with no attribution between it and the next is a second turn and a figure about the two is the sum of both.** Two figures were wrong in the first printing and both were wrong by drift after a sentence was rewritten and neither was wrong when it was written: a hundred and forty-five became a hundred and sixty-two, a hundred and twenty-two became a hundred and nine, a hundred and seventy-four was a hundred and sixty, a hundred and two was a hundred and five, and ninety-five was sixty-eight. **The block adds no speech-length figure to the fourteen-unprinted-referent class and none to the list of protected unprinted figures.**

1139. **The name budget with the arithmetic, and the sweep.** The budget for Volume 10 is five. **Batch 0001 spent three, `Marda Oakes` 51, `Rusen Ball` 29 and `Halla Wray` 63. Batch 0002 spent one, `Tobin Kernick` 53, in `chapter-0468.md`, one occurrence. The arithmetic is 3 + 1 = 4 of 5, and one slot remains for Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005 together.** `Marda Oakes` and `Halla Wray` appear in this block as returns and are not spends, and `Rusen Ball` is on no page of it at all; he is referred to as the man of about twenty-nine at a factor's table, which is the house form. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Tobin Kernick` once and no other proper noun of two words**, and every other hit is a sentence opening across a bold marker or a line opening with a numeral word, and each was read before the sweep was reported. Every person in the ten chapters is reached by age and by trade, and about nine people sat in one room and nobody in it has a name.

1140. **The measurements, recorded because they are facts about the world and not because they are targets, and there is no target in this item for any of them.** Block 0002 is **15,463 in ten chapters**, counted file by file with the title line included and the ten added, and the per-chapter figures are **1,797 / 1,668 / 1,582 / 1,504 / 1,519 / 1,372 / 1,452 / 1,679 / 1,444 / 1,446**. **The manuscript is 1,419,702 in four hundred and seventy chapters, and the four hundred and sixty chapters before this block are 1,404,239 on the same basis and are unchanged, and the sum over the files is the authority and no concatenated stream was counted.** Volume 10 is 32,369 in twenty chapters. `about` is **480 in 15,463, which is 31.04 per 1,000**, on the declared whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator with a case-sensitive numerator, or **485 and 31.37** case-insensitive, **and both are reported and neither is a figure to steer to or to bring down**. `the whole of` is 25. `about four people` is 7. `---` rules 0. **Question marks, exclamation marks, semicolons and em dashes are 0, 0, 0 and 0**, and there is no zero-punctuation mandate in this volume and none in this block. Per-line bold parity is even on every line of every file. Curly quotation marks balance in every file and on every line. En dashes 0. Triple newlines 0. Space before punctuation 0.

1141. **The barred tokens, counted before they were enforced, and the denominators.** **Across the ten new files, whole word, case-insensitive, every hit read: `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` are all at zero, and so are the four barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six`, and `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` and `council`, and `hundredweight`.** The barred kin-term is at 2 across four hundred and seventy chapters and is spent and this block spends no third of it. **The same tokens were recounted at manuscript scope on this phase's basis, which is a whitespace token with punctuation stripped from its ends, whole word, case-insensitive, occurrences summed over every chapter file: `failed` 21, `seam` 49, `roof` 99, `coping` 170, `Gatehouse` 3, `melody` 2, `Culling` 11, `eastern` 40, `hemp` 70, `charter` 20, `brother` 213, `thirty-six` 20, `fifty-two` 102, `sixty-one` 142, `sixty-six` 249, `stage` 32, `ledger` 141, `qualification` 2, `system` 21, `panel` 2, `council` 385, `hundredweight` 7.** **Two of those do not reproduce the figures in the live handoff, `Gatehouse` at 3 against 2 and `brother` at 213 against 172, and this phase did not resolve the difference and did not change a figure in the handoff over it; the two figures are printed here on this phase's basis with the basis named and a successor that needs them should count them again on its own basis rather than take either.** These are prohibitions written for two volumes against denominators of two and zero, and count before enforcing. **A self-reference sweep across twenty patterns testing the bare noun returns 0**, and `on the page`, `in that book` and `the page` return 0, and `the book` returns 5 and every one of the five is a world object: the public lot's book about a hundred and forty years old, twice, and the bench's book that is for what was agreed, twice, and the same book named again in the same sentence.

1142. **The duplication bases, their scopes, and what each returned.** **Raw token, headings stripped, bold datelines in, eight-word shingles, maximal-run basis: the ten new against all four hundred and sixty prior return a longest shared run of eight words, and every one of the 1,058 shared shingles is dateline frame, of the form *YR 318, a Sunday and not a market day, and the second hour and the fifth hour and…*, which is declared and not re-voiced.** The ten new against themselves return 494 repeated eight-word shingles, of which 453 are not dateline frame and every one of those is either the money-column frame (item lists, sum lists and the *is not in it and is not a figure of men* footing sentence), the house footing sentence *The cost of that day is a column*, the narrator's closing frame, the speech-certification frame *words in one turn of one speech*, or a canon phrase of the foreshore (*two miles and about nine hundred paces*, *a piece of driftwood with twenty-two notches cut in the edge of it*, *a woman of about fifty-one at the seaward end*, *the water came in at about the ninth hour and went back at about the fourth hour of the morning*), and **the longest run between two chapters of this block that is not a frame and not a canon phrase is the thirteen-word sentence *You have been to four places that all had somebody's job in them*, which two women on that foreshore say to the same man on two different ebbs, and Chapter 467 now says on the page that the woman of sixty-three heard the woman of about fifty-one say it loudly enough to hear every word of it four hundred yards below.** **Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more, the ten new against the whole manuscript and against themselves: 0, and zero is the correct result for both.** A block-boundary check on the dateline frame and the quotation-mark basis across blocks: clean.

1143. **The repair pass this block ran on itself, and what it found, and the standing lesson, which is now nineteen batches running.** **Twenty-two sites in ten classes across nine of the ten files, every one found by a person reading the files, and only two of the ten classes were visible to any instrument this project owns.** Six lines in six chapters had an odd number of bold markers and three paragraphs had a bold marker with no opening quotation mark or a closing one with no closing bold; **six speech spans in five chapters had the closing curly quotation mark inside the bold instead of outside it, which the per-file quotation balance found in about a second and which the bold-parity sweep could not see because the bold was closed.** A speech figure counted a quoted span instead of a turn turned a correct one wrong in Chapter 466, and a drifted figure turned a correct one wrong in two more chapters. A sequencing break put a paragraph between a man saying a thing and the thing he said. **A boy who was about nine in Volume 09's last chapters is still about nine in Chapter 462 and was first printed about eleven, which is the cheapest defect in the block and it is the class item 1130's month rule exists for: a month that is not a month, and here a year that was not a year.** A man of about twenty-seven was placed at neither of two meetings when the first of them was before he was born. A band of about a hundred and four was first printed as a band of about nine hundred, which is the two-counts rule broken inside a single clause. Nine generations walked nine stones in one sentence and then walked them again. A name was searched against the whole manuscript before it was spent and `Cray` was rejected because Volume 06 spent it, and `Quillon`, `Harrow`, `Cresswell`, `Penrose`, `Vosper`, `Karrick` and six others were rejected on the same sweep, **and `Tobin Kernick` is on the page once.** **The retired reporting frame: eleven occurrences of the four-word sequence *have said since that* were written into this block and all eleven were taken out, and one of them was in a completed chapter of Batch 0001 which was restored from git rather than edited, because a batch may not repair a prior batch's prose on a hunch and Chapter 0460 stands as it was delivered.**

1144. **The fourth head, the wall, the two roads and the other protected objects, and where each of them stands after this block.** **The form's fourth head is empty and nobody fills it in, nobody says that anybody has filled it in somewhere else, and the one time it is mentioned in the block it is mentioned as empty by a man who was asked whether he is needed.** The wall of eleven paces and about two feet high at a hundred paces from the landward end took the tide twice a day on the first of Thawmonth YR 319 exactly as it has twice a day since the four days it took to lay, and no surveyor is on that sand in this block and nothing about the wall is scheduled. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet has been on that sand four times since the middle of a month in Wolfmonth and there is nothing in the four of them that says why he comes when he comes, and nobody has asked him and he has not said.** **The two roads were not compared on a page, no one was sent from one to the other, the gate at the end of nine miles was not opened and nothing was scheduled for it, and the ninth word was not said once in this block.**

1145. **The three bars, the three nines, the two counts, and the thirteen unlooked-at items, all still separate and all still untouched.** **The kerb bar, the market bar and the fourth-months bar four miles west of Marl are three objects, the block touches none of them, and the bar four miles west carries no figure at all in this block, which is a decision and is the same decision Batch 0001 made.** The count on the page under the fourth step of an outside stair is at nine and no tenth asking was put to it; the day-book is at nine and a tenth is not a gift; the register is at seven questions and no findings; the bench's nine things in about nineteen years is a fourth nine and is not added to any of the others and is not added to a figure of persons. **About nine hundred sheets a month and about a hundred and four in one band on a fixed day are printed once in this block, in Chapter 464, and are not added and are not converted into one another.** **The unattributed two-line statement is at four of four and is spent and this block prints no fifth.** The thirteen unlooked-at items are unlooked at, and the Hushwater thread was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed.

1146. **The nine who refused in Volume 09, and the want of a woman of about fifty-eight, and what did not move.** **The nine are not contacted, not quoted, not sent for and not put in a room, and there is no council of nine on that foreshore and the word is not printed in this block.** The nine words from a yard that got no answer were referred to once, on the foreshore, as a fact about a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow who took them off a handcart about seven weeks before this block and put them in the front of her shirt and has said no once to a man of about twenty-nine who asked her whether she was going to answer them, **and nobody carried an answer and nobody is going to and this block did not print the nine words again.** The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened and is not scheduled and nobody was sent and the word *failed* is used of nothing. The offer is unanswered and is not answered with a figure. About eleven hundred people are on no roll and no sheet and no list. The two villages are not back on a road. The corridor's rule is bad and its rate is not printed and nothing in this block is a rate. The hearing stands and the quarantine instrument stands. The city is on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is unreachable. The press was not dated, the bell was not rung, no oath-stone was renewed, no cut was made and none is scheduled. **Stage 4 is half-spent and the qualification is not earned and not claimed and a sweep of the ten new files returns zero for *stage*, zero for *ledger* and zero for *qualification*, and no chapter in this block says that the ledger does or does not move, and a System panel is printed nowhere in this block and nowhere in this volume.**

1147. **The two men, and the accent, and the two losses of this volume, and where each stands after this block.** **The breach between the two men is open and is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, nobody walks a road with anybody for pleasure anywhere in this block, and the word for what the two of them are is printed nowhere in it and the barred kin-term is at 2 and spent.** **The span built over nineteen mornings is a hand's width out of level and is not a repair and this block does not report it as one, and neither of the two men is on a page of this block.** **The accent is not played a fifth time in the shape it has already played four times, and no memory of a kitchen is placed or displaced in this block, and nobody says one word to him about any of it: the only thing the man of thirty-two says on the page in Chapter 469 is a hundred and ninety words about whether he is needed, and not one of the hundred and ninety-one is about himself otherwise.** The road-marked left palm and the notebook and the sheet of nine inches by six and the two unreconciled numbers in his coat are untouched and unmentioned in this block.

1148. **What a Volume 10 Batch 0003 writer inherits as a fact about the world, in one paragraph, and it is not a summary.** Three port assemblies met once in about forty years and agreed on four clauses about a wagon and will meet again on no fixed day and two of the three carry the clauses and the third cannot because it is a weigh-house. A woman of about fifty-one at the seaward end has been asked what she wants once in twenty-two years and answered in a hundred and three words, and a woman of sixty-three has been asked once in forty-one years and answered in ten words, and neither has been thanked and about four people on each shore will carry both. A man of about fifty-three who carts salt has said that nobody keeps that road and that the whole of it is eleven houses that somebody's job, and the volume has four of five names spent and one slot left. A man of about thirty-two with a limp has been asked whether he is needed and has offered to carry one sentence about a wall four days up a made road and nobody has taken him up on it. **And Block 0003 begins on day 1237, the second of Thawmonth YR 319, a Saturday and a market morning, and every date from 1237 is YR 319.**

1149. **And what Volume 10 Batch 0003 must not do, in one list.** **Do not name the antagonist. Do not print a third of the barred kin-term. Do not print a fifth unattributed two-line statement, and do not print one by quoting a close. Do not print the word that names the founding paper in any construction, in a document, in a quotation, in a mouth or in narration, and do not answer the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule in prose. Do not print a System panel, a stage word, a ledger word or a qualification word, and do not let a chapter say the ledger does or does not move. Do not print a word about a roof and do not mention the tile on a coping. Do not un-say the ninth word, soften the leak, add a tenth line to the song, or have anybody regret it in a speech.
