1216. **The Morrow Coast Commons, and what it is made of, and that it has no keeper and no fixed day.** In `chapter-0498.md` the institution is a sheet on a flat stone at the landward end and about four mouths in four counties, and it has eleven houses in it and no keeper and no crown and no office and no register of persons, and its only rule is about the tide and is **thirty-four words long: the water is the only thing that decides anything on that coast, and it decides twice a day, and it is not paid, and it does not attend anything, and it cannot be asked.** There is no meeting, there is no room, nobody called anybody, and the three rooms that have each said a thing out loud have not said it together and have not fixed a day to say it again. **A woman of sixty-three who has sat behind a pan for forty-one years says out loud for the first time in that time that she has not opened anything and has not shut anything, and she is not thanked.**

1217. **The cost of the no, spoken by somebody other than him, which is the outline's own condition and is discharged on a Saturday.** In `chapter-0497.md` the man of about thirty-four from the port four days off goes up two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand to ask the woman of about fifty-one for the driftwood in her post, **and she says no in one word, and she has not been asked anything by anybody in twenty-two years and this is the second time in about four months and the first time anybody asked her why.** She does not say why. He writes two letters on a sheet with no head on it and carries them up a made road without telling anybody at the landward end. **At about the ninth hour a man of about fifty-three with a wagon says out loud, in front of about four hundred people, what the asking cost, and he is not Aren Kest and he is not the man of about thirty-four, and a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow says one word to him and would not say it twice.** The exchange is 255 words in five turns and the asking and the telling is 195 words in eight turns.

1218. **The four houses, the nine days, and his place in them.** In `chapter-0494.md` the fourth, seventh, ninth and eleventh of eleven houses lose the ground under the seaward wall of them in one night of the middle of Rainmonth and **none of the four is lost**, and eleven people put it back in courses that are not level in about nine days without anybody calling them and without anybody being paid. **Aren Kest is the sixth one over and not the first and is not the reason any of the four houses was got back**, and at about the seventh hour on the fourth day a woman of about thirty-four with a barrow says so to a man of about thirty-one with a barrow of sand and thirty feet off him and he hears it.

1219. **Five paths, the three that have a number and the one that does not, and the arithmetic of his own days that will not foot.** In `chapter-0499.md`, set in a fen town of about four thousand people nine miles off the Old Toll crossing, **the five roads are a made road to a salt port four days off, a made road to a market town of about two thousand people, a salt road out of this town to the coast, a road two days west of Merefen to a yard of four lime kilns, and two miles and about nine hundred paces of hard sand, which is not a road and which he counts anyway. The nights against them are nine, seven, six, four and five, which is thirty-one, and the room-keeper's own count on a chalk board over a yard stair is thirty-four, and the difference is three, and he has not found the three.** A man of about forty-four with a horseshoe tells him that nobody upends the board and everybody writes on it, and that a man who knows which of his two numbers is true has two numbers and a man who does not has one number and a gap, **and the gap is where the three nights are and they are the nights he has not got.** Thirty-one and thirty-four are two counts of two different things and are not added and not converted into one another.

1220. **The childhood room, placed again, and not sure of it, which is the volume's last cost and is the one item `outline/volume-10.md` §12 names.** In `chapter-0500.md` he puts three things in a kitchen in the wrong places on purpose: a bowl with the rim chipped out of it on the shelf above the pan instead of inside the press, a hook with a piece of horse-brass on it on the third nail from the left instead of the fifth, and a stool under a window that does not shut and never did. **He was not sure of the room and he placed it anyway, and the reason he placed it is that the bowl and the hook and the stool are his and the room is not.** This is the second placing of that room in the volume, the first at Chapter 459.

1221. **The last page, and what is on it and what is not.** Day 1380, the twenty-fifth of Rainmonth, a Tuesday and not a market day. He is in a room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane about two hundred miles off the coast and he opens nothing, closes nothing and signs nothing. **The wall is still a hundred paces from the landward end and four hundred yards is still four hundred yards, the deed is not called good by anybody and is not called bad by anybody, five paths are open and none of them has a keeper, and he cannot say which of his days belong to Merefen and has stopped trying to say it out loud.** Not a summary, not a wall winning or coming down, not a man standing on a road.

1222. **The word that names the founding paper is still not printed, in this volume, in any prompt and in any state file, and the volume ends with eleven houses and a wall four hundred yards apart and no paper joining them.** Five people hold one path each and none of the five has met another of the five; about four of the five have heard of none of the other four. **The ninth word, the driftwood's twenty-two notches, the seventh tide, the form's fourth head, the unattributed two-line statement, the nine who refused, the corridor's rule, the hearing, the quarantine, the city, the valley, the shut road, the offer of one sentence, the two wants, the fever, Hushwater and the missing oath-stone are all exactly where Batch 0004 left them.** The Hushwater thread was not taken and neither of its two figures was printed. Nothing was opened. No oath-stone was renewed. No crossing was conducted and the season's three were spent in Block 0001.

1223. **The ten footed money columns of this block and the check that was made and the one check that is weaker than it looks.** The totals are **120, 99, 142, 117, 92, 138, 156, 151, 125 and 139 pence**, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, each added in pence with the sum list printed before the total, and a mark is forty-eight pence in all ten conversions. **None collides with any of the eighty printed columns already spent, and all ninety are distinct but for the value seventy, which is duplicated inside Volume 09's own list and not by this block.** The forty recurring totals named in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository and were not re-read: **the check was made against the eighty printed columns and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The four barred numerals are at zero across the ten files as figures, as words and inside the certifications.

1224. **The inherited defect this block found and did not repair, which is the fourth time this project has recorded rather than fixed, and the reason.** `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0488.md` line 53 prints a sum list of **thirty** entries against an item list of **twenty-nine** prices, the extra entry being a second *four*, and the items foot at **ninety** while the printed total says **ninety-four**. **The block did not edit the chapter, because ninety-four is a canon figure in this volume's own series of spent columns and a batch may not repair a prior batch's arithmetic on a hunch, so the defect is recorded here and in `state/open-threads.md` and the page stands as it was delivered.** It is the cheapest defect in the volume and it is the class item 1130's month rule exists for: a thing that was not checked against the thing it is a total of.

1225. **The duplicate-paragraph figure, recounted, and why this file now carries two of them.** On a paragraph split of a blank line and a threshold of twelve words, **thirty paragraphs of twelve words or more stand whole in two or more different chapter files across Volumes 01 to 07, and two files repeat a paragraph inside themselves at `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0037.md` and `chapters/volume-02/chapter-0052.md`.** Earlier handoffs in this project carry thirty-three and three. **The two figures are the same measurement on a slightly wider paragraph split and this file records both rather than reconciling them, because reconciling them would mean editing a chapter of Volumes 01 to 07 to move a paragraph boundary.** None of the thirty involves a chapter of Volume 10's last block and item 204 in `state/open-threads.md` remains the whole-volume job.
## Volume 11, Batch 0001 — items 1226 to 1242, and the highest number in this file is now 1242

**This section is written after the ten chapters that open Volume 11, days 1381 to 1434 YR 319, and it continues from 1225. Volume 11 is open at Chapter 510 and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited by this block.**

1226. **The block's object, and it is a return and not a notice, and the two are on one board at the same height and about four of nine people who read them cannot say afterwards which of the two they read.** In `chapter-0501.md`, outside a weigh-house in a market town of about two thousand people four days up a made road from a coast, **a sheet asks for three things and has a fourth head cut into it and ruled and empty: a place, a figure of people, and a trade.** It says *send this back to the room above the weigh-house by the second day of Fallowmonth* and it says, at the bottom, in the same hand and the same ink as everything else, **Nobody is to be sent to find out.** A man of about twenty-nine who puts up notices for the road office and has done it for about eleven years nailed it up at the second hour, was asked about the fourth head by a woman of about thirty-one, and said that nobody had told him and that he had asked once in Wolfmonth and been told it was not his to decide.

1227. **The room the inquiry sits in, and the two nailed windows and the one that has always been open.** A hired room over a saddler's with four windows, **two of which have been nailed shut since before the town had a market on that street**, a table, nine chairs, and about nine people in it at the fourth hour. A chairman of about fifty-eight with a chain of office that is a stamp of metal on a leather strap with no seal on it and no office on it, two clerks, a man of about twenty-two with a book of his own that is not the room's book, a woman of about forty-four who has been copying for about nineteen years, and **a copyist of about twenty-six named Nesta Braith at a side table with a straightedge, a knife and a pot of paste, who is not one of the two clerks, does not sit at the table, and was not introduced to the room.**

1228. **Gault Prentice, about fifty-one, in a grey coat, and what he is and is not.** He arranged the inquiry, came up the lane to the room over the weigh-house **on foot with a satchel and did not send a rider**, said his own name once, and **did not say whose plan it is and did not say who he answers to.** He is right about the danger a controlled disclosure is built against, and the proof is on the page: **on the first of a month carried at thirty in the year before this one a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel came into that room, asked for a return on a road he named, was told there was one, was told there was no charge, and copied it out in about nine minutes at a table by the door, and nobody stopped him.** He is wrong about one number and the block does not resolve it. **He took the chain of office off and put it on the table on the eighteenth day of Harvestmonth and said the thing he had made was worse than he thought, and said he would take the notice down on Saturday in the room and not in the street.** He is not a villain and is not a knave and is not the antagonist of the book, **and the antagonist is not named in this block in any chapter, in `outline/volume-11.md`, in this file, or in any prompt, and Thread 89 and the Volume 02 close's §7.1 still hold the second severance for Chapter 894.**

1229. **The five returns, and the four heads are in nine counties, and the fifth one has had the fourth head cut out with a knife.** Nine counties were asked. **Five answered in eleven days: four sent the form back whole with the three heads filled and the fourth ruled and empty, and the fifth sent it back with the fourth head cut out clean with a knife and a quarter inch of the paper taken away.** The four that came back whole are not the same paper and the four heads are the same word cut by four different knives in four counties, and **no man in nine counties can say who cut the fourth head and nobody has ever been told what it is for.** A woman of about thirty-four says, in `chapter-0505.md`, that a county is not a man, and **the man who cut that line out is not a villain, has never been asked, and does not know that a form of his is on a table four days up a made road.**

1230. **Two returns from a coast four days off, and the three filled heads are identical and the fourth is empty on both.** The first head carries a word that about four people in that room can read as a place and about four say is a name. The second head carries **about nine hundred.** The third carries four trades. **The fourth head is empty on both, and there is no date on either, because nobody on that coast writes a month or a day and has not for about nine years, and the clerk of about twenty-two entered the day the boat came, and the day the boat came is not the day the thing under the first head happened and nobody in that room can say by how much.** **The two are in the same hand and not in the same ink, and the clerk is told to write down both, and the third return, if one comes, is the road.**

1231. **The four sentences of the letter, and the refusal, and the fourteen words.** A rider comes eleven miles up a lane at the first hour on the twenty-ninth day of Rainmonth with a letter of four sentences addressed to the keeper of the room by her office and not by her name. **The third sentence asks for a bundle of fifty of the sheets that go out of that room every month; the fourth says the bundle will be read in a hired room in a town of about two thousand people and by nobody else, and it says *read, and not copied, and not carried out of the room, and not taken away*.** **Mara Vey did the third sentence's opposite and sent the four heads instead, on her own paper, with a sentence of fourteen words under the fourth head: *the fourth head is empty here too and I have not been told why.*** The sentence is the first sentence anybody has written under that head in nine counties, it was written by a woman of about thirty-one with a pen who has been in that room two years and a half, and it is not a rule and not a request and not a refusal.

1232. **The outgoing sheet of the room over the weigh-house with a door on a lane has carried a fourth ruled line with no head on it for four and a half years.** This is the thread the whole volume is made of, and it is now on the page in Chapter 502: **the line has no head, there is no instruction in nine counties that says what goes in it, the keeper of that room has asked about it twice and been told twice that it is not hers to cut, and she has never cut it out and never filled it in.** **A second set of four heads is ruled in the same room on the Thursday and Friday after Chapter 506, with no figure, no place, no trade and no name on any of the four, and the fourth head on the second set is ruled about as much narrower than the three above it as a sheet of paper moves in a wet week, and the reason given is that a wider line looks like a space for a word.** In `chapter-0510.md` the copyist gives the full reason: **she is going to rule the fourth line a sixteenth of an inch narrower in the second county than in the first, so that a word put in it in one of them will not fit in the other, and a man in four years with nine counties of forms will be able to tell which county a word was written in without being told.** That is the distributed archive's first two nails and neither of them is a copy of a return.

1233. **The nine words, and the four sentences, and that neither woman was thanked.** In `chapter-0506.md`, on a line ruled by the woman of about thirty-one with a pen and left about two-thirds blank: **Controlled disclosure is another kind of the same silence.** Then, in a separate turn: **A room is not the people who are in it, and it is not smaller when you count the people who are in it, and it is not smaller because you have written four names at the top of a sheet. It is the same size. It is the same size with four names on it.** The other three of the four sentences are said on the Monday: the copy of a form is a better thing than the form because the form goes out and comes back; **make two, one in each county, and let the two of them be the same four heads, and let nobody in either town know that the other one exists, including the people who keep the rooms**; and if she did it she would tell the woman of about thirty-one with a pen in about four months and before anybody else. **The blank on the ruled line is not a figure and is not counted. Mara Vey's want is not restated in this block, she is not thanked, and the woman of about thirty-one with a pen is not thanked either.**

1234. **The two words that say the form works, and they are not Aren Kest's.** In `chapter-0509.md` a woman of about twenty-seven, the woman at the eleventh of eleven houses at the end of a made lane about a mile and a half from the hired room, walked up the lane at about the second hour with a copy of the return that went out on the twenty-fifth day of Harvestmonth. **She is not angry. She says she is not angry about the counting and is angry about the line, and that the fourth line is the only line on that paper that nobody put anything in and the only line anybody in that room has read twice.** The copyist of about twenty-six says *I ruled it* from the side table without getting up. **At about the sixth hour the man of about fifty-eight says *It works*, in two words, and then says four sentences: that he has been in the room eighteen days trying to make a thing that cannot be found out about and a woman of about twenty-seven has come up a lane with it in her hand; that this is a reason to write down what a form is and to stop putting a figure under a head that does not say whose and to have somebody whose job it is to be the person a lane can complain to; that a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel is answered with *that is a name on a form*; and that a name on a form is not nothing and he is not going to pretend in here that it is nothing because it is not what he came for.** About four of the nine people in that room have repeated the two words since and about five have not.

1235. **The return that went out, and the figure that is a figure of a body in a lane on a Tuesday.** In `chapter-0508.md` a man of about forty-one with a cart walked the lane on the twenty-fourth day of Harvestmonth, filled the form in on the Wednesday at a table by the door, and handed it to the clerk of about twenty-two on the Thursday and said one sentence on his way out: **that is the figure of the people who were in the lane on the Tuesday, not the ones who live there, and there is a man of about seventy in the fourth house who was in his bed and there were nine women at the well and none of them lives at the end of that lane.** **The clerk wrote the four hundred in, did not write the sentence under the second head, and did not leave the second head empty either, and about nine people in that room did not notice the choice and about four of the nine noticed it.** The clerk of about forty-four, who has been copying for about nineteen years and who has not been asked to copy anything since the second hour, copied the whole of it at about the sixth hour, fourth head included, **so there are now two objects in two counties that say the same thing about about four hundred people at the end of one lane and nobody told the four hundred.**

1236. **The question, and the answer, and the name in the back of a book of carts.** In `chapter-0510.md`, on the nineteenth day of Fallowmonth, a Sunday, a woman of about thirty-four from a weigh-house asks **can a form be stopped** and Aren Kest says **I have stopped one. Once. Six years ago and a little, and the witness I had for it was false, and it did not open again for about three weeks, and about nine people on the other side of it were never told that I had done anything at all. I have not done it since. I am not going to stand in this room and tell you that I never will, because that sentence is about me and you asked me a question about a form.** He is asked to go on and says that if you want a no from him about a form you should ask a man who has never stopped one, and **that there are about nine of those in nine counties and he cannot name one of them and neither can anybody in that room, because he has not met them.** The man of about fifty-eight writes his name in the back of a book of carts at about the eleventh hour, under nine market mornings of figures and above a list of four saddles, and **does not read it out, and the copyist of about twenty-six is the only person who saw, and she said nothing and was right to, because a man who asks to be written down is a different man from a man who is written down and there is no form in nine counties that has a head for the difference.**

1237. **The block's ordinary thing, which is the danger and is not anybody's doing.** **In the week after the second of Fallowmonth, nine counties sent back about nine returns each, and in the week before, four counties out of nine had sent about nine.** The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel put it in his own book on the Friday in a line of eleven words: **a form is a thing that finds out, and the more it finds the better it works.** The number of returns a county sends is not a decision and is not a panic and is nobody's doing, and **about four people in that town have said that the ordinary thing is the whole of the danger.**

1238. **The figures of persons this block puts on the page, and that none of them is added to another.** **About two thousand people in the market town; about four hundred at the end of the made lane; about nine hundred on the coast four days off; about nine hundred sheets a month out of the room over the weigh-house; about a hundred and four of those in one band on a fixed day; about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month in at that door; six hundred and eight carts down one street on one market morning; about nine people in the hired room; about four hundred people in the market town.** **Those are eight counts of eight different things and this block does not add any two of them and does not convert one into another, and about four hundred people at the end of a lane is not about four hundred people in a market town and is not about nine hundred people on a coast.**

1239. **The calendar, derived twice, and the year.** **The ten chapter days are 1381, 1384, 1389, 1391, 1396, 1398, 1403, 1410, 1417 and 1434, and the dates are the twenty-sixth and the twenty-ninth of Rainmonth, the fourth, sixth, eleventh, thirteenth, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of Harvestmonth, the second and the nineteenth of Fallowmonth, all YR 319, and the weekdays are Wednesday, Saturday, Thursday, Saturday, Thursday, Saturday, Thursday, Thursday, Thursday and Sunday, of which eight are market mornings.** The day-of-month half and the weekday half were derived separately from day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, and the market-morning claim was checked separately. **Every date in this block is YR 319 and the year turns on day 1596, which is the first of a month and the last day of Block 4, and not before.** Two in-text dates were checked against the same chain: **the sixth day of a month carried at thirty is a Monday and not a market day, and the sixth of Fallowmonth is day 1421 and a Monday**, and the block's own dateline for Chapter 510 is the nineteenth of Fallowmonth, day 1434, a Sunday and not a market day.

1240. **The ten footed money columns and the check that was made.** The totals are **74, 80, 86, 90, 95, 101, 107, 110, 116 and 123 pence**, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, each added in pence with the sum list printed before the total, and a mark is forty-eight pence in all ten conversions. **None of the ten collides with any of the ninety printed columns already spent, which are the ninety named in the Volume 10 Batch 0005 prompt, and the forty recurring totals named in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository and were not re-read: the check was made against the ninety and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The barred numerals are at zero across the ten files as figures, as words and inside the certifications. **Nine of the ten columns were re-voiced and re-ordered in this block's own repair pass, with every price, every sum-list entry and every total exactly as they were, because the block's first printing had nine columns standing on one item list and the longest run any column made against another was two hundred and five words.**

1241. **The inherited arithmetic defect in `state/current.md`, found by recount and not repaired, and it is the fifth time this project has recorded rather than fixed.** The Volume 10 close certified the manuscript at **1,413,316 in five hundred chapters** and certified its own parts as **1,387,333 for the four hundred and ninety chapters before Volume 10's last block and 96,168 for Volume 10.** **The two parts make 1,483,501 and not 1,413,316, and the difference is 70,185.** This block recounted all five hundred chapter files on the declared basis — a plain whitespace split, the title line included, each file counted on its own and the sum over the files the authority — and returned **1,483,501 for the five hundred, and Volumes 01 to 09 at 1,387,333 and Volume 10 at 96,168, both of which reproduce the Volume 10 close's own per-scope figures exactly.** The inherited total is therefore wrong and the two scopes it certifies are right, **and this block did not edit the Volume 10 close's figures, because a certificate is a record of what a phase certified and rewriting one is not a repair, and the finding is recorded here instead.** The live figure for the handoff is in `state/current.md` and it is the recount.

1242. **The block's own repairs, in one paragraph and not a section.** **Twelve instances of the retired reporting frame, four two-word halves of a money column, one self-reference in two constructions, one `brother`, six speech-length figures that had drifted after a hedge was cut out of a printed speech, one turn of thirty-eight that was wrong by a run of five, a turn of fifty-two in a certification recast to fifty-six because fifty-two is a barred numeral, a span of sixty-one in Chapter 503 and a span of sixty-six in Chapter 507 recast by cutting a word out of the printed speech, two bare hyphens where a curly mark belonged, a date that could not be both a Sunday and the fourth of a month, a woman of about twenty-nine at the end of a lane who was changed to about twenty-seven because the keeper of records is twenty-nine and a figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person is a defect no instrument in this project owns, and nine money columns written to one item list.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0002 — items 1243 to 1261, and the highest number in this file is now 1261

**This section is written after the ten chapters that carry Volume 11's second block, days 1438 to 1488 YR 319, and it continues from 1242. Volume 11 is open at Chapter 520 and no chapter of Volumes 01 to 10 was edited by this block.**

1243. **The block's object, and it is a copy.** **A copy of a form is the only form in nine counties nobody has ever thought to regulate, and that is on the page in Chapter 517 in five turns, and it is the reason a block in which two copies of everything are made is a block in which every copy is a second question asked of the same road.** The woman of about thirty-four copied a return for herself in about nine minutes at a table in her own kitchen with a borrowed straightedge, brought it into a hired room over a saddler's, and asked whether it was allowed. **The answer printed on the page is that it is not allowed and is not forbidden, and that those are two different sentences, and the second of the two is the one that is true, because there is no head anywhere in nine counties for a form that does not go out and come back.**

1244. **The two words for one lane, and the form's own sentence.** **The return asks for the name of the road as the people who live at the end of it call it. That is the sentence under the first head and it has been there about four months and it is the only sentence on the sheet that anybody has ever read twice.** A made lane about a mile and a half from the hired room, ending at eleven houses and a chapel, has two words on it: **the Green Lane, used by about four people at the landward end where the lane comes off the street past the rail of a common, and the Long Lane, used by six people at the eleventh of eleven houses.** Both are people who live at the end of it, so the form's question was asked correctly, and a man of about forty-one with a cart answered it with the word he already had. **The finding is printed in Chapter 516: a place has more names than a form has lines, and a form that asks for one of them gets whichever the person filling it in already knew.** Nobody turned it into a boundary, nobody turned it into a dispute, and the room did not decide what the lane is called and did not schedule deciding.

1245. **The fifth line, and the two of them.** **A clerk of about forty-six with grey in her hair, of the county two hundred miles off, ruled a fifth line on one of the second set of four ruled heads on the Monday of the twenty-seventh day of Fallowmonth, because the book in her county had five columns that week and the sheet had four.** The line is ruled from the same left-hand edge, it is empty, and it has no head cut into the wood above it. **Nesta Braith took it off in about four minutes with the knife she rules with, and said one thing about it, and the thing was not about the fourth head.** **A second fifth line was ruled in the fifth week of the block on a different sheet and went out in the Monday bundle with nobody ordering it and nobody cutting it, and it is empty.** **A fifth line is not a fourth head and a line that is ruled and then taken out again is a third thing, and there is no head anywhere in nine counties for a third thing, and nobody may fill either of the two and nobody may say anybody has.**

1246. **The two sets of heads and what the second county is.** Set one is in this county and set two is in the county two hundred miles off; the fourth line on set two is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower than the three above it, on purpose, and the reason given once is that a word put in one will not fit in the other. **The Monday cart goes up the made road and comes back on the Sunday, and a bundle of nine returns goes in each way, and a bundle of nine returns is not a part of about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month and is never added to it.**

1247. **Gault Prentice's compromise, the nine sentences, and the letter that was not sent.** **He offered to send the four names of the four readers to the nine counties in a letter, so that a road may complain to a person, and he said first and out loud that it was worse than the offer he made before.** The nine sentences are printed in full in Chapter 513. **He says he would rather be found than be right, and the room did not agree with him, and about four of the nine people in that room think he is right and about five do not, and the two sets do not overlap.** The four names are not written on the page and are not on any sheet in this county. **The letter went back into his coat at about the seventh hour with the four names still off it, and a thing that was not done is not a rule and is not a refusal and has no head on it, and the four names at the top of a sheet are still not written down and the offer is still on the table.**

1248. **The three sentences that do not answer the question.** A page came up the made road after **nineteen days** and carried three sentences and nothing else, and it does not mention the four names, does not mention the fourth head, and does not say whether the two sets of four heads are a standing arrangement or a thing that happened twice. **A place is not a road until somebody has been asked to call it one, and the asking is the road going on paper, and it cannot be taken back off the paper, and nobody who has not been asked has agreed to it. A figure is not a figure of people until somebody has said which hour it was counted at, and a figure of people that does not name an hour is a figure of a room. A road that can complain to a person is a road that has been found, and the finding is the thing the road was trying to avoid.** **Nobody was thanked, the keeper of records is not thanked, and her want is not restated, and the offer of two sets of four heads is still unanswered and is not answered in this block.**

1249. **A letter is a form that walks.** Printed in Chapter 514 and said once by the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel: **a letter is a form that walks; it goes out and it comes back, and it says at the bottom of most of them that nobody is to be sent to find out, and this one came back having been walked about two hundred miles by about nine different men who did not know what was in it, and none of them did, and that is not because of the ink, the reason is that it was folded once and it was in a bundle and a bundle is a kind of wall. And a form does not stop being a form when it stops moving.** The man of about fifty-eight said the wall is the answer and was told the wall is about half of it, and the two of them did not settle it.

1250. **The one word in a fourth head, and who put it there.** **A return came up the lane on a Sunday in the eighth day of Wolfmonth with one word in the fourth head, and it is the first return in nine counties in about four years with a word there, and the word is not a person's name and is not a keeper's name and is not the name of a road and is not on a sheet on a flat stone at the landward end of a coast four days off.** It came from the county whose return came back in Harvestmonth with the fourth head cut out with a knife. **A woman of about fifty-three who takes in washing said out loud, unasked, that she filled it in, that she was told to fill it in, and that she has wanted it stopped since the day the head was cut out of one in her county, and that both of those are true.** She was not thanked, was not asked to explain, was not convicted, and said the wrong question had not been asked. **The page says on the face of it that the rule that the fourth head stays empty was never the office's to keep: in about four years a clerk cut a line out with a knife, and in about four months after that a woman put one word in a fourth head with a pen, and neither of the two is an office and neither is a villain.** The fourth head on the Crown's form in the room where she stood is empty and nobody in that room filled it.

1251. **The refusal, and the cost of it, and who spoke.** **A clerk of about twenty-two was asked at about the fourth hour to hand over a book that is not the room's book and said no in nine words: *it is mine, and I would rather keep it.* Nobody in that room argued with him, and it is the first refusal in this volume that nobody argued with.** The book has about two hundred leaves, about nine of them written on, eight of them figures of carts on market mornings and the ninth a list of the four saddles in a shop nine doors from a weigh-house. **The man of about fifty-eight had counted it as nine market mornings and was told by the clerk that it is eight and the ninth is the saddles, and he said he would take the nine out of his own book and was told he would not, and the two of them did not agree.** The cost was spoken by **Gault Prentice**, who is not Aren Kest: **a room with a book in it that the room does not have is a room that cannot say what it knows, and a room that cannot say what it knows is a room that will be asked in about four years by somebody with a warrant, and there will be about nine hundred sheets a month in it and about four hundred and nine parish rolls and about a hundred and four in a band, and the man asking will be right and the room will not be able to put a number on the four things he is asking for, because one of them is nine leaves in a coat and there is no head in nine counties for nine leaves in a coat.** Nobody was thanked. **The two books are not reconciled and the clerk's nine leaves are not added to the day's book at nine, to the count under a fourth step at nine, or to the bench's nine in about nineteen years.**

1252. **The protagonist in this block, and what he is answerable for now.** `Aren` and `Kest` are at 11 and 11 across the ten files, in all ten chapters. **He is on the page in every chapter and he is the reason none of it works.** He reads the fifth line and says nothing about it; he reads a page of three sentences twice and is asked nothing; he waits to see whether anybody will say a thing out loud; he names the clock of a copy of a form; he stands at the rail of a common and does not go down the lane, and says that if he goes down it then the lane has been walked by a man of thirty-two as well as by a clerk of about thirty-four and that is two men where the form asked for one; he is about nine feet from a woman of about fifty-three who has just filled in a fourth head and does not move; he is about nine feet from a clerk of about twenty-two when the man of about fifty-eight asks for the book and says nothing and could have said one sentence. **He conducts nothing, gets nothing back, overrules nobody, is asked one question in the block and it is in Chapter 520, where a man of about fifty-eight asks him what the room is for and he answers that the room is where the two copies meet, that they have not met, and that he will not tell that room he is the one who will make them meet, and is not the reason a form works, a copy is made, a lane is walked, a word is written or a book is kept. The earlier version of this item said he was asked no question in any of the ten chapters and the page contradicts it; the claim is struck and the correction is item 1262.** **The breach between the two men is not repaired in a scene, an aside, a document or an apology, and neither of them is named on a page of this block, and the word for what they are is printed nowhere in it.**

1253. **The third return from the coast, and the two documents.** **Three returns have now come from a coast four days up a made road and a day off a boat, all three from the same hand and three inks, with the same word under the first head, the same about nine hundred under the second, the same four trades under the third and an empty fourth head on all three.** The clerk of about twenty-two has entered a date on all three, **the three dates are nine days apart and every one of them is the day the boat came, and nobody in nine counties can say by how much the day the boat came is not the day the thing under the first head happened, because nobody on that coast writes a month or a day and has not for about nine years.** **A Crown form with a fourth head ruled and empty in this market town and a sheet on a flat stone at the landward end of that coast with a word in a fourth head are two documents in two counties, and the paragraph in Chapter 515 that puts them near each other says so on the page, and nobody in the room has ever seen the second of them.** The coast is on the page in no chapter of this block and is reported in three. The twenty-second notch is not cut and the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years and nobody has been sent there.

1254. **The two figures of carts, and the bare leaves.** **A man of about twenty-nine who keeps an inn has kept a tally of the carts that come down that street on a market morning for about six years, on two sticks, one for the Saturday market and one for the Thursday, and the reason he keeps two is that a week is not a market day.** The Saturday figure is **six hundred and eight** and the Thursday figure is **five hundred and seventy-one**, and they are two counts of two different mornings and are not added to each other. He asked what the number was for and the answer printed on the page is that the room does not know. **Where a return carries two numbers the room now rules a bare leaf with no head over it and puts both of them on it, and there are three such leaves in six weeks: two figures of carts, two words for one lane and an hour, and one written across the two counties.** A bare leaf has no keeper and no head and is not a register and is not a list.

1255. **The calendar, derived twice, and the year.** **The ten chapter days are 1438, 1442, 1447, 1466, 1473, 1475, 1480, 1483, 1484 and 1488, and the dates are the twenty-third and the twenty-seventh of Fallowmonth, the second, twenty-first, twenty-eighth and thirtieth of Embermonth, and the fifth, eighth, ninth and thirteenth of Wolfmonth, all YR 319, and the weekdays are Thursday, Monday, Saturday, Thursday, Thursday, Saturday, Thursday, Sunday, Monday and Friday, of which six are market mornings.** The day-of-month half and the weekday half were derived separately from day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, and the market-morning claim was checked separately and reported as a third figure. **The year turns on day 1596, which is the first of a month and the last day of Block 4, and not before.** **The block's first printing had four of the ten weekdays wrong, the arithmetic having been done in the head and not from the day number, and the repair moved six datelines and four in-text date references and re-planned the nineteen-day lag between the question in Chapter 513 and the answer in Chapter 514 so that the question is asked on a market day and the answer arrives nineteen days later, which is a different and a better fact than the one it replaced.**

1256. **The ten footed money columns and the check that was made.** The totals are **81, 93, 102, 115, 124, 129, 133, 140, 149 and 153 pence**, ten distinct totals and ten distinct owners, each added in pence with the sum list printed before the total, and a mark is forty-eight pence in all ten conversions. **None of the ten collides with any of the hundred printed columns already spent, which are the ninety named in the Volume 10 Batch 0005 prompt and Batch 0001 of this volume's ten, and the forty recurring totals named in `state/open-threads.md` item 36 are printed nowhere in this repository: the check was made against the hundred and that is the weaker check and is stated rather than implied.** The barred numerals are at zero across the ten files as words, as figures and inside the certifications. **Every column was built as a distinct item list before it was written, and the longest run any column makes against any other column in the block is four words, and the same check against the prior manuscript is a frame and not a column.** **Every column was then verified verbatim against its own chapter page, item for item, sum for sum and total for total, and one of them failed that check because the printed total had been changed from 152 to 153 to clear a barred numeral and no pence had been added, and an item at one penny was added and the column foots at 153 and the page and the table are the same thing now.**

1257. **The printed speech-length figures.** **Thirty are printed, all thirty footed from the speech as printed with the speaker's attribution excluded, a turn counted as a turn and not as a quoted span, the word *about* preceding none of them, and none attached to a speech the page does not print.** Per chapter: **511 four, 512 three, 513 three, 514 three, 515 three, 516 two, 517 four, 518 two, 519 four, 520 two.** The largest exchange in the block is three hundred and twelve words and it is printed twice, once in five turns in Chapter 516 and once in nine sentences in one turn in Chapter 513, and the smallest is forty-seven words in three turns in Chapter 517. **Four barred numerals stood inside certifications in the first printing, two of them spans and two of them a money total, and all four were cleared by moving a word in a printed speech or by adding a pence to a column, and every figure was then re-footed against the page and not against a note.**

1258. **The figures of persons this block puts on the page, and that none of them is added to another.** **About two thousand people in the market town; about four hundred people at the end of the made lane; about four hundred that went under a second head on a return; about nine hundred on the coast four days off; about nine people in the hired room; six hundred and eight carts; five hundred and seventy-one carts; about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month; about nine hundred sheets a month; about a hundred and four in one band; about ninety returns in a fortnight; about four people at a rail of a common; six people at the eleventh of eleven houses; about four hundred and nine is the third series and is not derived from the other two; and about four hundred people who now each have a copy of a return, which is the same about four hundred as the residents and is not the figure on the sheet, and the two are never added.** The two figures of carts are two counts of two different mornings and are not added to each other.

1259. **What this block's own hand found and repaired before the final run, in one paragraph and not a section.** **Fifteen instances of the retired reporting frame, three of them introduced by the block's own later additions and every one of them re-voiced, and the block's first printing shipped them twice because the frame was written into fresh narration without a sweep being run before the narration was written; one self-reference in the phrase *this chapter*; four barred numerals inside certifications, two of them speech-length spans and one of them a money total, and the money total was cleared by adding a pence to the column rather than by moving the printed total, which is the only correct repair and which the first printing did not do; a five-word run shared whole by two chapters of the block; a date that could not be a Thursday and a market day; and a calendar error in four of the ten chapter days.** **The lesson is the one the block behind it closed on with a different object, and it is worth stating once: a certification is a second measurement of the prose, a head is a shape and not an order, and a day is a day number and not a feeling about a day.**

1260. **The measurements, with their basis named.** **Word count, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added: the block is 20,938 and the per-chapter list is 2,165 / 2,060 / 1,908 / 1,849 / 2,142 / 2,248 / 2,002 / 2,027 / 2,295 / 2,242 after the review repair.** The five hundred and ten chapters before this block recount file by file on the same basis are **1,504,444** and the manuscript is **1,525,382 in five hundred and twenty chapters**, and **the inherited figure of 1,413,316 in `state/current.md` at the Volume 10 close does not foot against its own parts and is not the figure a writer should cite.** Punctuation as a count of marks actually used: **17 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 4 semicolons, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes and 0 horizontal rules.** Per-line bold parity is even on every line of every file and the curly quotation marks balance at 187 and 187 across the block and at 23, 22, 11, 16, 24, 15, 25, 16, 24 and 11 per file and on every line, and the straight-quote count is 0. **`about` is 553 in 20,938, which is 26.41 per 1,000, on the declared whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator, and `the whole of` is 8 and `about four hundred people` is 10.** The seven filler words this project names stand at **27** in the block, all of them `counted` and `wrote` and none of them `held`, `steady`, `plain`, `breathed` or `nodded`. The barred tokens are at **0** across the ten files and so are the four barred numerals and the kin-term, and a self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns returns **0**. **`Aren` is at 11 and `Kest` at 11 across the ten files, in all ten chapters, reported separately.** The duplication, raw token, headings stripped, bold markers removed, eight-word shingles, maximal-run basis: **the ten new against all five hundred and ten prior return a longest shared run of forty-one words on a basis of raw tokens with punctuation retained and it is a dateline frame in Chapter 512 assembled out of the dateline frame in more than one prior chapter; the ten new against themselves return thirty-five words, and it is a clause in a cost paragraph in Chapter 518. **The first printing of this item said twenty-eight and twenty-four and named no normalisation, and every normalisation this project tried returns a larger number, so the undercount is recorded here and not explained away.**** Exact duplicate paragraphs, whitespace-normalised, twelve words or more: **0 and 0** recounted after this block's own last edit, and **a zero for a block is not a zero for the book, because thirty-three paragraphs of twelve words or more stand whole in two or more files across Volumes 01 to 07**, which is item 204 and is not repaired here.

1261. **The name budget, closed for this block.** **The budget for Volume 11 is five. Batch 0001 spent two, `Gault Prentice` and `Nesta Braith`, and this block spends none, and the arithmetic is 2 + 0 = 2 of 5, with three slots unspent and belonging to Blocks 0003, 0004 and 0005.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten new files returns `Aren Kest`, `Gault Prentice`, `Nesta Braith`, the two words the lane has, `Green Lane` and `Long Lane`, and two false positives at phrase openings, `The Green` and `The Long`, and every hit was read before the sweep was reported. **The next slot is still not for a surveyor, a clerk, a copyist, a boy with a rod, a man with a crowbar, a shovel, a sledge or a barrow, a man with a board, a man with a satchel, a man with a chain, a man with a tally-stick, a man with a form, a man with a cart, a man of about twenty-two with a book, or a woman with a barrow.** **Block 0003 is the block in which the word that names the founding paper is printed once, and that is a different kind of chapter from anything in these twenty, and the three remaining names are the wrong thing to spend on it.**

1262. **The review of Volume 11 Batch 0002, and the nine things it found that this block's own sweeps had not.** The review ran against the ten chapters as they stood at the close of the block and returned nine findings. **Three were false figures on the page.** Chapter 516's certification read *two hundred and twelve words in five turns* while the itemisation printed in the same sentence — six, one hundred and thirty, forty, four, one hundred and thirty-two — sums to three hundred and twelve, so the sentence contradicted itself on one line and the repair moved the figure and not the speech. Chapter 511 asserted that the sentence the protagonist said there *is twenty-three words long and it is the only thing he said in that room*, and the figure matched nothing on the page, the chapter prints two things said by him, and the word *only* was false; the sentence now says he said two things, that neither was said to anybody in particular, and that about four of the nine people heard the whole of it. Chapter 520's closing certification asserted that a man of thirty-two with a limp *was not asked a question and answered nothing* in a chapter that prints a man of about fifty-eight asking him *then what is the room for* and prints his answer two lines above the claim. **Two were narrator sentences that explained their own arrangement.** Chapter 518 said that two documents *have been put next to each other in this paragraph on purpose so that it can be said that they are two*, which is outline prose in a body's clothes; the paragraph now says what is true of the two documents. Chapter 519 said the cost line *is not the man who would have said it otherwise*, which is the narration announcing the hole it has left; it now says that the man of fifty-one in a grey coat looked at the man of thirty-two with a limp before he began and that the man of thirty-two with a limp said nothing.

1263. **The Sunday, and the rule, and the price, and the floor that has not been washed.** The hired room over a saddler's is shut on Sundays, and Chapter 518 is set on the eighth day of Wolfmonth, a Sunday, with about nine people in it, and the first printing of that chapter opened by saying only that the room had been shut on Sundays for about four months, which is a rule broken with no door opened, no rule mentioned and no price paid. **Chapter 510 already established the exception and the repair uses it: the cart for a weigh-house two hundred miles off goes on Mondays and comes back on Sundays, and that cart is the reason the room is ever open on a Sunday.** Chapter 518's dateline now says the room is shut on Sundays and is not shut today and why. A second paragraph gives the exception a person and a price: **the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key says out loud at the door, before she turns it, that a Sunday the cart comes back is the one Sunday the room is open and that anybody who came in on it would be washing the floor of that room afterwards and nobody else; about nine people came in, nobody argued with her about it, and the floor has not been washed.** The cost column for that Sunday now carries the price as an exclusion, and Chapter 520's dateline, which had claimed the room had been shut on Sundays and open on six other days since the twenty-third day of Fallowmonth and so contradicted Chapter 518 and Chapter 510 both, now says the rule and the exception and names the Sunday the cart came back. **A rule a room keeps and a rule a person pays to break are two different objects and the second one is not on any form in nine counties.**

1264. **Four figures in the state files that were wrong, and the three-way and four-way disagreement behind them.** `state/current.md` gave the `about` figure as 548 in 20,733 at 26.43 per 1,000; `state/open-threads.md` item 247 gave 549 in 20,738 at 26.47; and `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` gave 26.47 for the same measurement, all three written in one commit and all three different, and the true figure on the ten files as they stood was 545 in 20,733 at 26.29. **The true failure was not the arithmetic, it was that three files written in one commit carried three different values for one measurement, which means the number was carried forward rather than measured.** All are now the single recount after the repair, 553 in 20,938 at 26.41, and the struck values are named where they are struck. **The quotation balance was reported as *187 and 187 in every file* and 187 is the block total; per file the opening counts are 23, 22, 11, 16, 24, 15, 25, 16, 24 and 11, and the line-level balance was and is clean on every line.** **The longest shared runs were reported as twenty-eight words against the prior manuscript and twenty-four inside the block, with no normalisation named, and every normalisation tried returns a larger number: raw tokens with punctuation retained give forty-one and thirty-five, and stripping punctuation as well gives forty-two and thirty-six.** **The largest speech figure was reported as three hundred and twelve words in nine sentences in one turn in Chapter 513, and three hundred and twelve is also the size of the Chapter 516 exchange in five turns, so the block's largest is printed twice and the earlier certificate named only one of them.** The word-count basis is unchanged and the manuscript recount after the repair is now 1,525,382.

1265. **The four structural findings, recorded and not repaired, and the reason each is not repairable inside a repair pass.** **One, the block is a certificate and not a scene.** All ten chapters run `One` to `Five` and twenty-eight exchanges end in a bold paragraph of one shape quoting the word counts of the speech printed above them, and the instrumentation is on the page where the reader is rather than in the state file where the measurement belongs. **Two, the room splits down the middle and nothing moves.** *About four of the nine… and about five…* stands sixteen and twenty-six times across the block, every disagreement is abandoned in the same paragraph it is reported in, and not one person in ten chapters changes an action because of one. **Three, the male lead is a body at a stated distance.** He speaks in four of the ten chapters and in the other six he is *about nine feet from her and did not move*, or standing at a rail declining to walk the lane with the justification printed for him in the narration, and Chapter 519 printed a paragraph accounting for his silence. **Four, the money columns are generated padding.** About three thousand words, roughly a seventh of the block, every column built from a distinct item list and footing exactly, and every price drawn from the same seven values, and one chapter buying three brooms and four lots of coal and four lots of straw. **A repair pass that rewrites ten chapters is a second batch wearing a repair pass's clothes, and this project has been damaged twice by an automated pass over prose, so none of the four is repaired here.** Each is a change to the next block's cards and not to the block, and the four are items 249 to 252 in `state/open-threads.md` and §3 items 4, 10 and 11 of `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`.

1266. **The retirement of `workspace/volume-11/batch-0002`, and why it was a blocking defect and not a tidiness.** Two phase prompts were eligible at the end of the writing run: `workspace/continuation/next-0005`, which had no `.done`, and `workspace/volume-11/batch-0002`, which had no marker of any kind. Phase selection takes the first eligible prompt in sorted order, so the next dispatch would have run the generic continuation prompt, and the run after that would have re-dispatched `batch-0002` — **whose first printing hands the writer days 1435 to 1488, against a block on disk that runs 1438 to 1488 after this project's own repair of four wrong weekdays.** Re-running that prompt would have reintroduced the exact defect the standing lesson names. **The prompt is now marked retired in its own first line, in the form the dispatcher itself reads, and carries a `.retired` marker, and an earlier run of the same phase wrote it.** After this phase is marked done the only eligible prompt in the repository is `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`. **A phase may retire a prompt it wrote and no other, and this phase retired exactly one.**

1267. **The recount after the repair, with its basis, and the standing lesson.** **Block 0002 is 20,938 words counted file by file on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, per chapter 2,165 / 2,060 / 1,908 / 1,849 / 2,142 / 2,248 / 2,002 / 2,027 / 2,295 / 2,242; the five hundred and ten chapters before it are 1,504,444 on the same basis and the manuscript is 1,525,382 in five hundred and twenty chapters.** `about` is 553 and 26.41 per 1,000 and `the whole of` is 8. Punctuation is 17 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 4 semicolons, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes and 0 rules. Curly quotation marks balance at 187 and 187 across the block and at 23, 22, 11, 16, 24, 15, 25, 16, 24 and 11 per file. **All thirty printed speech-length figures foot from the speech as printed, the Chapter 516 one after the repair. All ten money columns foot against their own printed sum lists and the repair touched none of them. The barred tokens, the four barred numerals, the kin-term and a self-reference sweep across twenty-six patterns are all at 0, and `Aren` and `Kest` are at 11 and 11.** **The lesson, and it is narrower than the one this block closed on before it: a sweep that returns zero is a sweep that did not look at the thing. Every one of the nine findings was a number or a claim checked against another number instead of against the prose it described, and the two sentences in this block that were worst were not measurements at all but assertions about a person, which no instrument in this repository can read and only a person can.**

---

## Volume 11, Batch 0003, and the highest number in this file is now 1280

1268. **The block's object, and it is a rule and not a machine.** A copy of a copy of a sheet of shop-wrapping paper came into a hired room over a saddler's on the fourteenth of Wolfmonth, YR 319, out of a chest of waste paper belonging to a man of about sixty-three, and the sheet has the same four heads cut into it as the Crown's form on the table by the door, and a fifth line ruled under them with no head over it, and the fifth line is the difference between a form that has been going out of presses in nine counties for about a hundred and eighty years and this month's form, which has never had one. A four-figure number cut into the head of that sheet reads 1840 and **nobody in nine counties can turn it into a year, and the finding is about the shape and not about the number, and the sheet's own age is about a hundred and eighty years by its paper and not by its figure.**

1269. **The hand on the 1840 sheet, and the nine words in the margin of the second copy.** The hand on the head of that sheet is not any hand in nine counties today, and the woman of about forty-four who has been copying for about nineteen years proved it in about nine minutes by getting nine people in that room one after another to write the word *witness* on a leaf and putting the nine side by side against the cut head; the tenth hand has a longer downstroke on the second letter than any man in that room has ever put on a second letter. **A second copy of the sheet was found in the same chest on the same morning, on better paper, in a hand of about a hundred and forty years ago rather than a hundred and eighty, and it carries nine words in its margin that nobody in nine counties had read out loud before the ninth hour of that Saturday, and the nine words are *Leave the last line blank until you are told*. The room read them out loud and the woman of about forty-four read them twice, and the chapter says on the page that nobody may say whose they are: they may be the clerk's, who made the second copy, or they may be copied off the older sheet, and the room declined to spend an hour of that Saturday on the question and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud that a room which spends an hour on whose nine words are has decided the nine words are the most important thing on the paper, and the nine words are in the margin and not on the form.**

1270. **The rule, and the word, and the one time it is printed.** In Chapter 523, a woman of about sixty-three who has been in nine counties asking about forms since the third year she kept an inn brought a single leaf folded in three in a flat box, and it is not a return, and the man of about fifty-eight with the chain of office round his neck read it twice and pushed it across the table to the woman of about forty-four, who read it out: **a thing is not closed but by two who did not come to it, which is printed on the page as *Nothing shuts but two who did not propose it*, nine words.** The man of about fifty-eight said out loud that it is a refusal and that a refusal is the oldest thing anybody in nine counties can put a date to, and the woman of about thirty-four told him he would have to say the word, and **he said the nine words again and then said the one word, out loud, in that room, and the word that names the founding paper is the Compact, and it is printed once in the block and nowhere in the block anywhere else, and it is not in a document a clerk is filling in and not in a quotation from an archive and not in any narration before the mouth that says it.** The woman of about sixty-three is a person an inquiry can call and is not a keeper and is not a villain and is on no sheet in nine counties, and she was not thanked and said she would rather be asked, and nobody asked her. **About four of the nine people in that room think it is a rule and about five think it is a sentence somebody liked the sound of, the two sets do not overlap, the room did not vote, and nobody in that room agrees about whether printing it was right.**

1271. **The selection, the second cut, and the four things that are not a finding.** In Chapter 524 the man of about thirty-four with a sheet told the room before the room asked that he had taken a strip about two inches wide off the front of the second copy with a knife in about four minutes on the Saturday after the word was said, and the cut has gone into the paper a little at one end and not at the other. **On the strip, in a hand of about a hundred and forty years ago, is a second nine-word sentence, *A thing once asked is a thing twice agreed*, and it is shorter than the refusal and it is the thing that has been going out into nine counties ever since.** Nobody in that room can say who made the second cut. **A county is not a man**, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet said it in that room and asked for it minuted, and **it is the third time that sentence has been said in that room and Chapter 524 says so and names the two earlier occasions, which were the woman of about thirty-four's in Chapter 505 and his own in Chapter 514. A first printing of this item said he had said it there for the first time and it is wrong: he said it in Chapter 514 and Chapter 514 is in this volume.** The copyist of about twenty-six said out loud, unasked, that the hand is about a hundred and forty years old, that whoever wrote the answer wrote it as a thing that needed no name on it, that a man who needs no name on a thing is a man who has never in his life been asked for one, and therefore that he is dead and was not a keeper and is not a villain and had about nine years in a county office in the middle of the last century. **Those four things were written on a bare leaf with no head over it and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud that the four things are not a finding. The hand is not identified in any chapter of this block and the series row's question about who first wrote the original consent rule is not answered in prose.**

1272. **The finding on the page about the two of them.** A stroke through a ruled line is not a line: it is a stroke in a different ink from the rule, going past the rule at both ends, put there by a man who was looking at a paper. **A selection is a refusal with the two witnesses taken out, and a thing with two witnesses taken out is a thing one person can do alone on a Tuesday**, said by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow at about the sixth hour in Chapter 522 and said again in Chapter 524 in a longer form, and the paper on the wall of every room in nine counties does not have a head that says two, and if it did, the man with the strip would go and find a Tuesday. And the companion finding of Chapter 522: **a form that goes out with a struck-out line on it has told somebody something, and what it told them is that somebody was asked and somebody said no, and the stroke is the only part of that paper anybody can still be got to swear to.** Nobody in this block filled the struck-out line and nobody in this block can say that anybody has.

1273. **The coast, reported three times and on no page, and the two things that came up the road.** Three returns from a coast four days off have come up that lane since Wolfmonth and a fourth came up in this block, all in the same hand and three inks, with the same word under the first head, the same about nine hundred under the second, four trades under the third, an empty fourth head on all four, and no date any person on that coast wrote. **The two things that came up in the same fortnight are the fourth return and a sheet that is not a form, with four heads cut into it with a knife, a word in the fourth head, and four lines under the word that are a question nobody in nine counties asked: *You have nine hundred and about four trades and one word. Who is in the room when you answer? Nobody asked us. We are asking you.*** The room did not answer it, on the ground that a question nobody asked cannot be answered by a man with a badge without turning the man with the badge into the person who asked it, and the four lines went up the made road on the Monday under a return and through about nine hands in a saddler's shop. **No month name and no weekday is spoken by anybody from that sand in any chapter of this block, nobody on that coast has said a month or a day in about nine years, the twenty-second notch is not cut, the water has not been off that mark in twenty-two years, and nobody has been sent to that coast at all.**

1274. **The stone, and the two documents, and who moved it.** In Chapter 529 a stone a foot and a half long, with four heads cut into it with a knife and a word in its fourth head, was carried up the made road in an oiled-paper parcel by a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel, off a boat and then in a man's arms and not in a case, by a man who put it in the paper and a woman who wrapped it, neither of whom said anything to him about it. **It is the coast's own paper and not a Crown form, and for the first time in four months it and a Crown form have been in one room, and the chapter says on the page that they are two documents in two counties, that one of them is a foot and a half long on a flat stone at the landward end of a coast four days off, and the other is a sheet off a press in a market town, and that nobody in the room has the right to say which one is older.** The woman of about forty-four found that the four words are the same four words, that the fourth is the same width on both, that the fifth line where there is one is about a sixteenth of an inch narrower on the stone, and that the two are two castings off one thing that is not in this town and is not in this county. The man of about thirty-eight with a satchel said he would not have anybody in the room touch it in the first hour, and gave the reason that he did not know what was on the underside and neither did the woman who wrapped it. **Nobody in nine counties has told the man in the saddler's shop what was in the parcel. The wall on that coast is not on any page of this block and the driftwood is on two of them and never on the same one.**

1275. **The third name for one lane, and the finding.** A made lane about a mile and a half from the hired room, ending at eleven houses and a chapel, has three names. **The Green Lane** at the rail of the common, used by about four people who live at the end of it. **The Long Lane** at the eleventh of eleven houses, used by six, and said by the woman of about twenty-seven who was thanked by nobody and is not on any sheet. **The Chapel Way**, given in Chapter 526 by a man of about seventy in the fourth house, who had been in his bed on the Tuesday in Harvestmonth when the man in the cart walked half that lane, and who said the other names would be given first because his father gave it that one and his father before him and nobody at the rail has ever used it because the rail is not the lane. **The third of the three is the word on the return: a copy of a return, made in a kitchen at the end of that lane in about nine minutes with a pen and a borrowed straightedge, carries the Chapel Way under the first head in a hand that is not the man in the cart's, and its third head sits under its second one because the sheet it was copied from had a crease in it.** Nobody at the end of that lane filled any head. **A form does not ask a place its name; it asks a person for the name the person has, and a place that has three names has three people. Nobody was asked which of the three is true, nothing was scheduled about choosing, the room did not choose and the two words have not been resolved into one.**

1276. **The question nobody answered, and the copyist's last sentence in this run of days.** In Chapter 527 a man of about thirty-eight with a satchel asked, out loud and in front of the other eight, whether a rule is still a rule when one man changed it in a hand, and the man of about fifty-eight said out loud that he had about four sentences and every one of them was a way of not answering it and gave two of them and stopped. **The room did not answer it and the question is not answered in prose.** The copyist of about twenty-six said one sentence about it, and it was not about the fourth head, and it is the last thing she says in that run of days: that a line is not a rule and not a finding and is a thing a woman with a knife and a straightedge and no head over her table draws. She was not thanked and the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel thanked her for the sentence and for nothing in it. Seven people in that room each said one thing out loud they had not said before, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote all seven on a bare leaf with no head over it at about the eighth hour, the seventh of the seven being his own, and forbade anybody to say so, and none of the seven agreed with any of the other six. **A first printing of this item carried a closing paragraph that set out what all seven of them had said in the order they had said it; four of its seven claims were false against Chapter 527's own page and the paragraph is cut, because a chapter that reports a conversation instead of printing one is the defect and not the summary.**

1277. **The protagonist in this block, and the nine sentences, and the second name.** `Aren` and `Kest` are at 20 and 20 across the ten files and he is on the page in nine of the ten chapters, the tenth being a chapter set on a made lane. **In Chapter 528 he was asked nothing about a form, nothing about a rule and nothing about the fourth head, and two questions were put to him in front of the other eight: the first by the man of about fifty-three with a barrow, which he answered with one word, and the second by the man of about thirty-four with a sheet at about the seventh hour, which was about the eighth sentence and which he answered in one sentence. A first printing of this item said one question was asked of him and it is wrong; the page prints two. The chapter's dateline carries four hours and not three, because the body stages events at the second, the sixth, the eighth and the eleventh.** He spoke twice. The first was nine printed sentences in nine turns, and the ninth sentence is that the eighth was for the man with the sheet and not for the man with the barrow, and the copyist of about twenty-six wrote all nine on a leaf with no head over it and ruled nothing under them and left about two-thirds of it blank. **The record this room has been keeping for four months is a record of his own command failure, and he said so once, and about four of the nine people in the room believe him and about five do not, and he did not correct anybody, and the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat got up off the wall and stood at the open window and sat down again and said nothing.** The second time he spoke, he asked the man of about fifty-eight, out loud and in front of the other eight, to write his own name beside the chairman's own in the back of a book of carts, on the ground that **a book with one name in it is a record of a private man's failures and a book with two is a record anybody can be shown.** The man of about fifty-eight wrote both names, under nine market mornings of figures and above a list of four saddles, and did not read either of them out, and that is the second time he has written a name in that book and not read it out. **That is the whole of the decision, it is the only thing in ten days the protagonist causes, it puts his own name on a piece of paper that goes up a made road, and it is a cost and not a reward. He conducts nothing, gets nothing back, overrules nobody, and is the reason none of it works.** He is in Chapter 527 and says nothing there and **no chapter of this block prints a paragraph accounting for that silence and none may.**

1278. **The block's end, and the decision that is not a resolution.** On the seventh of Hearthmonth, YR 319, the room took about five hours to go over what it had and did not put one figure of people against another while it did. **The man of about fifty-eight stated the finding: a rule was changed by one man in a hand, and nobody in that room knows who he was, and nobody in that room is going to find out. He then decided to write down what the room had found and not to write down who, in the room's book, in his own hand, with the day and the hour against it, and to rule a head over that line, which is the first head he has ruled in that room in eleven years, and he gave the reason out loud: a rule that is not written down is not a rule, it is a habit, and a habit of four people in a hired room dies when the four of them are not in the room.** That decision is the first thing in eleven years that anybody in that county has decided to do on purpose and said out loud that they were doing it. **About four of the nine people in that room think writing it down is right and about five think it is a way of not doing the other thing, the two sets do not overlap, and the room did not vote.** The copyist of about twenty-six said she was not going to say thank you and wanted the record to say why: if a thing she did gets a head cut into the wood over it then it stops being a thing she did and starts being a rule, and she has a rule of her own and it is that she does not have rules. The man of about fifty-one in a grey coat said the cost out loud at the ninth hour and was not thanked, and **did not repeat the sentence he said in Chapter 513.** **Three documents are on that table and none of them is the form: the Crown form's fourth head is empty and has been for about four months, one woman's word is in a fourth head on a returned form, and a struck-out line is on a copy of a copy in an unsigned hand. The hearing is not adjourned, refused, retold or closed, and the last clause on the page is that the hearing stands.**
