1308. **Three things the volume's own records said, and the plan said, and did not survive being derived, and the pages are right in all three.** **First, the volume is two hundred and seventy days long inclusive and not two hundred and seventy-one.** 1650 − 1381 = 269 and 1650 − 1381 + 1 = 270, and the figure 271 is printed in `workspace/volume-11/close/PROMPT.md`, twice in `outline/volume-11.md`, once in `state/current.md` and once in the Volume 11 Batch 0004 entry of `state/batch-summaries.md`, and no file printed 270 before this phase. **Second, two chapters fall on a Sunday, at day 1434 and day 1483, and the close prompt and the live handoff both said that no chapter of Volume 11 falls on a Sunday and that the Sunday rule was therefore untested.** It was tested twice. At day 1434, the nineteenth of Fallowmonth YR 319, `chapter-0510.md`'s dateline reads *the room is shut on a Sunday and about four people are in it because the cart for a weigh-house two hundred miles off goes on Mondays and comes back on Sundays*, and the rule held. **At day 1483, the eighth of Wolfmonth YR 319, `chapter-0518.md`'s dateline reads *the room is shut on Sundays and is not shut today*, about nine people came in, a woman of about thirty-four said out loud that anybody who came in on a Sunday in that room would be washing the floor of it afterwards and nobody else, nobody argued with her, and the floor of that room has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth and was not washed in Chapter 0545 and nobody in that room said anything about it.** The rule was broken in public, on a day, and the price of breaking it is an object that is still dirty at the volume's last page. **Third, `outline/volume-11.md` §17 is wrong about seven calendar facts** — the duration, day 1626's weekday, day 1536's market flag, Block 0002's first day, Block 0004's first day, the fifty-four-days-per-block claim, and the header's copy of the duration — and the plan was not edited, and its rows for 1381, 1596 and 1650 and its list of month starts are all correct.

1309. **The close prompt's own standing instance for the column-ordinal check is wrong three times out of three, and this is the first finding in this project that indicts a document rather than a manuscript.** The prompt gives Chapter 0545 as calling a man's column his *eleventh* where his *ninth* stands in Chapter 0539, Chapter 0548 as calling another's his *fourteenth* where his *twelfth* stands in Chapter 0536, and Chapter 0549 as calling a third man's his *second* where his *second* stands in Chapter 0513 and his *third* in Chapter 0538. **The pages print the tenth, the thirteenth and the fourth.** The *eleventh* in Chapter 0545 is the eleventh of eleven houses and the woman of about twenty-seven who stands in its door; the word *fourteenth* does not occur in Chapter 0548 at all. **The six real ordinals are the man of thirty-two with a limp's ninth at day 1585 and tenth at day 1615, the man of about thirty-eight with a satchel's twelfth at day 1569 and thirteenth at day 1634, and the man of about fifty-one in a grey coat's second at day 1447, third at day 1578 and fourth at day 1641.** Three men, three columns, three monotone sequences, six figures, no conflict anywhere in the volume, and the three the prompt supplied are wrong and in each case the wrong number belongs to a different object in the same chapter.

1310. **Fifty-two speech-length certifications are printed in the volume and `state/current.md` certified that none were.** All fifty-four are in Chapters 501 to 520 and **none is printed in the last thirty chapters of the volume.** Every one carries the volume's own phrase *turns of one exchange*, and fifty of them are in the form *N words in M turns of one exchange* and two are at `chapter-0511.md` in the single-turn form. Re-footed off the page by a phase that did not write them, **fifty-two of fifty-two foot**, and three of them were wrong and are repaired: the 342-in-eight-turns at `chapter-0506.md` line 35, the 149-with-a-first-turn-of-four at `chapter-0506.md` line 69, and the 170-in-six-turns at `chapter-0510.md` line 21. **Three of the fifty-one the instrument returned clean were tokenizer false negatives and were confirmed by reading — 312 in `chapter-0516.md`, 200 in `chapter-0517.md` and 258 in `chapter-0520.md` — and the tell in all three is the instrument reading a spelled number in the chapter's own turn-list as an Arabic one; on the page they are 6 + 130 + 40 + 4 + 132, 6 + 11 + 3 + 106 + 29 + 45, and 205 + 6 + 47, all exact.** The live handoff's clause is corrected in this phase and the certification was false in the direction that hides work.

1311. **The Batch 0005 review's best finding was half a fix, and this close finished it: seventeen sites in ten chapters where the narrator stands outside the room.** The review found two chapters printing a phrase lifted verbatim out of their own prompt, in which the words *this project* are the author's and no register in this book has them, and it revoiced them and kept every fact. **What the revoicing kept was the typographic half of the same leak.** The residue was *it is said on the page once* at `chapter-0503.md`; *printed once on the page* at `chapter-0523.md`, the one file in this repository where the word that names the founding paper is written; *the reason somebody said no is on the page* at `chapter-0525.md`; three sites at `chapter-0542.md`, of which one is **the word *narration***; *I want it on the page* at `chapter-0543.md`; *on the page in the same words* at `chapter-0544.md`; two at `chapter-0545.md`, where a page and a bare leaf are confused in the one chapter that is about what a page costs; five at `chapter-0547.md`, of which two are the sentence the review wrote, *four lines apart on the page* and *a figure of nine hundred standing four lines from a figure of four hundred and nine*; *the reason is on the page* at `chapter-0549.md`; and *the column at the end of this page* at `chapter-0550.md`. **All seventeen are revoiced in the room's own idiom and every fact is kept, and the twenty patterns named in `state/current.md` now return 0 across all fifty chapters.** The class is a standing one and it is now eighteen batches running: **a rule about register cannot be enforced by a token count, because the words in it are ordinary English and every one of them is a word this book uses, and the list has to be printed and read by a person.** The fix the review made to its own sweep was to print the pattern list; that part stands and is inherited.

1312. **Four repairs the review did not make, three of them a claim the page contradicts.** In `chapter-0528.md` the paragraph that sets up the volume's second-best passage says *He spoke twice, and **the first time** he spoke it was about a man of about thirty-four with a sheet and it was nine sentences*, and the same page prints the man of about fifty-three with a barrow's question first with its one-word answer and the man with the sheet's second, and certifies the nine four paragraphs later with *And that was the nine*; it is **the second time**. In `chapter-0550.md` the dateline prints *the finding that room made in a month carried at thirty is in that room's book **on a page with no head over it***, and the same chapter prints four hundred words later that the finding went into the book with a pen and is under a head that is not its own; it is **under a head that is not its own**. In `chapter-0549.md` Gault Prentice says *about **four hundred and nine** have not* and uses the figure three times in one speech for people in a market town, and about four hundred and nine is about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month coming into a room two hundred miles off, and the page's own argument is that those three series are never converted; it is **the rest of them**. And in `chapter-0547.md` a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over the weigh-house is called *the copyist of about twenty-nine*, and the copyist in this volume is a woman of about twenty-six in the market town four days off and is named as such in seven chapters; she is **the woman of about twenty-nine**.

1313. **Nine repairs of figures, intervals and hedged counts, and each is a figure that was right on its own line and wrong against something three chapters or four hundred words away.** `chapter-0501.md` printed *that is Chapter 505 and it is nine days away* and now prints no chapter number and says that not one of the four is out of the satchel yet. `chapter-0509.md` printed a line of *eleven words* and the printed sentence is seventeen. `chapter-0510.md` printed *one hundred and seventy words in six turns* and the exchange is five turns of a hundred and thirty-eight. `chapter-0511.md` printed a man reading *four hundred* returns in two days and it is **about** four hundred. `chapter-0514.md` printed an answer of *about four words* and the page it prints is three sentences of 44 + 35 + 27 = 106. `chapter-0521.md` printed a claim that nobody had said the four words out loud *at all*, and the narrower true claim is that nobody had read them off *that* sheet, and its interval of *four days before, on a Friday* on a Wednesday is now *on the Friday before*. `chapter-0527.md` printed *cares about about four things*. `chapter-0530.md` printed *the third time* where the speaker has said it once and the woman of about thirty-four has said it twice. `chapter-0540.md` printed *for eleven days* and now prints *since the Thursday before*. And `chapter-0546.md` printed *about four hundred sheets a month* in the one column that enumerates all three series of the weigh-house, and the figure is **about nine hundred**, and **after the repair all forty-two printings of the phrase in the volume read *nine hundred***.

1314. **A woman of about thirty-one with a pen was called a man five times in two chapters, and a stone was put in the wrong county, and both are repaired.** `chapter-0546.md` printed her as *the man of about thirty-one with a pen* in narration, in a physical action, in a speech attribution and in a closing sentence, and `chapter-0548.md` printed her as a man in narration and in the sentence about thanking. **She is a woman in `chapter-0542.md`, `chapter-0547.md`, `chapter-0549.md` and `chapter-0550.md`, so the error is local to two chapters and is not a character the volume got wrong — it is an attribution a repair pass left behind**, and this is the fourth volume running in which a close has found a gender slip in a chapter that four surrounding chapters have right. And `chapter-0546.md` printed a piece of stone a foot and a half long as being *in a room in this county* in the same sentence that says *those are four documents in four counties*; it is **in another county**, and the sentence was right about the count and wrong about the county.

1315. **The measurements, recounted on the fifty files after the last prose edit, with every basis named, and both printings standing. These are the close's figures and item 1317 is the current one: a repair pass after this one took five words out of `chapter-0547.md`, so the volume, Block 0005 and the manuscript are each five lower than printed here, and every other figure in this item was recounted after those five words and did not move.** **The volume is 105,401 words in fifty chapters, on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the fifty added; Volumes 01 to 10 recount to 1,483,501 in five hundred chapters and the manuscript is 1,588,902 in five hundred and fifty. As this close received them the volume was 105,423 and the manuscript 1,588,924, and the whole of the difference is twenty-two words, and it is accounted for by class at `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md` §11.1.** Blocks **20,919 / 20,940 / 20,717 / 17,767 / 25,058**. `about` **2,752 at 26.11 per 1,000** case-insensitive and **2,728 at 25.88** lower-case-only, per block 571 / 557 / 547 / 406 / 671. `the whole of` **67**, per block 8 / 8 / 5 / 15 / 31. `about four people` **54**, per block 21 / 18 / 1 / 6 / 8, **and this is the first time the figure has been counted in three volumes and no ledger in this project tracks it.** The seven filler words at **194**: `held` 9, `steady` 2, `plain` 1, `counted` 22, `breathed` 0, `wrote` 160, `nodded` 0. Punctuation **68 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 10 semicolons, 9 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules**. Curly quotation marks **749 and 749**, balancing in every one of the fifty files, straight quotes **0**, curly apostrophes **0**, straight apostrophes **410**, bold parity even on every line of every file, all fifty files ending in a newline. Duplication, raw token, headings stripped, emphasis markers removed, eight words, a file never compared with itself: **34 words against the five hundred prior chapters, 35 within the volume, 18 for Block 0005 against the prior, 24 within Block 0005 with punctuation retained and 35 with it stripped.** Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more: **33 paragraphs in 67 chapter instances, 34 in 69, 32 in 65, and none of the three touches Volume 11.** Barred tokens, the five barred numerals and the kin-term at **0** in this volume, and the manuscript-scope figures are `hemp` 70 in 40 files, `thirty-six` 20 in 13, `fifty-two` 102 in 32, **`sixty-one` 143 in 52**, `sixty-six` 249 in 50 — **and the live handoff printed 142 for `sixty-one` and the one is in Volume 10 and not in this volume, which is the count-before-enforcing rule failing in a file a phase reads first and trusts.** `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `protagonist` `council` `hundredweight` `mechanic` `attribute` `status` `level` at **0** across all fifty chapters, and `failed` `roof` `coping` `seam` `charter` `brother` `melody` `Culling` `custodian` `Gatehouse` at **0**. The retired reporting frame, swept as a family, at **0**. **A capitalised two-word sweep returns `Aren Kest` 71 in 39 files, `Gault Prentice` 23 in 9, `Mara Vey` 22 in 3, `Nesta Braith` 22 in 15, `Hester Ollett` 3 in 1, the three words for one lane at 23 in all, and one paper's title at `chapter-0523.md`; the name budget is 2 + 0 + 0 + 1 + 0 = 3 of 5 with two slots unspent.**

1316. **What the close did not change, and the list is the point.** The unattributed two-line statement is still four of four and no fifth is printed. **The word that names the founding paper is still printed once, at `chapter-0523.md`, and this close did not remove it and did not print it.** The press still has no opening day and the bell was not rung. The corridor's rate is still not a figure and its rule is still bad. The about eleven hundred are still on nothing. The shut road is still shut and unscheduled. The two villages are still off the road and are still not told why. The offer is still unanswered. **The four names are still off the letter and the letter is still in a coat.** The breach between the two men is still open and the forty days of silence are still not a reconciliation. The mechanism is still unexplained and there is still no System panel in the volume. The city is still on a shelf and its three protected numerals are at zero. The valley is still unreachable. **The half leaf is still face down, it was cut on the eighth of Thawmonth YR 320 at day 1603, it came back on the sixth of Mudmonth at day 1631, 1650 − 1631 = 19, and the nineteen days Chapter 0550 prints is exact against the day the volume's last page is set on; nobody turned it over and this close did not schedule anybody turning it over and did not print what is on the other side of it.** The twenty-second notch is still uncut and nobody has been sent to that coast. The three words for one lane are still three. The Crown's form's fourth head is still empty. The fifth line is still ruled and nobody ordered it. The register is still at seven questions and no findings and the five nines are still five nines. The twenty-two notches are still years and not tides. The finding is still in the room's book under a head that is not its own and the fourth line still carries a man's name. **The four roads that are not roads were still not named and nobody nearly named them.** The room's floor has not been washed since the eighth of Wolfmonth. The hearing stands and there is no bench. The name of the antagonist is still not written anywhere, including here. The two unspent name slots are still unspent. The three bars were not merged and no figure was printed for any of them. **And the twenty-five words that recur word for word in `chapter-0546.md` and `chapter-0549.md` describing two different acts are still recurring, because a close records that and does not choose between two sentences that are each true.**

1317. **The Volume 11 close's own review, five findings, two of them repairs to the pages and three of them repairs to the records, and the record figures after them.** **A review of the close pass was run against the fifty files and it reported five defects, of which four were repairable here and one is controller-owned and is reported and not touched. The two prose repairs are both one line each and neither moves a fact, a name, a date, a figure of speech, or anything in `outline/volumes/volume-11-close.md` §11.3's list of what the close did not change.** In `chapter-0506.md` l.49 a woman of about thirty-one with a pen was called *the man of about thirty-one with a pen* in the third sentence of Mara Vey's third speech, and she is that woman ten times in the same chapter and correctly a woman in `chapter-0542.md`, `chapter-0547.md`, `chapter-0549.md` and `chapter-0550.md`; the clause that follows it, *and I would tell her before anybody else*, is a **different** woman — Mara Vey is one of the two women of twenty-nine in `chapter-0506.md` l.77 and l.53, and the *her* was always right and was not touched. That is the last standalone *man of about thirty-one with a pen* in five hundred and fifty chapters. In `chapter-0547.md` l.33 the close's own row 34 replaced *and it is on the page because* with *nobody wrote it down and it is here because*, and the sentence already carried *and nobody wrote it down* four words earlier, so the line printed the clause twice in succession; the second copy is removed, the row's replacement is corrected from +3 to **−2**, and the chapter's total movement falls from +9 to **+4**. The third repair is a count: the close enumerated **forty-one** rows in §11.1 and printed *thirty-eight* in five paragraphs, and thirty-eight was wrong everywhere it appeared, and the count is now **forty-three** with the review's two repairs in the table. The fourth is a table: `state/current.md` printed the five block lengths as **54 / 51 / 54 / 52 / 54** beside five correct start days, which is 265 against a 270-day volume, and the lengths are **57 / 51 / 56 / 52 / 54**; the start days were the sound column and were not moved, and the two block boundaries the error hid are after Block 0001 and after Block 0003. **The calendar was re-derived to check this and it foots end to end with a thirty-day year and the year turning at Thawmonth: 1381 Rainmonth 26 YR 319, 1438 Fallowmonth 23, 1489 Wolfmonth 14, 1545 Hearthmonth 10, 1597 Thawmonth 2 YR 320, 1650 Mudmonth 25, and every one of the five is a dateline the volume actually prints.** **The measurements after this pass, recounted on the fifty files: the volume is 105,396 words in fifty chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own; Volumes 01 to 10 recount to 1,483,501 and the manuscript is 1,588,897. Blocks 20,919 / 20,940 / 20,717 / 17,767 / 25,053. `about` 2,752 at 26.11 per 1,000 case-insensitive and 2,728 at 25.88 lower-case-only, per block 571 / 557 / 547 / 406 / 671, and neither the numerator nor the rate moved, because the five words that went were *and nobody wrote it down*. The net against the close's receiving figures is −27 and not the −22 the close printed.** The fifth finding is not repaired: `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` asks for Chapters 521 to 530, which have been on disk since Batch 0003, it has no `.done` and no `.retired`, and the dispatch loop reads `.retired` — **and writing that marker is a change to phase selection, which is not this project's to make, so it is reported and left.**

## Volume 11, Batch 0003 — verification and repair pass, items 1318 to 1327, and the highest number in this file is now 1327

**1318. The false scope on the Batch 0003 prompt, and what it cost to find out.** `workspace/volume-11/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` carries a `# SCOPE OF THIS RUN` header produced by `scripts/rescue_overscoped.py`, which reads the first four lines of a prompt and takes the first `Chapters N to M` it finds. **The first four lines of this prompt say *Continue the novel after the completed phase Volume 11 Batch 0002 (Chapters 511 to 520)*, so the script scoped the run to Chapters 511 to 515 — five chapters of the previous, retired batch.** Those five chapters are finished reviewed prose and Chapters 516 to 520 are built on them, so the scoped range cannot be written without destroying the volume. **This is the third false scope in this project's record and it is the same class as the other two: a number handed to a writer that is true about a different object.** Item 1317 already recorded that this prompt asks for Chapters that are on disk and has neither a `.done` nor a `.retired` marker; this item records what the rescue script then did about it.

**1319. What was done instead, and the basis.** **The ten chapters the prompt body is for, 521 to 530, were verified and repaired, and nothing else was written.** Chapters 511 to 515 were left untouched. No marker was written, no next-phase prompt was created, and no controller file was edited.

**1320. The six defects, which are six numbers checked against other numbers and not against the prose.** **`chapter-0522.md`**: a paragraph claimed a man was asked nothing and said four enumerated things, of which none is in his mouth and the chapter prints two turns, and `was asked nothing` is contradicted by a printed question eleven lines later. **`chapter-0528.md`**: a paragraph claimed he spoke twice, against five further printed occasions. **`chapter-0528.md`**: the month of the unanswered question by the man with the sheet was Harvestmonth against Fallowmonth in two other chapters including one in the same mouth, and Harvestmonth is the earlier month, so the repair moves Chapter 528 and not the other two. **`chapter-0528.md`**: nine sentences printed in ten spans with nothing on the page to say the ninth is one turn, repaired by four words. **`chapter-0529.md`**: a paragraph enumerated three things a man said, none of them on the page, and the chapter's only sentence from Aren Kest was reported in narration and never printed. **`chapter-0530.md`**: **not a defect, and this entry is corrected accordingly.** A man claims he had said a sentence once before and asks for it to be minuted as the fourth time. The four occasions are the woman of about thirty-four in Harvestmonth at `chapter-0505.md`, the man in Embermonth at `chapter-0514.md`, the woman in Wolfmonth at `chapter-0524.md` and the man here, and the man said it **once** before, so the original page was right. **A third pass read the two matching months as two matching speakers, changed the page to *twice before, in Embermonth and in Wolfmonth*, and thereby contradicted `chapter-0524.md` l.29, which prints the woman as the speaker and names the man as the second of the three; the change has been reverted and the chapter is byte-for-byte what it was.** The *fourth time* and the woman's *twice* were correct throughout and are still correct.

**1321. The word that names the founding paper, still printed once, and the check that was run rather than assumed.** A manuscript sweep returns the word at **one hit in one file, `chapter-0523.md`**, spoken aloud by a person in a room where somebody had to decide something, with the nine words *Nothing shuts but two who did not propose it* printed immediately before it and the chapter saying on the page that it is printed once. **The nine words in the margin of the copy of the copy are a different nine — *Leave the last line blank until you are told* — and they are printed once, at `chapter-0521.md`, and no chapter says whose they are.** The nine-word sentence on the strip is a third nine, *A thing once asked is a thing twice agreed*, printed once at `chapter-0524.md`. **None of the three nines is added to another, and the count under the fourth step, the day's book, the bench's nine and the clerk of about twenty-two's nine leaves are untouched.**

**1322. The calendar, derived twice, and the outline's own anchor error, reported and not repaired.** From day 1381 as the twenty-sixth of Rainmonth YR 319 and a Wednesday: **weekday half 0 mismatches over 10 datelines, day-of-month half 0 mismatches over 10, market-morning claim 0 mismatches over 10.** The ten days are 1489, 1493, 1496, 1500, 1503, 1508, 1513, 1521, 1529 and 1542. **`outline/volume-11.md`'s calendar table gives day 1626 as a Thursday and it derives as a Wednesday from the same anchor that gives the table's own day 1650 as a Saturday; the outline is not this project's to edit and no chapter of the block is set on or after 1596, so nothing on a page is affected and the finding is reported.**

**1323. The money, read on the page.** Ten columns, ten footed item for item, sum for sum and total for total, ten distinct totals at 99, 92, 111, 117, 125, 137, 145, 151, 158 and 165, **0 collisions with the hundred and ten spent columns, 0 barrelled totals, and the longest run any one column makes against any other in the block is one word.** Item counts 9, 10, 8, 11, 10, 12, 9, 11, 11, 12 against the eight-to-twelve band, and twenty-two distinct prices from 2 to 31 pence.

**1324. The duplication, on the basis named.** Raw-token, headings stripped, eight-word seed, maximal extension, per-file index: **longest maximal shared run 8 words against the five hundred and forty prior chapter files and 8 words among the ten new, both being the seed length and neither extended.** Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more: **0 against the whole manuscript and 0 among the ten new, and the two are reported in one sentence because a number without its scope is a claim about a different book.**

**1325. The counts that would change a decision.** Block 0003 is **20,672** in ten chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own. Volume 11 is **105,351** in fifty. The manuscript is **1,588,852** in five hundred and fifty. Volumes 01 to 10 recount to **1,483,501**, unchanged, which is the independent check that only the three moved chapters moved. **`about` is 548 in 20,672, which is 26.51 per 1,000** on the declared whole-word-over-whitespace basis with a case-insensitive numerator. `Aren` 21 and `Kest` 21, reported separately. **Eight printed speech-length figures, eight foot, none preceded by *about*, and none moved by this pass.** Barred tokens, barred numerals and the Volume 11 zero list at 0 in the ten files, reported at block scope and not at manuscript scope. A self-reference sweep across twenty-four patterns at 0. **The three figures above were the state after the third pass; they are recounted here after the fourth pass, which reverted `chapter-0530.md` and moved Block 0003 by −3, and the recount is on the files as they now stand.**

**1326. What did not move, and it is the whole of what matters.** The fourth head of the Crown's form is empty and nobody filled it. The twenty-second notch is not cut. The word that names the founding paper is printed once. The hearing stands. The antagonist is not named. No name was spent: **2 + 0 = 2 of 5, three slots unspent.** The three words for one lane are still three and were not scheduled for choosing. The four heads are four and the fourth is empty; the fifth line is a fifth line; the struck-out line is a struck-out line and not an empty one and not a filled one. No System panel, no crossing, nothing opened, nobody sent to the shut road four hundred miles off.

**1327. The lesson, and it is the one this file has now run for twenty-nine batches.** **The cheapest defect in this pass was a month, and the second cheapest was a man counting his own speeches, and neither was visible to a sweep, and all six were visible to a person reading the page against the sentence that describes it.** Every one of the six was a figure — a count of things said, a count of occasions, a month, a turn — and not one of them was a measurement of the manuscript. **A sweep counts tokens and a claim about a person has to be read, and this is the second time in two blocks that the whole of a block's defects has been in that class and none of it in the arithmetic.**

**1328. The review of Batch 0003, five findings, one prose repair that is a reversal, one record repair on twenty-one figures, one markup repair, one finding that is not a defect, and one that is not this project's to close.** **A review of the third pass was run against the fifty files and it reported five items; three were repaired here, one was checked and found to be a false finding, and one is controller-owned and is reported and left. Nothing was restarted and no good prose was replaced.** **The first is a high finding and it is that the third pass's own repair to `chapter-0530.md` was the defect.** That pass read *I have said it in this room once before, in Embermonth* as a miscount, because the man of about thirty-four says *A county is not a man* at `chapter-0514.md` on the twenty-first of Embermonth and again — so the pass held — at `chapter-0524.md` on the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth; **but at `chapter-0524.md` l.29 the speaker is the woman of about thirty-four, and the chapter names the man as the second of the three in its own next clause, *the first of the three was hers in Harvestmonth and the second was the man of about thirty-four's in Embermonth and this is the third*.** The man said it once. The four occasions are the woman in Harvestmonth, the man in Embermonth, the woman in Wolfmonth and the man in Hearthmonth, and the page was right and the *fourth time* and the woman's *twice* were right all along. **The repair is reverted and `chapter-0530.md` is byte-for-byte what it was before the third pass touched it; the error had been propagated into this file at 1320, into `state/open-threads.md` at 321, into `state/chapter-summaries.md` in the Chapter 530 entry and into `state/batch-summaries.md` item 6, and all four records now say the opposite and name the reversal.** **The second is that `state/current.md` set the standard with a sentence about not carrying a count forward and then did it: the third pass recounted the words and did not recount a single mark, sweep or name.** Twenty-one distinct figures in that file were stale against the fifty files, in five groups — five word and block totals, three marks, ten `about` and filler figures, and three name figures. They were `about` 2,752 to 2,753 case-insensitive and 2,728 to 2,730 lower-case, the rates 26.11 to 26.13 and 25.88 to 25.91, Block 0003's 547 to 548 and its 26.40 to 26.51, `the whole of` 67 to 66, `about four people` 54 to 53, `wrote` 160 to 158 and the filler total 194 to 192, curly quotation marks 749 and 749 to 751 and 751, straight apostrophes 410 to 409, the doubled-word figure 0 to 2, and `Aren Kest` 71 in 39 files to 75 in 41 with `Kest` 71 to 75 in two places. Every one is now recounted on the fifty files as they stand, and the review's own table of corrected figures was itself wrong once, giving the lower-case rate as 25.92 where 2,730 in 105,351 derives as 25.91. **The third is markup: `state/current.md` l.11 carried an odd number of emphasis markers, five, and an unclosed run bleeding into the next sentence, and the line is now even like every other line in every file. It is worth recording that the gate which catches this is a count of the marker, and that a count of the marker cannot tell a deliberate marker inside a code span from a real one — so a line may fail the gate honestly and the fix is to rephrase and not to add a second marker.** **The fourth is a finding the review reported and this pass did not act on, because the summary is right and the page agrees with it: Chapter 528's nine sentences begin at `chapter-0528.md` l.9 with *That man has been up and down this lane*, the eighth is *No,* at l.37, and the ninth is the two spans of one turn at l.41 and l.43, and the page itself says *because the ninth sentence was not finished*, so the enumeration in `state/chapter-summaries.md` foots and was left alone.** **The fifth is the marker and the missing forward phase, and it is not this project's to close — see `state/open-threads.md` 328.** **The measurements after this pass, recounted on the fifty files: the volume is 105,351 words in fifty chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own; Volumes 01 to 10 recount to 1,483,501 and the manuscript is 1,588,852. Blocks 20,919 / 20,940 / 20,672 / 17,767 / 25,053. `about` 2,753 at 26.13 per 1,000 case-insensitive and 2,730 at 25.91 lower-case-only, per block 571 / 557 / 548 / 406 / 671, and the net against the close's receiving figures is −72 rather than the −69 the third pass reported.** **What did not move: the fourth head of the Crown's form is empty, the twenty-second notch is not cut, the word that names the founding paper is printed once at `chapter-0523.md`, the half leaf is still face down, the hearing stands, the antagonist is not named, the mechanism is not explained, no System panel is printed, no name was spent, and the three protected numerals of the city on the shelf are at zero.**

## Volume 12, Batch 0001 — Chapters 551 to 560, "People Who Want That Life", items 1329 to 1336, and the highest number in this file is now 1336

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1328, and this section continues from it. Nothing in the canon above this line changed. The plot, the ending, the dates, the weekdays, the money, the names, the prohibitions, the name budget and the antagonist are exactly as items 1307 to 1328 left them. `outline/volume-12.md` is new and is a plan document, not a change to any of the above, and `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` are fixed and were not touched.**

1329. **The volume, and why a Volume 12 outline exists at all.** `outline/volume-12.md` is **The Garden of Endings, Chapters 551 to 600, dominant pressure mystery and investigation, recovery anchor a season in Merefen, relationship work the line that a beautiful place can still be an owned place.** Six months in the main world and eight months in the Quiet Orchard, matching the Volume 12 rows of `outline/series.md`. Five blocks of ten: Block 0001 Chapters 551 to 560, Block 0002 561 to 570, Block 0003 571 to 580, Block 0004 581 to 590, Block 0005 591 to 600. **The volume's question in its operational form: a place where nothing has been decided for twenty-two years offers to prove that a man can keep roads open, and the proof requires him to sign — what is the smallest thing he can do that is not a certificate, and who has to be asked first.**

1330. **The one inherited instruction this volume overrides, and the reason, on the record.** `state/open-threads.md` item 318 carries, in the list of what Volume 12 must not do, **"do not conduct a crossing or schedule one."** That clause was written by the Volume 11 close, whose own volume conducted none, and it was written at a moment when no Volume 12 outline existed and the next volume was assumed to be another fifty chapters in the same room. **`outline/series.md` is the plan of record and it is fixed, and its time ledger gives Volume 12 eight months in the Quiet Orchard, and its Volume 12 progression gives an emissary who asks, citizens who do not know how much their calm rests on a buried memory, a generation that chose to bury dissent and then forgot it had chosen, a reopening of grief and argument and choice, and a delegate who insists on the right to refuse contact. A Volume 12 with no crossing in it is a different book.** **The override is scoped as narrowly as the plan allows: no crossing is conducted, booked or scheduled in Block 0001; the mechanism is not explained in any of the five blocks; the roadmark is not looked at; the notebook's separate leaf with a seventh line that says *a person* is not looked at; a System panel is printed nowhere in the volume; and the words *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are printed nowhere in it.** The crossing is made in Block 0002 on a road opened by a person answering a question.

1331. **The world Volume 12 opens, in full, because it has to survive being summarised later.** **The Quiet Orchard** is a valley of terraced fruit on a long slope above a river, four days and about nine of hard going from the nearest made road, with no made road to it. **From the top of the slope down: the High Set, the Middle Set, the Long Set of about eleven terraces, and the Wet Set at the foot by the water. Between the High Set and the Middle Set is the cut, about four miles of stone-lined channel about two paces wide with a lip on the high side, which carries the upper water down and is the only thing in the valley that has ever needed mending on a day anybody chose. The settlement is Rooksend, at the middle terrace. The press-house stands at the foot of the Long Set.** The Orchard's governing body is **the Standing**, its own word, which meets when there is fruit, **and the manuscript's barred word for a council is not written anywhere in this volume and the Standing is not one.** **The valley's own word for the person who may speak for a road is an answer, and the valley has not had one in twenty-two years, and nobody in it has noticed, and that is the volume's central fact and it is a fact about an office and not about a person.** The valley's own name for what its grandparents' Standing did twenty-two winters ago is **the putting-away**: a strip of ground at the foot of the Long Set asked to be let go, about ninety people went out on it, the record of the going was put in the ground under the oldest tree in the Long Set, and an oath was taken that no argument about a set would ever be written down. **The oath and the burial are the same act, and the one thing that makes the peace possible is the one thing that makes the peace unchangeable.** **The word *quiet* is in the main world's name for the valley and is not in the valley's own name, which is the Orchard; the word came from a report nobody in nine counties can produce and it is the Standing's word and not the valley's, and the volume explains that in Block 0003 and not before.**

1332. **The Orchard's calendar, which is the third non-convertible frame in this manuscript.** **The fruit year has five sittings — bar, blossom, set, swell, pick — and a year is a turn of the five, and the word *year* is not used in a character's mouth there. An Orchard dateline carries a sitting and an hour and carries neither a month and a weekday nor an ebb and a tide, and no chapter of the volume may put a main-world month into an Orchard's mouth.** A yard keeps its count in days, a road keeps its count in tides, a path community keeps its count in crossings open and weeks shut. **The eight-month path interval and the four-month main-world interval are named once, converted once, and never converted again.**

1333. **The four new people in this block and the two that cost a name slot, with what each one wants.** **Merritt Gallow, about fifty-three, brought a letter four hands long and eleven people, asks a man with a limp to certify a valley as the one stable road, has not lied once in this volume, is right about the load-bearing thing — a road that can be found out about can be closed — and is wrong about the size of a room and about what a certificate is for. He admitted at the ninth hour on the sixteenth of Greenmonth that he was sent and would not say by whom, and said that he was sent to find out whether that valley has an answer, and would not spend a woman nine paces from a door in a street. Ilsa Wend, about thirty-eight, keeps a block of ground in the Long Set, sent the cutting on her own initiative without asking and without telling the Standing, walked nine days each way, is on no sheet in nine counties, and wants to be able to say that a thing she did was wrong without being the one who has to move; her sentence came out in the oldest voice in the valley and she could hear it come out and it was not hers.** **A woman of about forty-five from a river city is one of the eleven and the only one of the eleven who is looking at a different object; she said the word *continuation* once in a market and would not say it twice. A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a key is not new and not a name.** The name budget for Volume 12 is five and **this block spends two, so the arithmetic is 2 of 5 with three slots unspent.**

1334. **The calendar, derived twice from the single anchor of day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318 and a Sunday.** Ten datelines at days 1651, 1654, 1657, 1662, 1663, 1666, 1669, 1671, 1674 and 1679, **0 / 0 / 0 mismatches on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, reported as three figures and not as one.** They are Mudmonth 26, Mudmonth 29, Greenmonth 2, Greenmonth 7, Greenmonth 8, Greenmonth 11, Greenmonth 14, Greenmonth 16, Greenmonth 19 and Greenmonth 24, all YR 320, and their weekdays are Sunday, Wednesday, Saturday, Thursday, Friday, Monday, Thursday, Saturday, Tuesday and Sunday. **Four of the ten fall on a market morning and two of the ten fall on a Sunday, and the chapter in which the floor is washed is one of the two Sundays.** The block runs day 1651 to day 1686 and the volume runs day 1651 to day 1830, five blocks of thirty-six days inclusive each, **180 inclusive against the 180 the volume's own first and last datelines span, and the year does not turn inside the volume because Thawmonth the first of YR 321 is day 1956.**

1335. **The measurements, recounted on the ten files after the last prose edit, with every basis named.** **The block is 18,755 words in ten chapters on a plain whitespace split with the title line included, each file on its own and the ten added; the five hundred and fifty chapters before it recount to 1,588,852, which is the independent check that only the new chapters moved, and the manuscript is 1,607,607 in five hundred and sixty chapters.** Per chapter 551 2,058, 552 2,109, 553 1,975, 554 1,888, 555 1,788, 556 1,618, 557 1,829, 558 1,821, 559 1,703, 560 1,966. **Money: ten footed columns at 96, 88, 91, 98, 119, 134, 143, 154, 169 and 177 pence, item for item, sum for sum, printed total for printed total and mark-conversion for mark-conversion, every column verified off its own page after the last edit, ten distinct totals, and 0 collisions against every printed *is … pence* total extracted from all five hundred and sixty chapter files on disk.** Item counts 9, 10, 10, 10, 10, 10, 10, 8, 10, 12; prices 1 to 39; a mark at forty-eight pence in every conversion. **Punctuation 9 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 2 semicolons, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 0 rules. Curly quotation marks 85 and 85, per file 9 / 10 / 8 / 7 / 9 / 10 / 11 / 10 / 6 / 5; straight quotation marks 0; curly apostrophes 0 and straight apostrophes 69; per-line bold parity even on every line of every file; space-before-punctuation 0; double blank lines 0; doubled words 0; all ten files end in a newline.** `about` **440 at 23.46 per 1,000** case-insensitive and **433 at 23.09** lower-case-only. **`the whole of` 19. The seven filler words at 31**: `held` 1, `steady` 0, `plain` 0, `counted` 12, `breathed` 0, `wrote` 18, `nodded` 0, and the total of the seven is 31. **Duplication, raw token, headings stripped, emphasis markers removed, eight words, a file never compared with itself: 9 words against the five hundred and fifty prior chapters, and 21 words within the block, and the 21 is the sentence that says the question under the fourth head is the one from Thawmonth, shared by Chapters 559 and 560, and it is a fact about an instrument and not about a frame. Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, whitespace-normalised, markers stripped: 35 paragraphs in 71 chapter instances across the manuscript, and exactly one touches this block, being the nine-word question at `chapter-0542.md` and `chapter-0559.md`, which is one document and not two.** **`Aren` 9 and `Kest` 9, reported separately. Barred tokens, the barred numerals and the retired reporting frame at 0 in the ten files. A self-reference sweep across twenty named patterns at 0.**

1336. **The four defects this block's own first drafting contained, all found by reading and not by a sweep, and all four are the class this project has paid for in eight consecutive batches.** **A man of about sixty-one was a figure this block was not allowed to print, and he became a man of about sixty. A money total of one hundred and fifty-two pence contains the barred numeral fifty-two inside the number itself, so the column was re-ordered to foot at one hundred and fifty-four and the total was not moved, because the correct repair for a barred figure is to change the column and never the printed total.** Four instances of the narrator standing outside the room, of which three were the words *this chapter* and *this volume* and one was *the page says*, were revoiced in the room's own idiom with every fact kept. **And one money column footed, summed and converted correctly, printed a correct total, and was still wrong, because it contained the word *sixty* in an item name; a column is not only an arithmetic object.** **One structural correction is worth naming because it changed the world and not the prose: the eleven who came were said in three chapters to have walked four days, had come on a cart, and had been in the town for two days, and the three are now one arrival on the Saturday before the first chapter of the block with two of the eleven arriving on the Monday, and every chapter says the same thing.**

## The review of Volume 12 Batch 0001 and the repairs it forced, items 1337 to 1346

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1336. Nothing below moves a plot beat, an ending, a date, a name, a money price, a prohibition or a chapter, and nothing adds or removes a chapter. Every repair is one of the three the house allows: add a pence to a column and foot it again, re-word and re-order without touching a figure, or revoice a sentence in the room's own idiom with every fact kept.**

1337. **Four money columns collided with footed columns in earlier volumes and have been re-footed.** Chapter 0551 was 96 pence against `volume-10/chapter-0480.md`, 0552 was 88 against `volume-09/chapter-0449.md`, 0553 was 91 against `volume-10/chapter-0481.md`, and 0554 was 98 against `volume-10/chapter-0471.md`. **They are now 100, 104, 109 and 111.** The repair added pence to one item in each column and moved the printed total and the mark conversion to match, which is the same repair the block already used to clear one hundred and fifty-two pence of the barred numeral fifty-two. **No price was lowered and no total was adjusted by hand.**

1338. **The basis that finds a collision, and why the basis the state file printed could not.** The house form of a footed column's total is `A mark is forty-eight pence and X pence is`, and that form alone returns **91 distinct totals in 93 instances** across the five hundred and fifty chapters before this block; the block's ten are clear against that set. **A bare `is … pence` sweep over the same chapters returns 130 distinct values and includes the mark's own definition 154 times, so it cannot separate a column's total from a price inside a sentence, and a claim of *0 collisions* resting on it is a claim about a different measurement.** A successor should extract the house form and print which form it used.

1339. **Two money columns were clones of Volume 11 columns and have been re-worded and re-ordered at their existing prices.** `chapter-0557.md` shared **62 words** with `volume-11/chapter-0546.md` and `chapter-0558.md` shared **37 words** with `volume-11/chapter-0545.md`, in each case the same ten or eight items at the same prices in the same order with only the last price or two changed. **Every price, every total and every mark conversion in both is exactly as it was: 143 pence at Chapter 0557 and 154 pence at Chapter 0558.** **The lesson, and it is the same lesson the state file prints about register: an item list is a list of ordinary nouns and ordinary prices and no sweep in this repository can see that it is somebody else's.**

1340. **One speech was lifted out of Volume 11 and put in a different mouth.** `chapter-0558.md` had a man of about fifty-one in a grey coat delivering twenty-nine words of the speech that closes Volume 11 at `chapter-0550.md`. **It is revoiced in the grey coat's own idiom with every fact and every figure kept: about nine people in the room over the saddler's on a Wednesday, about four hundred in that street on a Thursday, five months, a thing meant to stay unseen from the street, a woman who said a word and about nine people deciding what to do about a word instead of about a letter, and the sentence said in that room in Thawmonth.** Nothing in it is now Aren's sentence and nothing in it was removed.

1341. **The retired reporting frame is at 0 in these ten chapters and is not at 0 in the manuscript.** Four instances were found in the block — `have said since that` twice in `chapter-0557.md` and `chapter-0558.md`, `has said since that Monday that` in `chapter-0558.md`, and `nobody has said since that anybody was in it` in `chapter-0559.md` — and all four were revoiced with every fact kept. **The same sweep returns 939 across the five hundred and fifty chapters before this block, and its commonest lead words are *said*, *out* and *saying*, so the pattern is ordinary English in this book and a zero gate on it would be a gate that lies in the useful direction.** The state file's claim of 0 across five hundred and sixty chapters was false and is corrected.

1342. **The block's calendar was wrong in three documents at once and the seven days are now accounted for.** `outline/volume-12.md` §8 gave Block 0001 days 1651 to 1686, §17 marked 1686 its last day, and `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` asserted both, while Chapter 0560 is the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth, which is day 1679. **Days 1680 to 1686, being Greenmonth 25 to Longlight 1, had no chapter and a successor would have skipped them silently.** They are now the first seven of Block 0002, Block 0002 runs day 1680 to day 1722, and the volume's five blocks are **29 / 43 / 36 / 36 / 36 days, beginning on days 1651, 1680, 1723, 1759 and 1795, which is 180 against the 180 the volume's own first and last datelines span.** No market-day figure moved: the volume still carries fifty-one and the block still carries four.

1343. **The party is eleven and the room's attendance is nine, and Chapter 0560 now says so.** Nine of the eleven were in the market town on the Sunday the room was shut and two came on the Monday, and `chapter-0552.md` says both halves and says the two were not counted into the nine. **Chapter 0560 had a bare *about nine people* in a list of four figures eighteen lines below *about eleven people who had come up a made road*, with nothing saying which nine; the nine is now named as the people who stood under the board outside the weigh-house on the Sunday the room was shut, and the page says the nine and the eleven are one party on two days and are not two parties and are added to nothing.** The block's repeated *about four of the nine* and *about five of the nine* is a split of the room's opinion and is **not** the outline's split of the party's eleven.

1344. **The outline's split of the eleven has not been put on the page and is the one thing Batch 0002 may add.** `outline/volume-12.md` §6 item 4 holds that about four of the eleven have seen the valley and about seven have not, and that the two figures are never added. **It is not on any page in Block 0001.** It is recorded in the outline now, with the note that the block's four-and-five of the nine is a different series, so that a successor cannot read one as the other.

1345. **Three figures in `state/current.md` were wrong and are corrected, and the class is a number attached to the wrong basis.** The `about` rate was printed as 23.46 per 1,000 on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator of 440, which is not its rate; **the three bases are 440 case-insensitive whole-word, 440 on a token equality, 434 lower-case whole-word and 439 with surrounding punctuation stripped, and the rate is 23.37 or 23.05 as the basis is chosen.** The block's item count was printed as 106 and is 99, an arithmetic slip on a correct count. The item prices were printed as 1 to 41 and run 1 to 40.

1346. **The claim that the two legs of the duplication measure were 9 and 21 words was false, and the whole of what the review found is in `state/current.md`.** The block's longest run against the five hundred and fifty prior chapters is **28 words and is the nine-word question printed as a document**, the run within the block is **21 words and is the same instrument**, and the two the file did not see were the cloned columns at item 1339 and the lifted speech at item 1340. **What remains is the volume's own frame and refrain — the room's location frame, the *cost of that Thursday is a column* frame, and the bare leaf — at 12 to 25 words, and Volume 11 shipped 18 to 26 on the same frames with a maximum of 34, so a successor should read 28 as inside the house band rather than treat the frame as the defect.**

## Volume 12, Batch 0002 — Chapters 561 to 570, items 1347 to 1360

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1346 and this section continues from it. Every figure below was counted on the chapter files after the last prose edit of this pass, and each carries its basis, because a figure without a basis is a claim about a different book. This section is the record of the writing pass, not of a review; a successor must re-count before it quotes anything here.**

1347. **The block, the days, and the two frames, and the three are not added.** Block 0002 is **18,429 words in ten chapters**, plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added, recounted after the second review pass's last prose edit. Per chapter **561 2,273 · 562 1,748 · 563 1,518 · 564 1,769 · 565 1,750 · 566 1,906 · 567 1,693 · 568 1,554 · 569 2,201 · 570 2,017.** The block sits inside a declared span of **day 1680 to day 1722, forty-three days inclusive**, and the span is honoured as a span and not as a list of chapter days. **The block prints two main-world datelines and no more, at day 1685 and day 1711, both derived from day 930 and both reproducing the anchor exactly: Chapter 0561 is the thirtieth of Greenmonth YR 320, a Saturday and a market day, and Chapter 0569 is the twenty-sixth of Longlight YR 320, a Thursday and a market day.** **The first printing of this item gave *ten* main-world days — 1685, 1686, 1689, 1692, 1696, 1700, 1704, 1708, 1711 and 1722 — and that list was a figure its own method could not have produced: seven of the nine values are not a date anywhere in the block, the eighth, 1722, is the block's last day and carries no chapter, and only 1685 and 1711 are on the page. The list is withdrawn. Days 1680 to 1684 and days 1712 to 1722 carry no chapter, and both stretches are the ordinary condition for a block in this manuscript and are not a hole.** **Two of the ten chapters print a main-world dateline and eight print an Orchard one, and the two sets are not added and the eight are not a subset of a market-day list.** Chapter 0561 prints **the thirtieth of Greenmonth, YR 320, a Saturday and a market day** and Chapter 0569 prints **the twenty-sixth of Longlight, YR 320, a Thursday and a market day**; both reproduce the derivation exactly. **The eight Orchard datelines carry a sitting and an hour and carry no month, no weekday and no tide, and 0 / 0 / 0 is the return on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim across the ten.** Four of the ten fall on a main-world market day (1685, 1692, 1704, 1711) and **two of those four are printed and two are not, and no Orchard chapter claims a market morning anywhere in its text.** Two of the ten fall on a main-world Sunday, at 1686 and 1700, and **neither of them is the room over a saddler's, which is shut on Sundays and is four days' carting and a day's walk away.**

1348. **The money, on the page and not from a state file: ten footed columns, one in every chapter, at 147, 128, 159, 173, 183, 187, 199, 178, 164 and 191 pence.** Item counts **10, 9, 10, 11, 10, 10, 11, 11, 9 and 11 — 102 items in columns of eight to twelve**; distinct prices inside a column run 2 to 40; ten distinct totals; **The collision check, and the method that actually works, and the figure this item first printed that could not have been produced.** **The ten totals were tested by their exact spelled strings — *one hundred and forty-seven*, *one hundred and twenty-eight*, *one hundred and fifty-nine*, *one hundred and seventy-three*, *one hundred and eighty-three*, *one hundred and eighty-seven*, *one hundred and ninety-nine*, *one hundred and seventy-eight*, *one hundred and sixty-four*, *one hundred and ninety-one* — against all five hundred and seventy prior chapters, and every prior occurrence of every one of the ten was read. Five of the ten have no prior occurrence at all. Five have one each, and all five are a speech-length certification in Volume 11 or a line count in Volume 02 and not one is a footed money column: 159 at `volume-11/chapter-0508.md`, 173 at `volume-11/chapter-0503.md`, 183 at `volume-11/chapter-0510.md`, 187 at `volume-11/chapter-0519.md`, and 191 at `volume-02/chapter-0071.md`. 0 collisions, and the 0 is now built on a test whose every hit was read.** **The parser-based figure this item first printed — a prior set of 131 distinct totals in 143 instances — is withdrawn, and item 1373 and thread 367 withdraw it independently and for the same reason: it came from a parser that was not hyphen-tolerant, so it was a blind set and a collision check against a blind set is a blind result.** A successor should use the spelled-string test and read the hits, and should not use the bare `is … pence` sweep at all, which returns the mark's own definition 154 times and cannot separate a column's total from a price in a sentence. Every mark conversion in all ten foots at forty-eight pence. **No column prints a barred numeral inside its own total and no item list contains the word *sixty* or any of the four barred numerals**, and both of those were checked on the item lists and not only on the totals. **The item lists were built without reference to any column on disk and were then checked directly against all 117 footed money columns in the five hundred and seventy prior chapters, and the result is stronger than a duplication figure: the longest run any one of the ten shares with any prior column is seven tokens, and every one of those seven-token runs is a run of consecutive prices or the *is one hundred and …* total formula, and measured on a run that must contain at least one real item-name word the answer is zero for all ten. No column in this block shares an item name in a shared run with any column on disk.**

1349. **This block prints no column ordinal, and that is a deliberate variation and not a missing figure.** Batch 0001 and the blocks behind it close each column with *it is the Nth one he has ever kept*, and a successor reading ten columns in this block with no ordinal in any of them is not looking at an omission: **a correct ordinal would have to be counted across four and a half years of chapter files and the honest repair inside a writing pass is not to assert a number that has not been counted.** The frame was kept and one clause was varied per chapter instead.

1350. **The duplication, on the basis named, and the three numbers a successor must not inherit — and the correction of the two this item first inherited, and the correction of the two that corrected it.** Raw token, headings stripped, emphasis markers removed, lowercased, an eight-word seed with maximal extension, and a file never compared with itself. **The first printing of this item recorded 10 and 10. A review raised them to 17 and 20. Both of those are also wrong, in the same direction, and the honest figures on the basis named here are 23 against the five hundred and seventy prior chapters and 20 inside the block, with 24 and 21 on the punctuation-stripped basis.** The twenty-three are *under it and not in it, and a question under a head is not an answer to it and does not go in*, at `chapter-0560.md` against `chapter-0565.md`; the twenty inside the block are *and none of them is a price and none of them goes in at either end of this column, and*, at `chapter-0565.md` against `chapter-0570.md`, and the twenty-one on the stripped basis are the cost-paragraph frame, *and the cost of that sitting is a column and it is the man of about forty-four's, and a*, at `chapter-0563.md` against `chapter-0570.md`. **The 17 and the 20 that item 1373 installed are real runs and are not the longest, and that is the defect this item was written to correct, recurring inside the item that corrected it: a run is a maximum only if nothing longer exists, and the pass that installed 17 and 20 quoted two examples and did not establish that nothing longer was there.** **All four runs are the volume's own frame and coda and not a lift: a question written under a head is not an answer to it, a thing that is not a figure of anybody, and a column's closing formula. None of them is a money column and none is a speech.** The instrument was re-run by two passes that wrote neither this block nor this item; the first reproduced Volume 12 Block 0001's recorded 28 words exactly on all three bases tried, and the second reproduced Block 0001's 28 and this item's 23 and 20, and the 23 was confirmed a second time with a longest-common-substring match on the same pair. The same pass re-extracting the money columns hyphen-tolerantly is what found the four collisions this volume's Batch 0003 shipped and did not catch, so the lesson is general and it is now written into `state/open-threads.md` at 367. **Volume 11 shipped 18 to 26 on the same frames with a maximum of 34, so 23 and 20 are inside the house band and not an alarm, and no prose in this block was reopened for the number.** Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, whitespace-normalised: 0 between the ten new chapters and the five hundred and seventy prior ones, and across all five hundred and eighty files the parallel-paragraph histogram is 39 at two and 1 at three, none of which touches this block.

1351. **The barred lists, the barred numerals and the self-reference sweep, all at 0, and the retired frame at 0 with the reason it is not a gate.** `failed` `seam` `roof` `coping` `Gatehouse` `custodian` `melody` `Culling` `eastern` `hemp` `charter` `brother` and `protagonist` `council` `hundredweight` `stage` `ledger` `qualification` `system` `panel` `mechanic` `attribute` `status` `level` are at **0 across the ten chapters**, and the barred numerals `thirty-six` `fifty-two` `sixty-one` `sixty-six` are at **0**, and `continuation` is at **0**, which is the third block running and the word is spent. **The twenty named self-reference patterns are at 0 — and they were not, and the two that were are the reason the family has to be swept by its bare noun.** **Chapter 0565 carried *the first time in this book that a road has answered instead of being described* and Chapter 0566 carried *there is no way in this book to say that a man was moved*, and both are revoiced with every fact kept: the first now says a road in nine counties has never answered instead of being described before this and this one has, and the second drops the four words and says there is no way to say that a man was moved, because nothing was moved and nothing came.** **The twenty-pattern prepositional sweep returned 0 on both of them, because *in this book* is not one of the twenty, and `this book` is a bare noun and the bare noun is what Volume 09 thread 15 already required be tested. The register's own trajectory is the standing reason this is a defect and not a preference: *this book* by volume is 25, 29, 32, 29, 7, 4, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0 across Volumes 01 to 11, and this block put two back after four volumes at zero. A successor sweeping register must test *this book*, *the book* and *this novel* and not only the prepositional forms, because the words in every one of them are ordinary English and this book uses all of them.** **The retired reporting frame, swept as `\w+ since (that|then)`, is at 0 in these ten chapters and 939 in the five hundred and fifty before Batch 0001, and its commonest lead words there are *said*, *out* and *saying*, so it is not a zero gate; eight instances did reach the page in this block's first drafting, every one of them *have said since that*, and all eight were revoiced with every fact kept and in four different idioms rather than by converting one form into another.** Two of the eight also removed this block's only 9-word overlap with a Volume 03 paragraph.

1352. **Punctuation, quotes and hygiene.** Question marks **13**, exclamation marks **0**, semicolons **0**, em dashes **0**, en dashes **0**, horizontal rules **0**. Curly quotation marks balance in every file and across the block at **154 and 154**, per file 23 / 10 / 6 / 17 / 13 / 19 / 9 / 17 / 26 / 14; straight quotation marks **0**; curly apostrophes **0** and straight apostrophes **52**; per-line bold parity even on every line of every file; space-before-punctuation **0**; double blank lines **0**; doubled words **0**; all ten files end in a newline. **No Volume 12 file uses an em dash and Volume 11's fifty chapters use nine between them, so the zero here is the house position and not an accident.**

1353. **`about`, with every basis named, because this file has been wrong about it twice.** **430 case-insensitive whole-word, 427 lower-case whole-word, 430 on a token equality, and 18,433 words**, so the rates are **23.33 per 1,000** and **23.17 per 1,000** on the first two. **There is no target and there is no band, and 23.33 sits inside the 22.85 to 27.30 that Volume 11's blocks ran and beside Batch 0001's 23.37.** `the whole of` **18**. The seven filler words at **11**: `held` 2, `steady` 0, `plain` 0, `counted` 3, `breathed` 0, `wrote` 6, `nodded` 0. **No figure of a person in this block stands four lines from another figure of a person without saying which is which, and the one place a definite total was formed out of two indefinite halves — the nine on the lane and the four near the bar — now says the two of them together are about thirteen and does not say thirteen. That sentence was FALSE when this item first printed it and is true now, and the false one was four lines from another: Chapter 0562's cost column sets aside *the nine who were on the lane and the four who were near the bar*, and four lines below it the chapter gave *about nine people in that valley heard him ring it and about four did not*, a different afternoon and a different pair of places, with nothing on the page saying they were not the same nine and four. The page now says so, and no figure moved.**

1354. **The name budget, with the arithmetic.** The budget for Volume 12 is five. **Batch 0001 spent two, `Merritt Gallow` and `Ilsa Wend`. This block spends none: 2 + 0 = 2 of 5, with three slots unspent**, and the block's four new persons who carry wants are all described by age and trade: a woman of about thirty-eight who keeps the block, a man of about forty-four who holds the second hoe, a man of about fifty-one who keeps the water at the top of the Long Set, a woman of about fifty-eight who turns the drying cloths, a woman of about sixty-four who ties the picking cords, a woman of about fifty-five who keeps the Low Gate, a man of about sixty and a man of about thirty-one of the Standing, a carter of about forty and a carter of about nineteen, a boy of about fifteen and a boy of about nineteen, and a woman of about seventy who said one thing about a leaf. **A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns `Long Set` 14, `Aren Kest` 12, `Wet Set` 3, `Low Gate` 2, `High Set` 2, `Middle Way` 1, `High Row` 1, `Low Set` 1, `The Low` 1, `The Long` 1, `The Crown` 1 and `And Aren` 1, and every one of the twelve was read before the sweep was reported.** Nine are places or the words of a place, two are sentence-initial false positives, and `The Long` is a fragment of *the Long Set*. **`Aren Kest` on a direct count is 13 in eight of the ten chapters, and the sweep's 12 is one short because the instance at `chapter-0562.md` in `**And Aren Kest stood**` is consumed by the sentence-initial `And Aren` false positive; the direct count is the one to use, as at item 1338 of Batch 0001's record. The first printing of this item said *nine of the ten chapters* and that was wrong by one: he is not named at all in `chapter-0563.md` or `chapter-0565.md`, which are the two chapters in which he is in the room and says nothing, and the direct count of files containing the name is 8.**

1355. **The five places this block named in the Orchard, and they are places and not people and they cost nothing.** **The Low Gate**, a bar of wood lying in the grass at the foot of a lane about a day's walk above the end of a made road, kept by a woman of about fifty-five with a board carrying four questions. **The Low Lane**, the lane the gate is at. **The Middle Way** and **the High Row**, the two names above it on the slope. **The Wet Set** is carried from Batch 0001 and is in this block's sweep. **A successor should know that these four are new and that they are not in `outline/volume-12.md` §5.4, which lists the High Set, the Middle Set, the Long Set, the Wet Set, the cut, the settlement at the middle terrace and the press-house, and the outline was not edited by this phase.**

1356. **The geography the block fixes, and the two figures a successor may not convert.** The made road out of the market town ends at a cross of two stones and a heap of broken metalling, and the market town is **about four days of carting** from that end of it, which is the same measure as the eleven who came four days and is not the same measure as the nine days a woman walked. **The Low Gate is about a day's walk above the end of the made road.** The valley's own measure — **about four days and about nine of hard going from the nearest made road** — is quoted as the valley's measure and **no figure is printed anywhere in this block for the distance between the gate and the settlement at the middle terrace, deliberately, because the two published figures do not foot into one and a third number would be a new invention.** Going out through the gate is free and coming in is not, and the man of about thirty-one in Chapter 0569 walked the whole way out on that fact and was right.

1357. **What the crossing is on the page, and what it is not.** **A bar of wood lying in grass, a woman with a board, four questions asked in order, and a person answering.** **It is not a door and there is no building over it and no lock and no keeper of a lock, and it is not a bell: the press-house bell is rung at four points of a sitting by a boy of about fifteen who is paid a penny for the four of them and who has said he rang it because the hour was the hour and not because of anything.** The questions are the four that went under a fourth head on nine leaves in a room two hundred miles off in Thawmonth; **the chapter says the two wordings are not the same words and are one question and does not add them, and the question itself is not reprinted a third time.** A man who cannot answer the fourth does not come in: a carter of about forty went out in the swell sitting with two sacks of pears and has not come back in, and the fruit of that day is on no road in nine counties. **Nothing in the block explains why anybody can cross, and no chapter looks at the roadmark, names the works under the abbey, or says what an anchor is.**

1358. **The rule that governs the block and the three people it cost.** **A man who can see what is wrong with the channel may say so out loud to one other person and may not say it to a second, and the reason is that the reason the channel wants mending is in a record that is not allowed to be read.** He says the fault out loud once, in Chapter 0563, to nobody in particular, and a man of about fifty-one hears it and never repeats it. He walks four miles of the cut in Chapter 0567 and says one fact and no fault, and the man of about fifty-one says out loud that a man who says nothing is not the same as a channel that is sound and wants the two written in different places. **And in Chapter 0569 a man of about thirty-one walks out of the valley to ask the only person in nine counties who could certify the cut, and is refused, and the cost of the refusal lands in Chapter 0570 on a block of ground on the second terrace, which is thinned wrong for a season by a man who has not been taught that block and who says he does not mind and wants it said that he said so.**

1359. **The nine things Batch 0003 inherits, and the one of them that is a trap.** The three sittings this block sits in are the swell and the pick, and a block that runs two sittings may not convert a sitting into a month. **The woman of about sixty-four's count has not been touched and nothing in this block explains it: she counts about sixty stones round the oldest tree in the Long Set at the eighth hour, the number has not been a different number in thirty years, she has been asked twice in thirty years and has never said, and Chapter 0568 is built on a man standing beside her and not asking.** The putting-away is now on the page three times and not explained: a strip of ground at the foot of the Long Set asked to be let go, about ninety people went out on it, the woman of about thirty-eight was about sixteen and said yes and did not go, and the record of it is at a place on that slope where a thing is not said out loud, and Chapter 0568 puts a man beside the oldest tree and a woman of about seventy says one thing about a leaf. **The rule that a person who holds a block of ground cannot be the answer for the ground is new to this block and is a local custom of that valley said out loud by a man of about sixty and in the mouth of a character, and it is not a law of nine counties and no office in nine counties has heard of it.** The trap it sets is the volume's: the answer to a road is a person and the price of being made one is the ground, and the woman has not given it up, and nobody has told her she may.

1360. **What did not move, and it is the standing condition of the volume.** The Crown's form's fourth head is empty and is now about six months empty and no office and no person has filled it and nobody in this block can. **The half leaf is face down on a table in the room over a saddler's and no chapter in this block turned it over, scheduled anybody turning it over, said what is on the other side of it, or printed how many days it has been there**; Chapter 0570 says only that it is still face down. The floor of that room was washed on the twenty-fourth of Greenmonth and Chapter 0570 says it was washed before he came up here and does not print an interval for it, and nobody in nine counties knows who washed it and nobody was thanked. The five documents are in five places and none of them is the Crown's form, and Chapter 0570 says so on the page. Nobody is thanked in any of the ten chapters and no summary this pass wrote thanks anybody. The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between and no chapter of this block accounts for his silence. The breach between the two men is open and neither of them is in this block. The shut road four hundred miles off is shut. The twenty-second notch is not cut. The three series of the room over the weigh-house are three series. The antagonist is not named and no chapter said whose plan the letter is. A System panel is printed nowhere in this block. **The protagonist conducted nothing, got nothing back, overruled nobody, answered no question about a form, was the reason no thing worked, and is thanked by nobody; he is a person a market town of about two thousand can find, and he is four days and about nine above a bar of wood lying in the grass, and nobody in nine counties has been told.**

## Volume 12, Batch 0003 — writing pass and records, items 1361 to 1372

**The highest number in this file before this section was 1360. These are the items the midpoint block of Volume 12 created and they are all on the page in Chapters 571 to 580, each of them checked against the chapter it describes and not against another figure.**

1361. **The putting-away is read, and what is read is the argument and the remedy and a mark nobody can place.** A plank of green wood about two hands long with the marks cut into it and not written on it lay under a flat stone at the foot of the oldest tree in the Long Set for twenty-two winters. It says: a strip of ground at the foot of the Long Set asked to be let go; about ninety people went out on it; four sets of them argued about it for about nine days; they were right that it would have come to a quarrel that none of them could have stopped; and the remedy was that no argument about a set would ever be written down again. **The woman of about thirty-eight can read the first half and the man of about fifty-one can read the second, neither will read the other's, and they read it out between them about four times before they stop.** The plank is on the grass in the light a hundred yards up the slope, flat side up, and the man of about fifty-one said out loud that it is not going back in the ground, and the hole is filled in with its own loam and the flat stone is on top of the loam and not on top of the plank.

1362. **The oath and the burial are the same act, and the man of about sixty of the Standing has said so out loud, and nobody answered him.** *Then there has been an argument about a set written down in this valley for twenty-two winters, and it has been under a tree, and there are about nine people standing round a hole looking at it.* **The volume does not choose between the fact that a record kept in the ground is a record nobody can be shown and the fact that this is also the whole of the difficulty, and neither does this block, and no chapter of this block makes anybody angry.** The man of about thirty-one of the Standing said the same thing in his own terms nineteen years ago and it was never said in one breath with the other half until this block.
