1363. **A second set of marks on the same plank, in another hand, with no reason attached: water, out at the foot of a wall and in again at the head of it, on the day the strip was let go.** The man of about fifty-one read it out loud and said that he is not going to say the two things were done together unless somebody puts it in front of him, and nobody did. **This is the reason the fault at the fourth hundred pace of the cut has wanted for about nine years, and it is now on a piece of wood in daylight in the valley that made it, and it is a mark and not a list and no office in nine counties has it.**

1364. **The woman of about sixty-four's count is a copy and has been for thirty years, and the copy's source is now on the page.** The number is cut into a slate about the length of a hand that lives in her apron; the woman of about seventy told it to her in her first year and told her to say that number and let nobody count for themselves; the same number is cut into the top of the plank, put in the ground twenty-two winters ago by the same woman of about seventy, who said at the time that she was given it and was copying it. **She looked at the arc once, at the eighth hour of the sitting before the dig, and it is not the number, and she would not say what either number is, and it cannot be given back.** She has never counted anything in thirty years, she is paid in fruit and not in money, she ties about a hundred and forty of about nine hundred cords in a sitting, she is not a keeper of anything, and nobody has ever asked her what she is counting.

1365. **The second asking of the woman of about thirty-eight was not about her, and it is the thing that got the ground dug.** At the eighth hour of the bar sitting, in the open, with about eleven people on the ground instead of four stools in a room: *is there anybody in this valley who will let a person dig at the foot of the oldest tree in the Long Set?* **She refused to have it written down, because a written question is a question to an office and there is no office in this valley, and she said she would rather be refused in the open than permitted in a room and would not say it twice.** Nobody has told her she may and nobody has told her she may not, and the leaf with the four lines is still on the board with the fourth ruled and empty and the word **noted** in the margin in a different ink.

1366. **The rule the man of about sixty made, and he said out loud before anybody had to find out that it is not a good one.** Nobody who holds a block of ground inside nine yards of that tree will dig it; every one of the four holders is asked; a holder who says she would rather not does not stop it and does not have to give a reason and will be believed about anything else she says for the rest of her life. **The man of about thirty-one said that makes honesty in this valley cost a woman something and that the man of about sixty made it in about four minutes, and the man of about sixty agreed.** All four holders said she would rather not, it made no difference, he wrote nothing down, and nobody has asked any of the four what she would have said if it had been asked a different way.

1367. **A finding about a road in this valley has to be made by somebody who can be moved off the ground here, and that is why the man from outside may not make it.** Three reasons are on the page and the third is the one the man of about fifty-one would give if he had only one: if a man from off the hill holds the pen then in about nine years there will be a paper with a name on it and this valley will be a place with a name on it; a man who finds a fault and gets it acted on gets put in charge of it inside about four days; and the man of about fifty-one saw a hand do something on the stone of that lip in the swell sitting and has never said one word about it, and if a man from off this hill puts a straight edge down then every finding this valley ever makes about its own water has a stranger underneath it. **Aren Kest said no out loud, said it was not modesty and not the rule, said he wanted the job so much he could not account for how much, and nobody thanked him for saying no and nobody said out loud that he should have done it.**

1368. **The straight edge went across the lip of the fourth hundred pace at about the sixth hour and it is not a mend and not a finding.** The man of about fifty-one held the lip, the woman of about thirty-eight held the far end, and the boy of about fifteen held the chalk, and about four people stood and looked and the man of about thirty-one came down and looked. **A line of chalk is not a finding until three people have written it down and none of the three has written anything yet.** Nobody in that valley has had a mend anybody can name in about nine years and this was not one. Aren Kest carried nine barrows of spoil down to the Wet Set and emptied them, and the man of about fifty-one did not watch him, did not thank him and did not tell him he had done it well.

1369. **The word the main world uses for that valley is on the page and it is the main world's.** A carter of about thirty said it at the Low Gate in answer to nothing, and the woman of about thirty-eight said it once at the head of the Long Set at the ninth hour with about eleven people within about nine paces and has not said it since. **It is in a report of about four hands of writing that went out of this valley in the year the strip was let go with a party and did not come back, and no one in nine counties can produce it, and the man of about thirty-one says his father's father was on the Standing in that year and never spoke of it to his father and never to him.** A woman of about seventy said she has a name for the place, that it is not that, that it is not a word anybody from outside can use, and did not give it. **No chapter of this block says what the valley calls itself and no chapter may.**

1370. **The sentence is on the page, said once, on a made road with nobody else on it, by a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room over a weigh-house with a door on a lane two hundred miles off.** *A beautiful place is not a free place.* Her own terms: that valley is the most beautiful ground she has stood on in about eleven years and it is beautiful for the reason it is not free, and the peace is the holding and the holding is the beauty and they come apart the moment you ask a person what they think, and the result is that about nine households of the low ground find out that the thing they were peaceable about was a holding, and they did not have a vote in it and are not the ones who were wrong. **She said in advance that she would not write it down for him, and gave him no paper and no method and no name, and neither of them said one word about the four years and a half, and there was no room on either side of that road with a third person standing in it, and nothing was repaired on a milestone and nothing was asked to be.**

1371. **The main-world cost of the block is a place in a room, and the room now opens on market mornings.** From the first of a month the hired room over a saddler's opens at the second hour on market mornings and shuts at the seventh, and because the door has to be locked from the lane while there are people on the stair, the key is in the lane and the woman of about thirty-four who keeps it is in the lane. **For the first hour since the room began she has not been in it on a sitting day, and she said it was a place in a room and not a punishment, because a punishment would mean somebody did it to her.** The man of about fifty-eight said out loud before he did it that it is his and not hers. The copyist of about twenty-six said again that she is not going to write the fourth head twice, because once is a copy and twice is a habit. The hearing stands and there is no bench in the room. **The half leaf is face down upstairs, nobody turned it over, nobody has been booked to turn it over, and nobody has said out loud how long it has been lying there, which is the one figure the object does not give up.** Nobody in nine counties knows who washed the floor on a Sunday in Greenmonth and nobody was thanked, and no chapter of this block prints an interval for it.

1372. **What the block opened on purpose for Batch 0004, and what did not move.** **The water question is on the table in about nine households' worth of words and was not begun: four sets of people have to find the money between them, they have not been asked, two of them have not heard of it, and about nine households on the four lowest terraces are the people who will pay for it and are not the people who were wrong.** A question asked in the open at the ninth hour of Chapter 0580 was answered by nobody and nobody has been scheduled to answer it. A woman of about thirty-eight has said out loud twice that she would like to be told she was wrong and nobody has done it for her. **The fourth head of the Crown's form is ruled and empty and about six months empty. The finding of Chapter 0558 is not softened, not recited and not answered out loud. The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between. The breach between the two men is open and neither of them is in this block and the barred kin-term is at two across five hundred and eighty chapters and is spent. The shut road four hundred miles off is shut, nobody has been sent to that coast, the twenty-second notch is not cut, about eleven hundred people are on nothing, the three series of the room over a weigh-house are three series, the city is on a shelf with its three protected numerals at zero, and the antagonist is not named and no chapter said whose plan the letter is. A System panel is printed nowhere in this block and *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are at zero in it, reported at block scope and at volume scope and never at manuscript scope. The man from outside is four days and about nine above a bar of wood lying in the grass and nobody in nine counties has been told. Nobody is thanked, in any chapter, in any speech, and in none of the five state files this pass wrote.**

1376. **The unattributed *she* in Chapter 0570, and why it is the same defect as a bad figure wearing different clothes.** Chapter 0570 turns on the order of four things on a board, and in the paragraph that establishes that order two named women stand about forty words apart: the woman of about forty-four, who keeps the board and has ruled the four divisions, and the woman of about thirty-one, who read the leaf's order out of her own head against the board and did not put it right. **The speech that follows — *Ours is a person first …* — carried a bare *she* with those two women as its only antecedents, and the next speech is attributed to the woman of about forty-four, so a reader had a one-in-two choice on the sentence the whole chapter is built on.** The page now names the speaker before the speech, the woman of about forty-four, who keeps that board, which is the reading the block's own state section records, and no figure and no fact moved. **The general rule this establishes: a bare pronoun is a figure of a person standing four lines from another figure of a person, and a reader has no instrument for it.**

1378. **Two instruments failed in this pass and one record was right and would have been made wrong, and this is the third time in four blocks that a re-run instrument has been more confident than the number it was checking.** **The duplication figure this item gave as correct is not correct, and the correction is at 1350: the honest figures are 23 and 20, not the 17 and 20 this item endorsed. Two passes hit the seed-length failure here — a `zip`-based gram set that cannot extend a run past its own eight words, returned 8 and 8, and a full scan that timed out — and a third, a substring search over a concatenated corpus, returned 12 and 12 and was also wrong. Only a pass that indexes the eight-grams once, groups the occurrences by file, and extends left and right on the pairs that share a gram returned a number, and it returned 23 and 20 on the retained basis and 24 and 21 stripped; the 23 was then confirmed a second time by a longest-common-substring match on the same pair, which is the cheap check and the one to run first.** **The method that works, in the order to try it: longest common substring between the two candidate files, which is exact and takes no time; then the index-extend method for the whole corpus; never a gram set whose length is its own seed, and never a substring search over a concatenated corpus.** The money instrument failed the other way: a first pass reported all ten columns colliding, which was a regex whose capture group swallowed the mark's own definition, and a second pass dropped the hundreds digit and reported nine collisions, and a third mis-anchored on the total statement and reported totals that are not on the page. **The collision conclusion was reached instead by testing the ten spelled totals as strings against the whole prior manuscript and reading all five hits, which is a method whose every output is a thing a person can look at.** A fourth failure belongs beside the other three and is the one a successor is most likely to walk into: the item-list comparison in this file and at thread 372 was re-run here and the ten columns in this block share **no** item name at the same price in the same order with any of the one hundred and ten footed columns on disk, and the longest adjacent run of identical item names at any price is two, which is ordinary shop vocabulary and not a clone. **The standing instruction, which belongs beside item 367 and thread 367: a measurement and the instrument that produced it are one claim, and an instrument that returns exactly its own seed length has measured nothing and is not a small result — it is no result.**

**This section is appended under the three rules at the top of this file: items are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, and a volume is not archived while it is being written.** Items 1 to 1381 stand, are not renumbered, and 1381 is the last item of the second pass over Batch 0002. **The block was not rewritten. It was already finished prose on disk when this pass was dispatched against its identical prompt, and the reason is the dispatch hazard at item 1381 and at `state/open-threads.md` 374.**

1382. **Superseded in part by item 1392, which is the current state of the markers; the instruction stands.** **The dispatch, and what a writer handed this batch is required to do before drafting one word.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/` carries a `PROMPT.md` and **no marker of any kind**, while `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0571.md` to `chapter-0580.md` are finished prose with their summaries, their chapter summaries and their continuity items all in place. `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/` now carries a `.done`, so Batch 0003 was the next unsatisfied prompt and this pass was handed a finished block. **The instruction a successor needs is one line and it is the same line this project has needed for nine batches: count the chapter files and read the forward prompt before drafting anything. A prompt directory's marker is the only thing that tells a dispatcher a batch is done, and markers are controller-owned and this project writes none.**

1383. **Superseded in part by item 1390, which corrects the direction of the error; the finding and the three sentences stand.** **The finding, and it is one fact described three different ways, and the wrong way is a calendar.** `chapter-0567.md` is dated the **third hour of the pick sitting**. It is the chapter in which the man of about fifty-one walks four miles of stone with the man from off the hill, in which a fault is found at the fourth hundred pace, and in which at about the seventh hour he comes back down the high side, asks twice whether there is a fault, is told no twice, and says on the page that both answers were true and both were about a different thing. **Three sentences in `chapter-0573.md` and `chapter-0579.md` put that day in the swell sitting: the man of about fifty-one's telling of four households, his recollection of the lip, and Aren Kest's wanting the job since about the fourth hour.** All three are the pick sitting. **The five sittings run bar, blossom, set, swell, pick, and a year is a turn of the five, so the swell sitting stands earlier in the year than the pick sitting: as printed, the valley remembered an event before it had happened, and a block whose subject is a record that was kept for twenty-two winters and could not be shown had a character misremembering a day in front of about nine people.** The block's own rule is that no character in that valley converts one sitting into another, and the defect was a conversion made by the writer.

1384. **The second finding is in the same sentence and is not a sitting, and its repair improves the scene.** `chapter-0579.md` had the man of about fifty-one say that of a hand laid flat on the lip he was there and he saw it. **He was not there. `chapter-0567.md` puts him at a terrace from about the two-mile mark until the seventh hour, puts the hand on the lip at about the fifth hour, and says in terms that the man from off the hill did not tell him when he came back down.** The claim is now the four hours he actually watched, the twice-refused question, and the fact that both answers were true and about a different thing. **The argument against Aren Kest is stronger on the new facts than on the old, because the new facts are about a man who found a fault and held it and refused twice and would not say why, and the old facts were about a miracle nobody witnessed.** **Consequence a successor must not reverse: nobody in Block 0003 witnesses the left palm. The block's rule is that a chapter may have a person notice it and may not have a person hear him explain it, and after this repair the block has the second and not the first, which is permitted and is not required, and the palm thread is therefore untouched by the block, as `chapter-0577.md` leaves it with nobody within nine paces of a gatepost.**

1385. **The third finding is nine years, walked against looked, and the repair keeps every figure.** `chapter-0573.md` had the man of about fifty-one say he knew the fault because he had looked at four miles of stone for nine years. **`chapter-0567.md`, in his own mouth, says he has not walked four miles of anything in about nine years.** He now says he has had the water at the top of that Long Set for nine years and knows what is under the fourth hundred pace without having walked four miles of anything in about nine years. **The lesson in the general form: a book that keeps a man's tenure as a figure will have two sentences about what he did in that tenure, and the second one has to be checked against the first, and the instrument that catches it is a person reading the two chapters side by side.**

1386. **The fourth finding is one word and no sweep in this repository can see it.** `chapter-0578.md` printed *the volume of it does not make anybody angry.* **The phrase means quantity and the manuscript also has thirteen volumes, and the twenty self-reference patterns sweep the bare prepositional phrases and not the bare noun.** It is now *the amount of it*. **This is the second time in three batches that a repair or a revoice has put a word in that collided with the project's own structural vocabulary, and the first was a `roof` at `chapter-0576.md`, which is on the barred list and was caught only because the barred list is run after the repairs and not before them. The standing instruction is unchanged and is now two instances deep: a register rule cannot be enforced by a token count, because every word in it is a word this book uses.**

1387. **Superseded in part by items 1390 and 1391, which correct one explanation and one basis in this item; the measurements stand as the third pass took them and are recounted at 1389 and in `state/open-threads.md` 382.** **What the third pass verified and changed nothing in, every figure counted on the ten files after its last prose edit.** Calendar **0 / 0 / 0** on the day-of-month half, the weekday half and the market-morning claim, each half derived from the day number off the single anchor of day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, and never from the other half: 1722 is 792 mod 7 and a Monday, 1723 is 793 and a Tuesday, 1746 is 816 and a Thursday, 1750 is 820 and a Monday and not a market day, 1758 is 828 and a Tuesday. **Chapter 0576 prints the first of Harvestmonth and a Thursday and a market day and is day 1746; Chapter 0577 prints the fifth of Harvestmonth and a Monday and not a market day and is day 1750. ## The review repair over Volume 12 Batch 0003 — items 1388 to 1392, and the highest number in this file is now 1392

**This section is appended under the three rules at the top of this file: items are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, and a volume is not archived while it is being written.** Items 1 to 1387 stand and are not renumbered. **The reviewed artifact was a repair pass and not a draft, which is the fourth review of a repair in this project and the second of one in this volume. The block was not rewritten, no chapter was restarted, no plot beat, dateline, weekday, money total, name or ending moved, and the one prose edit this pass made was inside a sentence a previous pass had added.** The review's findings and their dispositions are in `reviews/volume-12/batch-0003.md`, which is the durable record; this section is the canon and the figures.

1388. **The review, its eight findings, what the repair found while checking them, and which are claims rather than counts.** A reviewer read the third pass's diff, all ten chapters and the five state files that pass wrote, and returned eight findings, of which **one was a prose defect, one was an instrument defect, and six were claims or records** — a prompt that is about to be fired asserting that its own ten chapters are unwritten, a change that was in the diff and in none of the records, a character disclosure with no continuity item, a rationale that described its own defect backwards, four chapter files quietly costing a concatenated count four words, a summary whose inserted word changed a referent together with a welded pair of speeches in the same chapter, no review record in `reviews/` at all, and a controller-owned ledger that has been reading `phase-000-bootstrap` since the first chapter. **The repair then found two more while checking the items the review had already checked, and both are the same standing class: the `about` count in these files was printed on a numerator and a denominator taken on two different bases, so the pair did not reproduce on the basis the file names, which is the class `reviews/volume-10/batch-0001.md` records, where a rate was certified on one basis and carried on another and three files quoted a figure that was neither; and the rolling-window index in `state/current.md` labelled two runs of items as Volume 12's that are Volume 11's. Both are at 1391.** **The finding worth the most is the first, and it is not about the prose**, and that is 1392. **The finding that cost least to apply and would have cost most to leave is the third**, and that is 1389. **The general form of the eight, and it is the form `reviews/README.md` states for this project as a whole: no review has found a plot defect in the delivered prose. Six of these eight were a sentence in a state file that was wrong about the book, and in five of the six the prose was right and the sentence about the prose was not.**

1389. **The Chapter 0574 disclosure, which the third pass made and no record of it accounted for, and which is now canon.** `chapter-0574.md`, at the eighth hour of the blossom sitting, has the woman of about sixty-four tell the woman of about thirty-eight: **a man from off the hill stood where you are standing in the pick sitting, and I told him in my own mouth that I count, and I did not find out until this morning that there was anything else in it.** Three things about it are load-bearing for a successor. **One, it is grounded and it is not an invention: `chapter-0568.md`, at the eighth hour of the pick sitting, has her say *I know when my day is finished because I count*, and the two words *I count* are the words the disclosure is about, and the third pass put them in her mouth here having put them in her mouth there.** **Two, the woman of about thirty-eight is nowhere in `chapter-0568.md`; the only figure of thirty-eight in that chapter is a price, thirty-eight pence, in its cost column, so she was not in the arc at the pick sitting and can be told about it at the blossom sitting and could not have been told it earlier by anybody in that scene.** **Three, it is a change in what a habit means and not a fact added: for thirty years the woman of about sixty-four has said *I count* because it is what she says about what she does, and she has now learned that there is something else inside the sentence, and she learned it from being asked what she was counting rather than being asked to count.** A successor may not turn it into a kept thing, a secret, a mystery or an object, may not say what the number is a number of, may not have her look at the stones with her eye on them, may not have her tell a third person, and may not have the man of about sixty or the boy of about fifteen hear it. **The speech now has its own paragraph and its own attribution, which it did not: the third pass had appended it to the end of the speech before it inside one line, which passes a bold-parity gate at three pairs and reads as a patch, and the fix is the book's own form for a second turn.**

1390. **Item 1383 is superseded in part, and the part that was wrong was the direction of the error, which is the part a successor would have repeated.** 1383 says that the swell sitting is earlier in the year than the pick sitting and that *as printed the valley remembered an event before it happened*, and it cites the block's rule that no character in that valley converts one sitting into another. **The first half is right and the second is wrong, and they cannot both be.** The five sittings run bar, blossom, set, swell, pick and a year is a turn of the five, so a reference from the swell sitting to a day dated in the pick sitting is a reference to an **earlier** sitting of the same turn, which is a mis-dating and not an impossibility; nothing in the printed text remembered anything before it had happened, and no reader could have seen a memory fail. **What the three sentences actually did was send one day, dated in `chapter-0567.md` at the third hour of the pick sitting, back to a different sitting, and the three speakers were consistent with one another and all three were wrong against Chapter 0567, so the block's rule was not violated by a character at all: the violation was the writer's, and what it broke is the rule at the top of this file, that an item number means the same thing in the live window and in the archive, and the standing one at `AGENTS.md`, *do not silently invent a fact that changes the world's rules*, which is what re-dating a day the page has already dated is when no one notices.** The repair was right and is unchanged; the sentence that described why is corrected in `state/current.md` and in `state/batch-summaries.md`, and this item is the record. **The general form, which is the finding: when a state file explains a repair, the explanation is itself a claim about the book, and it is the one claim in a repair that no instrument checks and no reader of the chapters will ever contradict. It is the cheapest thing in this project to get wrong and the most expensive, because the explanation is what a successor quotes when it meets the same fact again.**

1391. **The recount instrument, four files that were costing a concatenated count four words, a numerator taken on a different basis from its denominator, and an index that pointed two runs of items at the wrong volume.** Three findings, all found by checking what the review had already checked rather than by reading the chapters, and all three are claims in state files rather than defects in the book. **One, four chapter files in closed volumes carried no trailing newline: `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0271.md`, `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0275.md`, `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0497.md` and `chapters/volume-10/chapter-0500.md`. Per-file counting, which is the basis every state file in this repository states, was and is unaffected at 1,644,389 before this section and at 1,644,395 after the Chapter 0574 edit; a concatenated `cat` of the five hundred and eighty files returned 1,644,385, four short, because the last token of each of the four files was glued to the first token of the next. All four files now end in a newline and the two bases agree. No prose was touched in any of the four and no per-file word count moved.** **The hazard was not the four words; it was that this project's standing rule is that a figure which does not reproduce is the error, so the next pass would have been licensed to spend itself reconciling a four-word discrepancy in four files nobody had touched, and the more likely outcome is that it would have moved a figure in five state files and a prompt instead.** **Two, the `about` count printed in five files as 494 case-insensitive and 491 lower-case at a stated basis of *a case-insensitive whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator* does not reproduce on that basis. The numerator was taken by splitting on the space alone, which glues a line-opening `about` onto the token that ended the line before it. On the basis these files name, the figures are 500 and 496 in 18,240 words, 27.41 and 27.19 per 1,000, and the space-split count is 495. The honest pair is now printed everywhere with the method named, and the 494 and 491 are recorded here as first printings rather than quietly replaced.** This is the class `reviews/volume-10/batch-0001.md` already records, where a rate was certified on one basis and carried on another and three files quoted a figure that was neither, and the form of it is always the same: the number is not wrong, the sentence describing it is, and a successor who re-derives the number on the stated basis gets a different number and concludes that the file is broken rather than that the sentence is. **Three, `state/current.md`'s own rolling-window index, the paragraph that tells a writer which run of items is which, labelled 1318 to 1327 and 319 to 327 as Volume 12's verification pass, and the section headers those numbers point at are both headed Volume 11. It is corrected in place and the correction is named in the file. The class is the one this whole section is about and it needs no instrument at all to find: a sentence in a state file that is wrong about the book, in a repository where the book itself is on disk to be read.**

1392. **The dispatch hazard is now inside the prompt that is about to be fired, and the one thing this pass did not do is named here so that nobody looks for it.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0002/` now carries a `.done`, written by the controller and not by this project, **so Batch 0003 is the first unsatisfied prompt in the repository, and Batch 0003's ten chapters are finished prose, and Batch 0004 carries no marker either, and 51 of the 53 batch directories under `workspace/` carry a `.done` or a `.retired` beside their prompt.** The mitigation until now was a warning in files a writer is supposed to read and in a prompt that did not tell a writer to read them, **and the prompt in fact said the opposite: its own first line read *This run writes Chapters 571 to 580 and nothing else* and acknowledged only that 551 to 570 were finished prose on disk.** `workspace/volume-12/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` now opens with five paragraphs that say the chapters are written, name the file range and the per-chapter figures, name the missing marker and the census that makes it an omission rather than a convention, tell the writer that the first two moves are to count the files and to read the Batch 0004 prompt, forbid writing 551 to 600 and forbid creating a second prompt for a batch that has one, and state what the run is for instead, which is to verify and repair in place and to look for one fact described two different ways across chapters, since no instrument in this repository can find that and a person reading three chapters against a fourth found it in a day. **The brief below that warning is the brief that wrote the block and stands unchanged, so that a successor can verify the block against the thing it was written from.** **What this pass did not do: it wrote no `.done` and no `.retired` and no other marker, it did not edit `state/phase-ledger.json`, and it created no prompt for any batch. The ledger reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` with `status: planned`, `attempts: 0`, `range: null` and `actualModel: null` against five hundred and eighty chapters and three hundred and seven commits carrying the `novel:` prefix, and phase selection, retry and timeout read that file; it is controller-owned, it was flagged to the workflow owner by the review, and it is recorded here and in `state/open-threads.md` 383 rather than written.**

---

## Volume 12 Batch 0004 — items 1393 to 1402, and the highest number in this file is now 1402

**This section is appended under the three rules at the top of this file: items are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, and a volume is not archived while it is being written.** Items 1 to 1392 stand and are not renumbered. **This was a writing pass and not a review: the block did not exist when the run began, `chapters/volume-12/` held 551 to 580 and no more, and the ten files were drafted from `workspace/volume-12/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, then measured, then repaired where the measurements found defects.** Every figure below was counted on the ten chapter files after the last prose edit in this pass.

1393. **A finding about a road in this valley is three people, on a slate, in chalk, and it is the first one anybody here has made about their own water in about nine years.** The three are **a man of about fifty-one who keeps the water at the top of the Long Set, a woman of about thirty-eight who keeps a block between two head stones, and a boy of about fifteen who rings the bell**, and the third being the boy is not a detail: at the sixth hour of the set sitting he was the only other person on the lip at the fourth hundred pace, and four names would need four people who saw it and the only fourth saw it afterwards. **The boy is paid a penny for the bell and is paid nothing for the finding, was told the bell is a penny and this is nothing, and asked for it to be written that he said he would do it for nothing before he did it and not after, and that was written down, by the woman, who had not been asked to write anything.** The valley has had no foolscap for about twenty-two years and the woman's house has had no ink in four years, so the surface is a slate and the mark is a stick of chalk, and the chapter says on the page that it is a bad surface and the only one three people can be found on. The order is the valley's own: a person, then where it is, then what it is, then what goes on it. **Nobody put a name at the top of it**, because a name at the top is a thing somebody can be pointed at, and the boy said that a slate with three names under a thing is also a thing somebody can be pointed at, and nobody had an answer, and he has not asked again. A successor may not fill a fourth head with the finding, may not turn the slate into a form, may not say the finding was carried to anybody, and may not have the man of about thirty-one of the Standing carry it to a market town.

1394. **What is under the lip at the fourth hundred pace is now on the page in three hands, and the reason is on the page with it, and this is the first time either has been written down.** The lip on the high side carries **for about nine yards on a bed that has gone, and under the lip is a line of rubble about two hands thick**; and in the fourth line, in the same three hands and in chalk, is what the plank's second set of marks says: the water was let out at the foot of a wall and put back in at the head of it, on the day the strip of ground at the foot of that slope was let go, and about ninety people went out on it and four sets of them argued about it for nine days. **A successor may not add a fifth hand, may not convert the rubble into a cause, and may not say the man of about fifty-one has said the two things were done together, which he has still not said and which nobody has put in front of him.** A slate with three names on it is a finding and **not** a document an office can hold, and the seven objects are the Crown's form with its empty fourth head, the foolscap sheet with four ruled lines and a man's name in the fourth, the returned form from a county two hundred miles off with a woman's word in its fourth head, the piece of stone a foot and a half long in a room in another county, the leaf about nine inches by six with a scar in a tree and nothing under it, the leaf of foolscap with four lines a man of thirty-two wrote for himself and has never taken out, the board off the middle terrace with four divisions ruled and three filled, **and the plank**, and no two of them are near each other in this block and a successor that puts two near each other must say on the page that they are five documents in five places and none of them is the Crown's form.

1395. **The woman of about thirty-eight has said the thing she asked for twice, and she said it to a man who is not the answer for a road.** She had said out loud twice that she would like to be told she was wrong, in the blossom sitting and at the ninth hour of the set sitting, and the man of about fifty-one said in Block 0003 that he does not think she was wrong and is not going to tell her that she was not. **In this block she asks for nothing, and at the ninth hour of the pick sitting she tells a man of thirty-two with a limp that he was wrong about his own refusal, in the oldest voice in that valley, which is not her own and which she hears arrive, **and the page counts nothing and certifies nothing, because the speech is printed in six quoted turns and the review found the printed *nine sentences* beside it a speech-length figure that its own speech did not carry, and the repair removed the count and left the voice.**** This is the thing the block's ninth chapter is for and it may not be spent twice: it is the first time in the volume that the sentence *I would like to be told I was wrong* has been answered, and it is answered to the wrong person, and the woman of about thirty-eight notices that. She also uses the oldest voice deliberately inside the chapter's first half, when she agrees to sign, and says so out loud, and asks that the three of them be clear she heard it and is signing regardless. **A successor may not have her use that voice a third time in this volume, and may not have it be the vehicle of anything she chose.** The three-nineness of the thing she says to him is a speech-length figure and is not certified anywhere and no successor may certify one.

1396. **The four sets were asked one question, printed in full on the slate and not counted on the page, and answered in the order yes, no, yes, no, and the four sets are not the four terraces and are not the four names of the sittings.** The question is **what is your share of four miles**, written on the slate under the three names, read out by the man of about fifty-one in the same voice at every door at the woman's insistence, with no figure attached and with a number and a nothing both allowed as answers. **The page no longer prints a word-count for it: it said four over a question that is seven, and a correct count is still a count, and this block prints none.** **First asked, the middle of the Long Set: yes**, and the man of about thirty-one of the Standing gave his figure in writing in his own hand and not on the slate, because a slate is a finding and a share of four miles is not a finding. **Second, the top of that Long Set: no** — nine terraces above the water and no money in a hand at the end of a pick, and the fruit is not picked for about six weeks and the water stops before that, and he said he was not going to be asked again this month. **Third, the four lowest terraces: yes, before anybody had finished the question**, from a woman of about thirty-one with her four neighbours, of whom one said it was a mad thing, one said nothing, and one said she would rather not and was not asked whether she would rather not. **Fourth, the settlement at the middle terrace: no**, from the woman of about forty-four who keeps the board: *a record read by four people and not by the valley is not a record the valley can fund*, and the nine households are not the argument, the nine households are the bill. **Neither refusal is villainy, nobody was asked to change, and nobody said out loud that either of the two who said no ought to say yes.** The four sets are a set of holders and are not the Long Set of about eleven terraces, are not the four lowest, are not the four holders inside nine yards of the oldest tree, and are not the four sittings; a successor may not add them to one another and may not convert a set of people into a set of terraces.

1397. **The mending goes from the top down and the fourth hundred pace is last, and that is the cost and not anybody's fault.** The two sets that said yes have money, the money buys men, and there are not nine men within four days and about nine of hard going who can lay that bed. **There are four, and three are the man of about fifty-one, the man of about forty-four who holds the second hoe, and a carter of about nineteen who came in on his own answers, and the fourth is a man of about sixty-four on the second terrace whose hands will last about nine days.** At the top the stone is out where a cart can get to it and at the fourth hundred pace it is not, and a cart that cannot get to it is about four days of barrowing a day. **About nine people laid about three hundred paces of bed the wrong way up** — they began on the low side and then laid it the way you lay a path, small down and big on — **and the top three hundred paces is going to stay wrong until a year nobody has arranged for, and about a hundred paces between the three hundred and the four hundred has not been touched.** The man of about fifty-one let the three hundred paces stand for about four hours to see whether they would come to it on their own, and said out loud at the top, **four hundred paces early and on purpose, that when they get down there he is going to say nine of them cannot do it, so that nobody can say afterwards that they did not know.** A successor may not have the three hundred paces fixed in this volume, may not say a fault is not a fault where the page says it is one, and may not move the fourth hundred pace.

1398. **The ditch, which is the reason four lowest terraces have not gone under in twenty-two winters, and which is not in the plank and never was.** A ditch about two paces wide along the top of the fourth terrace, **cleared every sitting by six people for about nine days**, and the six are the four lowest — a woman of about thirty-one, her four neighbours, and a boy of about nineteen who comes down from the middle when he can. **In this block it is being cut by one, because the six are four terraces below nine men who are mending the channel.** The man of about fifty-one has said out loud that the ditch is not a fault and is not a reason for anything and is a piece of ground about two paces wide that six people cut every year. The woman of about thirty-one's arithmetic is on the page: four men back on the ditch means **the ditch in four days instead of sixteen and the four of us dry this year**, and she says she would take the four men and is not going to take them, because a ditch is a thing six of them cut every sitting and the channel is a thing nobody has mended in about nine years and four men on her ditch buys one year off him, and **seven days of a season that nobody is going to give back**, and he loses nothing. **The first printing of this item quoted the refusal the other way round — *the four of us under for sixteen instead of nine* — which is what the page said before `reviews/volume-12/batch-0004.md` finding 1, and which contradicted the same chapter's own account of what a cut ditch does. The item was wrong about the book and the sentence it was quoting was the defect; that is the class at 1390 and 1403 and it is now recorded here rather than quietly replaced.** This is the first cost in the block and it lands about nine days before the water, on a person who is not in the argument and has never been in it. **A successor may not have the ditch cleared, may not have it in the plank, and may not convert it into a fifth document.**

1399. **Nine days and a morning is the counted figure, and this block is where the figure that two men had got from the main world was checked.** The figure entered the block as **a figure the man of about thirty-one of the Standing put in a made road about nine yards short of a cross of two stones, which he had from two men, the man of about fifty-one and a carter, neither of whom had counted.** The check was made at the fifth hour on the morning the water arrived, by **a woman of about thirty-one with a stick and about nine stones put down the high side of the channel at the water's pace**, and it came to **nine days and a morning**, and the man of about fifty-one wrote the morning on the slate because she asked him to and has not been thanked and is not going to be. The chapter says on the page that being right by accident is not a thing to be glad about and is not a thing to be sorry about, and that this is the only number about this water anybody in that valley has arrived at themselves. **The distribution is on the page and may not be changed: first terrace to second in about a day and a half, to the third in three, to the fourth in five, and the fourth has a house on it; the water comes down at about the seventh hour and stands in the first line of trees by about the eighth; and it goes back off the four lowest at about the fifth hour of the day after the ninth day.** The nine households were told on the day before by nine words at nine doors written on a slate by the man of about thirty-one at the woman's asking, and four of the nine said nothing and three said it would not happen and one said it had happened before and did not last.

1400. **About nine hundred cloths came off about nine hundred feet of line, and the decision was taken by a person who was not asked to take it.** The only flat ground within four hundred paces of the fourth hundred pace is a strip about nine hundred feet long at the middle of the Long Set, and it carries about nine hundred drying cloths, and barrows need flat ground to run on. **A woman of about fifty-eight who turns those cloths, is not a keeper of anything, and has never been asked the Standing's advice on a working morning, decided at the eighth hour that they come down**, and the reason she gives is the chapter's: the cloths are not the man of about fifty-one's, about four fifths of them are the fruit of people who cannot get at a barrow run, one is hers, and the person who should decide is the one who turns them. **A day, four people from the third hour, into the press-house and under the middle terrace wall, and the ones under the wall come out damp, and about two in three of the nine hundred come out dry — a figure she has not counted and had written down that she has not got.** **The woman of about thirty-one on the four lowest was not in that forty yards, nobody went down and told her, the cloths came down at the third hour and were under the wall by the ninth hour, and she was told at about the ninth hour of the day after.** The man of about thirty-eight read the slate and put it face down on the grass, which she has never done to a slate, and would not let the man of about thirty-one pick it up: not now, it is hers and he would be standing in her light with a slate. **Nobody was angry, and that is on the page as the other fact about that eighth hour**, and about four of the nine gave as their reason that nobody in that forty yards had done anything wrong and about nine gave as their reason that it was worse than angry. **The man of about sixty of the Standing was told at the ninth hour, asked three questions, was told he was not asked and by whom and was given no answer to whether it was right, and said there is no room to put it in, that is not a rule and is not a precedent, that in about four years somebody will say a woman once did a thing on her own and they will be right, and that he is not going to say they are wrong and is not going to say they are right, and told them not to write it down.** A successor may not have that decision made again, may not make the man of about sixty answer the third question, and may not turn two in three into a counted figure.

1401. **The standing rule that governs him has been separated from his reading of it, and the separation is canon.** The rule, made by the man of about sixty in the blossom sitting and unchanged, is that **a finding about a road in this valley has to be made by a person who can be moved off the ground here. The woman of about thirty-eight said out loud in the pick sitting that the rule does not say a person who holds nothing, that he has been treating the two as the same thing since the set sitting, and that she is the proof: she holds four blocks, has held them nine years, dug a hole at the foot of the oldest tree with a spade in her own hand in front of about eleven people, said yes to about ninety people when she was about sixteen and did not go, and nobody in that valley has ever been able to move her off that ground including her.** Two consequences are on the page and both are new. **One, a man who stands on a rule is not obeying it.** **Two, and this is the one a successor must not soften: in a place where nothing is voted, a man who does not say no is a man who has already been told.** In this block he was asked for about forty paces of the fourth hundred pace, the one job he is able to do, and **he did not say no out loud**; the man of about fifty-one said out loud that he was not going to give the rest of the reason because he gave it last time and has not found a better version in about nine days, and that the reason has not gone away. **Nobody thanked him for not doing it and nobody said out loud that he should have done it, and neither of those may be said in this volume.** He barrowed from the second hour to the ninth hour and is good at it and it is not what the valley needs, and the man of about fifty-one did not watch and did not say he had done it well. The man of about thirty-one of the Standing came down to ask the question and did not ask it and said nothing at all, the first time in about nineteen years. **He did not put a straight edge on the lip of the fourth hundred pace and did not hold one end of it; the chalk line cut in the set sitting is now under about four inches of new course and cannot be seen from the top, and nobody cut a second one.** A successor may not have him decide the refusal again, may not have him put a straight edge there, and may not have anybody thank him for not doing it.

1402. **What the block ends on, and the seven things it did not move, and the figures a successor must re-derive rather than inherit.** **The water is back where it was**, at about the fifth hour of the day after the ninth day, and the four lowest terraces have their water and the top of the Long Set has its water, and the channel between them is what it was except for **about four hundred paces of good new bed at the fourth hundred pace**, with the top three hundred paces wrong and about a hundred paces between them untouched. **The turn of the five sittings came at the ninth hour and the bar sitting begins**, and a year is a turn of the five and no month is a sitting and no sitting is a month, and the chapter says so on the page. **The money was found by the two sets that said yes and is gone, and it bought about four hundred paces of bed, about nine hundred feet of barrow run, about nine hundred cloths off about nine hundred feet of line in one day, a month of bread for about nine men, a boy's penny on four days of a sitting, and about four days of a carter; the two sets that said no were not wrong about that and have not been asked whether they were right.** **And these did not move:** the Crown's form's fourth head is ruled and empty and about six months empty and nobody filled it; the half leaf is face down upstairs and nobody turned it over and nobody has been booked to turn it over and **nobody has printed how many days it has been there**; the answer is not made and the woman of about thirty-eight has still not been told she may speak for a road; **the bar of wood is still in the grass at the foot of the Low Lane, it was never lifted, nobody has asked whether it should be, and the carter of about forty has still not come back in and the fruit of that day is on no road in nine counties**; the two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between; the twenty-second notch is not cut and nobody has been sent to that coast; the shut road four hundred miles off is shut; the city is on a shelf with its three protected numerals at zero; the antagonist is not named and no chapter said whose plan the letter is; **nobody in nine counties has been told that a man of thirty-two with a limp is about nine terraces above a bar of wood lying in the grass**; the breach between the two men is open and neither of them is in this block and the barred kin-term stays at two; no System panel is printed in this block and *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are at zero in it; and **nobody was thanked in any chapter, in any speech, and in none of this item.**

1403. **The review of Batch 0004, run by a phase that wrote none of the block, and a second pass over this repair run by a third phase: nine findings from the review and four from the second pass, thirteen in all, thirteen real, thirteen repaired in place, and no chapter restarted and no plot moved.** A review that reports a number of findings and is itself a claim is not a measurement, and this item is the record of all thirteen and of the two classes none of them could see. **Every figure below was recounted on the ten chapter files after the last prose edit of this repair, in one run and on one basis, and the figures the writing pass printed came off `wc -w` and do not agree with them, which is the tenth time this repository has compared a numerator and a denominator taken on two different tools.** Items 1 to 1402 stand and are not renumbered.

**One, the speech-length figure in 0589 that its own speech did not carry, and that the block's own standard bars.** The page said she *said nine sentences in the oldest voice in that valley* and, four lines below, that the silence ran *for about as long as a man takes to say nine*, and the coda printed *Nine sentences, in the oldest voice in that valley*. **Her speech is printed in six quoted turns and runs to thirteen sentences, so the figure was wrong as well as being the thing Batches 0001 to 0003 printed zero of.** Repaired three times over rather than moved to thirteen, because the bar is on the certification and not only on the arithmetic: the count is gone from all three places, the silence is now a thing that happens while spoil dries on the low side, and the coda names the voice. **A successor may not reinstate a count on that speech and may not certify a length on any speech in this block.**

**Two, the antecedent in 0589.** The coda said *the boy of about fifteen rang it, meaning the bell*, and the nearest antecedent was the barrow standing empty on the low side, so the gloss patched the meaning and not the reference. Repaired to *rang the bell*, four words, no other movement.

**Three, an enumeration one short of the list it describes, in 0588, which the review's instrument could not see.** The review's coda counter matched *are N figures of* and not *are N separate counts of*, and the 0588 persons claim was in the second form. **The list is five — about nine people standing in forty yards, about nine households on four terraces, about ninety people twenty-two winters ago, three names on a slate, and a man of about sixty-four on the second terrace — and the page said four.** Repaired to *five*. **All twenty-nine numeric claims across the ten cost codas were then counted by hand against the lists they describe and all twenty-nine foot**, and the forms that have to be matched are *are N figures of*, *are N separate counts of*, *are N shapes* and *are N more figures of*. **A claim with no number in it is honest and is not to be given one**: 0587 and 0589 both end their persons list unnumbered and both are left that way.

**Four, a word-count in 0582 that was checked against nothing.** The page said *The question was four words long* and then printed the question in full four lines below, and *What is your share of four miles* is seven. **The first repair corrected it to seven, and the second pass removed the count instead, because this block prints zero speech-length figures and a correct count is still a count.** The page now reads *The question is on the slate under the three names, and nobody added to it.* **The same pass removed the unverifiable count in 0588**, where the page said the man of about fifty-one *said four words, and the four words were that the cloths were not his* — five words, and a paraphrase rather than a quotation, so the claim was unverifiable by construction.

**Five, a count in 0587 that the first repair saw and mis-sorted, and that is the worst defect in the block.** The page said the woman of about thirty-one said *the same nine words at each door* and that *the nine words are on a slate*, and then printed the slate in full two lines below: **Tomorrow the water comes down.** Five words. **The first repair's own sweep printed all three instances, classified them as being about an object and not about a speech, and wrote that classification into this item as a closed question, which is worse than the defect because a successor inherits it as settled.** All three counts are gone; *say the same thing at each door*, *what she said is on a slate* and *a woman who walks nine doors with a slate in her hand*. **The nine doors stay, because nine households and nine doors are on the page and a count of doors is not a count of words.**

**Six, a person described two ways inside one chapter, in 0586.** The dateline read *a man of about fifty-eight off the hill with a barrow* and the body establishes the man from off the hill as **a man of thirty-two with a limp**, which is also what 0589's dateline says. Repaired. **The woman of about fifty-eight who turns the cloths is a different person and is on that bank in the same chapter, and the two must not be merged.**

**Seven, a sentence in the 0585 coda that had come apart.** It read *and four divisions on a board four days and about nine off are four shapes*, where *four days and about nine off* had swallowed the *off* of *the board off the middle terrace* and left *are four shapes* with no subject. Repaired to *four divisions on the board off the middle terrace*, which is the house form 0583 and 0588 use.

**Eight, a speech announced and never printed, in 0588, pre-existing and three lines above a line the first repair edited.** The page said *The man of about fifty-one said the thing nobody else would say* and the line under it is the woman of about fifty-eight's, and four lines under that the page says of him *He did not argue and did not agree*, so the man of about fifty-one is announced as the speaker of a speech that is not his. Repaired to **Nobody else was going to say it and the man of about fifty-one was not going to say it either**, which agrees with the line below it and gives the *Then* in the woman's mouth an antecedent in her own preceding sentence. **A speech that is announced on the page is printed on the page, and a character who is described as not arguing is not the character who said the thing nobody else would say.**

**Nine, the coda as a template.** *Nobody thanked anybody* closed ten of ten and *none of them goes in at either end of this column* appeared in ten of ten. The cost-paragraph openers had already been varied and *And the cost of that* is at zero in ten. **The two closing formulas are now varied and the fact is not:** the exact string closes **seven of ten** and the other three render the same fact in their own words, in 0584, 0585 and 0586, and *at either end of this column* is at **seven of ten**, with 0582, 0586 and 0588 carrying three different forms of the same joke, and the longest run of words two chapters of this block share is now **twenty-one**, recounted at `reviews/volume-12/batch-0004.md` finding 11 with self-matches excluded, in 0584 against 0587, and it is the volume's own frame sentence *and the two sets do not overlap and she has not been asked which of the two she thinks she is*, which stands twenty-seven times in Volume 12 and is the volume's argument rendered as form rather than a lift of prose. The twenty this item first printed did not reproduce. **Nobody is thanked anywhere in the block, which is a rule about the world, and the sentence that says so is a form, and a form that is in ten of ten is a form and not an argument.** The first repair's 0586 closer was rejected by the second pass and rewritten: it repeated eighteen lines of the same chapter almost word for word, **which trades a ten-of-ten repetition inside a book for a duplication inside a chapter, and the second is the worse of the two.**

**And the two things that were not repaired, and why.** The stale `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and is escalated at `state/open-threads.md` 391 rather than touched. **The turn of the five sittings arriving at the ninth hour of a Wednesday, in 0590, with nine men laying stone on a Sunday in 0589, is a world-rule question and not a defect in the page: the block's only stated weekday rule is that the room over a saddler's is shut on Sundays, and 0589's Sunday is a Sunday in the valley, not in the market town.** It is recorded at `state/open-threads.md` 394 for the volume close, **because changing it means moving a day in a day list that the volume outline fixed and the next prompt inherited, and that is not a repair.**

**The block as it stood after the repair this item records, recounted on the files in one run, and superseded on every figure by 1404.** **20,165 words in ten chapters**, per chapter 2,376 / 1,975 / 2,299 / 1,997 / 1,789 / 1,849 / 1,643 / 2,087 / 2,021 / 2,129; the ten deltas against the writing pass are 0 / −6 / 0 / +61 / +40 / +57 / +2 / +9 / +25 / −19, which is +169; **Volume 12 was 75,708 in forty chapters and the manuscript was 1,664,560 in five hundred and ninety, and 1,588,852 + 75,708 reproduced the second figure from the first.** **Ten money columns at 157, 181, 184, 201, 206, 211, 223, 227, 224 and 219 (the Volume 12 close later moved Chapter 0583's column off 184, which is a footed column at `volume-03/chapter-0138.md`, and it now reads 203) pence, all ten footing item for item, sum for sum, printed total for printed total and mark-conversion for mark-conversion, eleven items in every column, and none of the ten colliding with the distinct totals the house form returns over the five hundred and eighty chapters before this block, in 161 instances of which 119 are hyphenated and 42 are not.** **The 133 distinct totals this sentence first printed does not reproduce and is 149 on the basis named beside it; the instance count and both halves of the hyphen split are right and the set size was wrong, which is the shape this item itself condemns three paragraphs above. It is corrected at 1404 and the 133 is recorded here as a first printing.** The ten chapter days re-derive from day 930 with **0 mismatches on the weekday half, 0 on the day-of-month half and 0 on the market-morning claim**, and 0589's Sunday contains nothing in the room over a saddler's. Barred tokens, barred numerals and the soft list are at **0**. **`about` was 522 case-insensitive whole-word in 20,165, which is 25.89 per 1,000, and 508 lower-case, against 524 at 26.21 in the 19,996 the writing pass left it, both on the same basis in the same run, and 0590 and 0588 remain the two highest chapters in the block, because their codas are its two largest enumerations and the figures in them are canon figures a person in that valley could not have counted exactly. The rate is not a figure to steer to and the prompt says so.**

## Volume 12 Batch 0004, the second repair pass — items 1404 to 1406, and the highest number in this file is now 1406

**This section is appended under the three rules at the top of this file: items are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, and a volume is not archived while it is being written.** Items 1 to 1403 stand and are not renumbered. **This was a repair pass and not a draft: the ten chapters were finished prose on disk when the run began, the prompt for this block was on disk with no marker beside it, and the correct work was to read them and look for one fact described two different way across chapters, since no instrument in this repository can find that.** The batch's own prompt had already been carried forward with four superseded figures and that was corrected by the earlier pass at `state/open-threads.md` 395. **Eleven findings, seven in the prose and four in the records, eleven real, eleven repaired in place, no chapter restarted and no plot beat moved.** The full record is `reviews/volume-12/batch-0004.md`. **Every figure below was counted on the ten chapter files after the last prose edit in this pass, in one run and on one basis, and the figures the two earlier passes printed are recorded as first printings rather than replaced.**

1404. **The seven prose repairs, and every one of them is one fact described two different ways or one figure checked against a number instead of against a page.** **One, the ditch in 0584.** The page said at the sixth hour that a cut ditch is what has kept the water off the four lowest for twenty-two winters, and nine lines later the same woman refused four men on her ditch on the ground that they would leave the four of us under for sixteen instead of nine — **the help made it worse, which is the opposite of what she had just said the ditch does, and the refusal argued for the harm.** The two clauses are rewritten and nothing else moved: four men now gives her the ditch in four days instead of sixteen and the four of them dry this year, she says she would take them and is not going to take them, and the reason is the one the page already supports — a ditch is cut every sitting, the channel has not been mended in about nine years, and four men on her ditch buys one year off him. **Seven days of a season nobody is going to give back stays and is now stated once.** Item 1398 quoted the defective arithmetic and has been corrected to the page. **Two, the 0584 coda** said *the four who said no* where 0582 and 0590 both have **two** sets saying no out of four; repaired, count held at four. **Three, 0585's pot of earth** ended in a sentence no one can parse, *nobody in this room has been told that in nine days and has not asked*; repaired, and this is the second time this chapter has carried a sentence that came apart, which is the shape of a repair rather than the shape of a draft at thread 375. **Four, 0585 lifted forty consecutive words out of 0576** on the punctuation-stripped basis, the half-leaf paragraph with only its commas moved, which was the longest run of words any chapter of this block shared with any of the five hundred and eighty before it; the paragraph now says the same four facts in its own order and the run is gone from the top of the table. **Five, 0586 said about four hundred paces of the same work still to be done in five places** on a morning when three hundred paces were already done, against 0590's own *about a hundred paces nobody has touched at all between the three hundred and the four hundred* and 0589's *I am not asking you for the four hundred, I am asking you for the forty*; repaired in the dateline, the body, the speech, the coda and the closer, and **the four hundred is kept where it belongs, as the length of the mend beyond the fault.** **Six, 0587 printed *hisself*.** **Seven, 0590 said the water came down on the ninth day and that the ninth day was a morning**, against 0587's own arrival on the first morning at about the seventh hour and the counted figure of nine days and a morning; repaired, and **the ninth is now a morning as well as a day and that is on a slate in this valley and is not in a book**, which puts the counted figure on the page instead of only in this file. **A successor may not make the ditch the thing that puts water on the terraces, may not have four sets say no, may not restore the copied paragraph, may not put four hundred paces back in 0586, and may not have the water arrive on the ninth day.**

1405. **Two more prose repairs that are not a fact described twice, and the protagonist's name, and the three figures in this repository's own records that did not reproduce.** **The 0590 coda** gave *the dearest and the smallest* to the woman of about thirty-eight and then *the dearest of all* to the woman of about thirty31, and 0582 gives it to the woman of about thirty-one alone in a bold line; as printed two women held the same superlative and one did not hold it at all, **and the sentence also put a woman of about thirty-eight and a woman of about thirty31 in one clause with neither named by her work, which is the exact collision the batch card warns about.** Rewritten once, both fixed: the woman of about thirty-eight, who keeps four blocks on that middle, gave a share and has not said what her part came to, and the share of the woman of about thirty-one who keeps the four lowest **was the smallest of the four and the dearest of the four.** **The 0581 coda** listed *a penny for a bell* among four figures of persons; a penny is not a person, and the clause exists to keep the boy's penny out of the column, which is the right instinct in the wrong category; the list now holds the boy who is one of the three names, count held at four, and the penny clause is kept whole. **`Aren Kest` stood at 1 in ten chapters, in 0589 alone, in the block he is in**, against Batch 0003's 6 in five of ten, and the batch card is explicit that Batch 0003's figure is that block's shape and not a licence for the cost block. **He is physically on the page in two chapters and is now named in both: in 0586 in the paragraph that introduces him on the bank, and in 0589 in the sentence that says nobody has asked him which of the two he believes. He stands at 3 in two of ten and was not named in the eight chapters where he is not present**, because a name in a chapter a man is not in is a false claim about a person. **Nothing he does was changed: he is not put in charge of anything, is not asked to inspect anything, is given barrows and nothing else, is good at them, and still does not say no out loud.** **And the three record figures.** The house form over the five hundred and eighty chapters before this block, extracted hyphen-tolerantly on that basis, is **161 instances in 149 distinct totals, 119 hyphenated and 42 not**; `state/continuity.md` 1403 and `state/open-threads.md` 388 both printed **133** distinct, **the instance count and both halves of the hyphen split reproduce exactly and the set size does not**, which is the correct half attached to the incorrect whole that thread 388 itself names, and it is the eleventh time this repository has taken a numerator and a denominator on two different bases. The longest run of words two chapters of this block share is **21**, not the 20 that 1403 printed, recounted with self-matches excluded, and it is the volume's own frame sentence and is left in the prose. **A successor must not trust any of the three and should write the counter before the columns, not after them: four instruments written for this pass were each wrong at least once, and the first reported every block chapter as sharing 2,398 words with itself, which is the seed-length failure at thread 376 in its purest form.**

1406. **The block recounted on the files after the last prose edit in this pass, every basis named, and the seven things a successor must not do with a figure printed here.** **Plain whitespace split, title line included, each file on its own and the ten added: Batch 0004 is 20,378 words in ten chapters, per chapter 2,403 / 1,975 / 2,299 / 2,060 / 1,821 / 1,875 / 1,643 / 2,087 / 2,019 / 2,196.** Volumes 01 to 11 recount to **1,588,852** in 550 chapters, Batches 0001 to 0003 of this volume to **55,543**, Volume 12's forty chapters to **75,921**, and **the manuscript is 1,664,773 in five hundred and ninety chapters, and 1,588,852 + 55,543 + 20,378 reproduces it.** A concatenated `cat` of the five hundred and ninety files returns the same 1,664,773 and **no chapter file in the manuscript is missing its trailing newline**, so the per-file and concatenated bases agree. **The 20,165 and the 1,664,560 the two earlier passes printed were correct for the files as they stood before this repair and are not correct now, and the 1,664,391 the writing pass printed is a first printing of a shorter book.** **Ten money columns at 157, 181, 184, 201, 206, 211, 223, 227, 224 and 219 (the Volume 12 close later moved Chapter 0583's column off 184, which is a footed column at `volume-03/chapter-0138.md`, and it now reads 203) pence, eleven items in every column, prices from 2 to 41, all ten footing item sum, printed total and mark conversion, ten distinct totals, no barred numeral in any total, over-figure or printed price, and no over-figure at or above forty-eight.** Collision, by two independent routes: **no collision against the 122 distinct footed totals extracted from the money paragraphs of the 580 chapters before this block, and none against the 149 distinct totals the hyphen-tolerant house form returns over the same 580.** **The calendar re-derives from day 930 with 0 mismatches on the weekday half and 0 on the day-of-month half**, four of the ten days are market days, 1791 is a Sunday and is an Orchard chapter with nothing in the room over a saddler's. **Barred tokens, the four barred numerals at block and at Volume 12 scope, the soft list, `continuation` and the retired reporting frame are at 0**, and the frame is **939** in the 550 chapters before this volume, which is why it is not a zero gate. **`about` is 525 case-insensitive whole-word and 511 lower-case in 20,378 words, which is 25.76 and 25.08 per 1,000 on a case-insensitive whole-word numerator over a whitespace denominator**, with 0590 at 34.2 and 0588 at 34.5 the two highest chapters because their codas are the block's two largest enumerations. Punctuation **1 question mark, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons, 0 em dashes**. **`Aren Kest` at 3 in two of ten.** A capitalised two-word sweep returns **Aren Kest, Long Set, Low Lane, The Crown** and one line-wrap artefact, **and no name was spent, so Volume 12's budget is still 2 of 5 with three slots unspent.** **Twenty-nine coda enumerations counted item by item and all 29 foot.** The longest run of words shared by two chapters of the block is **21**; the longest against the 580 prior chapters is **82**, and it is 0585's dateline against 0576's, which is the volume's own main-world dateline form correctly varied. **A successor quoting any figure from this paragraph must count it first: this project has now printed seven different manuscript figures for the same five hundred and ninety chapters and each was correct on the day it was printed and none is correct now.**
