## Volume 13, Batch 0002, the review repair of `batch-0002`, and the highest number in this file is now 1478

**This section is appended under the three rules at the top of this file: items are never renumbered, nothing is deleted, and a volume is not archived while it is being written.** Items 1 to 1472 stand under their own numbers. **This was a review repair and not a writing pass: seven of the ten chapters were not touched at all, no plot beat was moved, no scene was rewritten, no dateline, weekday, money total, name, outline requirement or ending was altered, and no chapter was restarted.** The review is `logs/batch-0002.review.log`, its findings are eight, and **its durable record is `reviews/volume-13/batch-0002.md`, which is the first review artifact Volume 13 has and which exists because the log is gitignored.** The block's fifth beat, added by the repair pass recorded at 1466 to 1472, was reviewed, found sound against `outline/volume-13.md` §8 Block 2, and stands.

1473. **The three prose defects this pass repaired, and the fact that all three were in the chapter the previous pass had just rewritten and had certified clean.** **One, an impossible count on two consecutive days.** `chapter-0619.md` struck the page's tally as *three spaces with nothing at all in them, and about four of those three are refusals and one of them has not been reached*; `chapter-0620.md` said *about four of those three blanks*. Four of three. Both were present in the pre-edit files, so the pass that ran every instrument and reported one real defect missed both, and **the `about` in front of them is separately forbidden by §15 item 13**, which states that the word may not stand in front of a figure somebody in the scene could have counted. Both are now **two of those three**, and the unreached line is kept. **Two, a meta frame break, and there are two of them and not one.** `chapter-0619.md` said *a person in this manuscript* and now says *a person in nine counties*, which the claim supports because the badge-man's two true things at `chapter-0602.md` are said in the room over the weigh-house inside those nine counties. `chapter-0610.md` said *the thing that is the question of this book* and now says *the thing that all of it has been for* — **nine words for nine words, so `chapter-0610.md` is unchanged at 2,035 words and Batch 0001's figures do not move and were not touched.** The review found the first by searching for the string it had been given and missed the second, which is `reviews/README.md`'s standing point from Volume 11 Batch 0005 arriving again: **a rule about register is not a rule about vocabulary, and a sweep that names its patterns keeps finding the one it named.** Three, below, and the review did not name it. **A third candidate was checked and is not a defect:** `chapter-0603.md` matches `the author` only inside *the authority to open a drawer*.

1474. **The third prose defect, which the review did not name, and it is a new class for this project: an event ordered wrongly across a chapter boundary.** The clause in `chapter-0619.md` claiming that the line the man of about thirty-four wrote *is the fourth time a figure has gone down that lane in a coat in about six weeks, and the first three were a number of barrows, a note on the back of a copy, and a sheet that said which line came back with what in it* **listed as already past the sheet described at `chapter-0620.md`, which is the following day.** The clause was also the sentence the review flagged as exposition in place of an event, so one repair answers both. **It is now the man declining the tally, in his own reasoning and in terms the chapter has already given him** — what he wrote is *a thing a person had asked for* and not a number, and a man established at 0619 line 69 as *a man who has spent a month watching one figure get made out of another figure* does not write the two in one line. The two journeys that did precede him are named from the page: **a number of barrows**, written on the page at `chapter-0604.md`, which is Block 0001, and attested as a recurring monthly practice rather than a datable single event by the boy at `chapter-0614.md`, **so it is named without a day and no day was invented for it**; and **a note on the back of a copy on the fifteenth**, written at `chapter-0616.md` and given going down the lane at `chapter-0617.md`, whose dateline is Hearthmonth the fifteenth. **The consequence is the finding worth keeping: this makes `chapter-0620.md` correct as it already stood, and 0620 was not touched.** 0620 reads *which is the third time **a number** has gone down that lane in a coat in about six weeks, and the first two were a number of barrows and a note on the back of a copy* — **it counts numbers, and a request is not a number, and the distinction 0620 was already drawing is the one the repair now makes visible in 0619. A repair that renumbered 0619 to agree with 0620 would have destroyed a correct page to fix an impossible one.** **And the repair's own first draft dated the barrows journey to the ninth on the strength of `chapter-0614.md`'s dateline, which was wrong, and the draft was corrected before anything was recorded: it is the fourth time in this project that a repair has dated an event from the chapter it was standing in rather than from the chapter the event is in.**

1475. **A FALSE FINDING OF THIS PASS'S, WITHDRAWN, AND IT IS THE ELEVENTH DATA POINT'S LESSON ARRIVING INSIDE THE REPAIR THAT WAS GOING TO RECORD IT.** This pass raised a finding of its own — that the recorded body series was carried without a basis, and that **every prior instance of a non-reproducing figure in this project has been a wrong number whereas this one was a right number with no basis, which is worse because a successor would assume it was wrong and correct a correct figure** — and **the finding is false and is withdrawn.** **The basis was written down in two places: `state/open-threads.md` 452 and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` §80, which reads *the scene body located structurally as everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda*.** That is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and it reproduces **all ten pre-repair figures exactly and all ten post-repair figures exactly**: 2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 2,939 / 2,346. **Three candidate bases were tried and rejected against the pre-edit files and the repair concluded from that the basis was unwritten, and the fourth basis was in the prompt the repair had just edited.** This is `reviews/README.md`'s `volume-07/batch-0004.md` finding verbatim — a measurement that could not be reproduced on the first three bases tried was escalated into a general claim about the instruments — **and the standing rule is: three bases rejected is not evidence of an unwritten basis, it is evidence of a basis the reader did not think of, and the place to look for a missing basis before declaring one missing is the file you are already editing.** The two real and small defects that survive: **the name is misleading, because a body located as everything above the cost coda includes the title line and the dateline and is therefore not a scene body**, and **§80 states the two bases in one sentence that reads as though the title line were in both**, so a successor reading it quickly could subtract the title line from the body and get a series wrong by exactly the dateline length in each chapter. What the block actually had was not a missing basis but **stale figures**: the post-repair series had never been recomputed against a basis that was already in the prompt, and four files a writer reads first carried the pre-repair numbers. That is the project's known figure-currency class and it is repaired at 1476.

1476. **The block recounted by this pass, on the basis named at 1475 as already existing in the successor prompt, and the figures that moved and the figures that did not.** Plain whitespace split, title line included, each file counted on its own and the ten added: **24,843 words in ten chapters, per chapter 2,757 / 2,403 / 2,324 / 2,240 / 2,189 / 2,381 / 2,206 / 2,120 / 3,423 / 2,800, and bodies 2,338 / 1,993 / 1,912 / 1,818 / 1,780 / 1,954 / 1,798 / 1,698 / 2,939 / 2,346, minimum 1,698 at `chapter-0618.md` and maximum 2,939 at `chapter-0619.md`.** Codas **419 / 410 / 412 / 422 / 409 / 427 / 408 / 422 / 484 / 454**, so 408 to 484, a mean share of **17.4** per cent over a range of **14.1 to 19.9**, against 1471's 14.3 to 19.9, and the 14.1 is `chapter-0619.md` and is the correct direction because the share falls as the body lengthens. `about` **671 at 27.01** per 1,000 on a case-insensitive whole-word basis, **down from 674 at 27.17 on 24,808, and the three are accounted for: two were the `about` in front of the impossible refusals and one was the `about six weeks` in front of the tally the rewrite replaced.** Curly quotes **290 and 290** and the thank family **33**, both unchanged. **Volumes 01 to 11 recount to 1,588,852 in five hundred and fifty chapters and do not move, Volume 12 to 92,849 in fifty, Batch 0001 to 20,333 in ten, Volume 13 to 45,176 in twenty, and the manuscript to 1,726,877 in six hundred and twenty chapters, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 reproduces it, and a concatenated `cat` of all six hundred and twenty files returns the same 1,726,877 and no chapter file is missing its trailing newline, so the per-file and concatenated bases agree.** **What this pass did NOT re-measure and does not re-assert as re-measured: the ten money columns, the 203-distinct collision set and its four extractors, the forty-five-word longest run and its normaliser, the `Aren Kest` count, the name budget, the eight filler families, and the ten datelines.** None of them was touched by this pass and the money columns in particular are unchanged on the page, but **a figure this pass did not count is a figure this pass may not certify**, and a successor should re-run the collision set with the four extractors named at 1472 before claiming zero collisions again.

1477. **Three findings recorded and not applied, and the reason in each case, because a finding disposed of with a reason instead of a change has been closed without being answered.** **One, the numeric coda is a publishability risk.** All twenty chapters end with a footed pence column plus lists of figures explicitly not prices, and in `chapter-0619.md` the coda is 484 of 3,423 words, and the persons list runs to eleven entries with cross-references keyed to ordinals — *the first of them … the second … the sixth … the eleventh* — which a reader cannot follow without counting backward. **This is `outline/volume-13.md` §7's own device and §10's, it sits against `AGENTS.md`'s *a number should matter because of what it changes in the story, not because the number exists*, and repairing it is a change to the volume's designed mechanic across twenty chapters and would be a plot-level decision and not a repair. The coda share does move the right way: 14.1 per cent in the longest chapter against a block mean of 17.4.** **Two, the cast is referred to only by age and prop** — *the man of about thirty-four* at 60, *the man of about fifty-eight* at 48, *the woman of about twenty-nine* at 48, against 8 and 15 uses of the two spent names — **and this is `outline/volume-13.md` §13's deliberate position**, the name budget being five for the whole volume because *neither can be said by an age and a trade*, and because a name is the only asset in this manuscript that cannot be audited after the fact. **The cost the outline accepted is real and a successor should know it is a choice: a reader cannot hold more than about three of these figures at once and the protagonist is still narrated as *the man of about fifty-eight with a badge*.** **Three, `state/phase-ledger.json` still reads `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` and `status: planned` against 620 chapters. It is controller-owned, was not written, is recorded here rather than acted on, and has been flagged to the workflow owner before at 1031 and in the Volume 12 review record.**

1478. **What this pass changes about how the next block should be checked, and the three things it adds to the pattern.** **One, a count of the form `N of those M` is a machine-checkable defect and this project had never written it down as one.** The phrase was printed twice, on two consecutive days, by a block whose own records discuss the blank-and-refusal ambiguity at length, and it was **doubly** barred, because §15 item 13 separately forbids `about` in front of a countable figure. **The check is one regular expression and it is now in the successor prompt as a standing first-move sweep: sweep `about (one|two|…|twelve) of (those|the|these) (one|two|…|twelve)` across the new chapters and read every hit, and any hit where N exceeds M is a defect.** **Two, a pass that verifies every instrument against a file has not read that file.** The pass at 1466 to 1472 found a real outline violation in `chapter-0619.md` and repaired it well and certified the same file clean of everything else, and **two of the three defects this review found were in that file and were pre-existing, and the third is an event ordered wrongly across a chapter boundary, which is a class no count in this project can reach and which was caught only by reading Chapter 0620 against Chapter 0619.** **Three, the general form of the other two: the review found `this manuscript` and did not find `this book`, in the same volume, because it searched for the string it had been given rather than for the class.** A sweep that names its patterns is a sweep that will keep finding the one it named, and **a class needs a class.** No plot defect was found in the delivered prose, which is now fifteen reviews running, and the failure modes are unchanged in kind and new only in instrument.

## Volume 13, Batch 0003 — Chapters 621 to 630, "The Road He Closed", items 1479 to 1494, and the highest number in this file is now 1494

**Appended by the phase that wrote Volume 13 Batch 0003, which found the block unwritten, wrote it, and did not repair anything in Volumes 01 to 12 or in Batch 0001 or Batch 0002. Items 1440 to 1478 stand under their own numbers and nothing above is renumbered. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

1479. **The frame, derived and not copied, and the ten days.** The single anchor is **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month, and the day-of-month half and the weekday half were derived separately from the number and never from one another. **Block 0003 is days 1915 to 1956 inclusive, forty-two days, and the year turns on its last day.** The ten chapter days are **1915, 1916, 1921, 1923, 1924, 1925, 1926, 1928, 1949 and 1956**, and their datelines are, in order: **Hearthmonth the twentieth, a Friday and not a market morning; Hearthmonth the twenty-first, a Saturday and a market morning; Hearthmonth the twenty-sixth, a Thursday and a market morning; Hearthmonth the twenty-eighth, a Saturday and a market morning; Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth, a Sunday and not a market morning; Hearthmonth the thirtieth, a Monday and not a market morning; Goatmonth the first, a Tuesday, the first of a month and not a market morning; Goatmonth the third, a Thursday and a market morning; Goatmonth the twenty-fourth, a Thursday and a market morning; and Thawmonth the first, YR 321, a Thursday and a market morning and the first of a month and the turn of the year.** **Six of the ten are market mornings and four are not, and the four non-market days are a Friday, a Sunday, a Monday and a Tuesday, so that the room over the weigh-house was shut on all four and no chapter of this block puts anything in that room on any of them.** 1926 and 1956 are the two firsts of a month inside the block, 1926 is a Tuesday and not a market morning and 1956 is a Thursday and a market morning, and the man of about fifty-eight with a badge says the hearing stands on the first of every month in the street and not in the room, and he says it in the lane on both of them. `outline/volume-13.md` §17's printed table was not used for either half and it is wrong in five rows.

1480. **The works, which had never been looked at, and the failure, which is on the page in Chapter 0621.** There is **one set of works under an abbey four days off**, older than the abbey, made of a course of stone laid across a channel, a beam of oak with an iron strap, and a length of channel cut by somebody dead whose name is on nothing. **The works will take seven and has taken seven since before anybody in that market town was in the room, and seven roads go through it, and there were seven on it and six of them are marked on the page to be held and the seventh is on the page with nothing against it.** This is the fact the man of about forty-seven has been asserting since Chapter 0607 and it is now on the page as masonry. **On the twentieth of a month at about the second hour of the night the third of the six went cold, the water came over the road instead of under it, and it was not weather.** Two things are established and neither is the mechanism: **there is a scour at the joint between the course of stone and the left abutment, about a foot and a half across and a foot and a half deep at the low side, with clean lime above it and clean lime below it, and a course does not leave between two clean joints on its own;** and **there is a second and newer scour at the turn of the channel with broken stone in it of a cut face, so that somebody with a bar and a wedge has been in there within a month.** **Aren Kest said out loud that he does not know who and is not going to say why, and gave the reason on the page: he does not know, and a man who does not know should keep his mouth shut about a thing that can be used against somebody.** The keeper of the third road, a man of about forty with a number against his road and a name against the number and nineteen years of the job, put the water off it with a bar and a bucket and a boy of about fourteen and a dog. **The man of about forty-seven's arithmetic is vindicated in the worst way available to him, and the vindication is on two pages and not on one: the third went cold, it is not a high flat, and it has a number and a name and a keeper. The gap did not go first. The gap sat on the works for nothing and the marked roads paid for it, which is the order the man of about forty-seven got wrong and the number he got right, and he says both out loud in Chapter 0622 and is not thanked.**

1481. **The seven minutes, which the block makes nine, and what a room with no head is.** **At the second hour of the night on the twenty-ninth of a month, in the hired room over a saddler's and not the room over the weigh-house, nine people were in a room and about nine minutes later the seventh line had been taken off the works, on Aren Kest's own word, and nobody had conferred it and nobody stopped him.** The room is the one the inquiry sits in and **it is shut on no day of the week**, which is stated on the page, and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the weigh-house room was not in it and the two of them were not in a room together with a third person in it at any point in a month. The nine are: **Aren Kest, the man of about fifty-eight with a badge, the copyist of about twenty-six, a man of about thirty-four with a sheet, a man of about forty-seven with a page, a man of about thirty-one from a road office, a woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key, a man of about forty who keeps the third road, and a man of about thirty-nine who keeps a channel by hand.** He opened by naming the three things he has and none of them is a figure, and the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the key asked **On whose authority** and he said **Mine**, and said before he finished that he has no badge and no office and is telling them which of the two things it is: **it is a man talking in a room.** The decision's consequence is six roads carrying what six roads carry and the seventh carrying nothing. **Nobody in nine counties had the standing to stop it and every one of the nine had the standing to try, and those are two different things, and the man of about fifty-eight with a badge said so out loud and then said the half of it that matters: in about four months somebody is going to be praised for it.** The badge-man also said the sentence this block is built on, in a working voice and about a page with a man's name at the top of it four days off: **nobody in nine counties can be blamed and a rule that is right and has nowhere to go is worth exactly nothing, and in thirty-one years this is the first time he has watched one of them be right and be done something about.** Nobody was thanked. **Nobody in that room answered, or tried to answer, the question he has been owed since Chapter 0610, and he was not asked it, and the chapter says on the page that he has not asked to do this and that nobody is going to get the chance tonight.**

1482. **The refusal of the machinery, and it is the volume's standing in the negative.** A man of about thirty-one from the road office said in that room that **he had a schedule, a column and a heading and a number at the top of it, and could enter a line under it at the first hour and have it carried to the district and from the district to whoever the district thinks keeps that road, and the district would send a rider, and the whole of it is about four hours of riding and a clerk who does not know her name and will have to find it.** Aren Kest said **do not do that**, and gave the reason: **a page that tells a woman about her own road is a page that tells a man at the top of it what a road is worth, and there is a man in nine counties who can put a value on a gap and he does not need to know my name to do it, and I am not going to build a second one in a room at the second hour of the night on a Sunday.** **The road office clerk came down the lane at the third hour on the Monday, said before anybody spoke that he was not going to use the schedule, and said that the machinery was ready at seven in the morning and a man told it not to at two in the morning, and that the two of those were about five hours apart and neither of them knew the other was going to happen.** THE INTERVAL IS FIVE HOURS AND NOT NINE, the same speech's *two people in two days* is *inside one day*, and both were printed wrongly until a review of this block caught them.

1483. **There is no address, and the standing of four and a half years is the reason, and the reason is not going to be taken back.** In Chapter 0626, at the counter under the weigh-house on a Monday, the man of about thirty-four with a sheet said he wanted to put a name on his sheet and could not, and told the lane what a clerk in a district office said to him: **a line is not a road, and a number on a piece of paper is not a thing I can send a rider to.** The copyist of about twenty-six then said the thing she has never said out loud in four and a half years, and it is the block's fourth cost and it is new: **there is no address. Not for that road, and not for any of the seven. There is no list of keepers in nine counties, because a list of keepers is a list of roads and a road may not be described here until it has answered. That is the correct standing. It is why two contradictory warnings about the same four miles of channel sat in two books for three weeks. It is why the two in three of about nine hundred drying cloths was never counted. And it is why a man of about thirty-four with a sheet cannot write a name at the top of a piece of paper about a woman four days of carting away, and will not be able to tomorrow, and there is nobody he can go and be angry at.** Nobody said she was wrong. **The man of about fifty-eight with a badge said he had spent thirty-one years saying to rooms that there is no head in nine counties and that a rule needs one, and had never once had to say it about a woman, and is not going to pretend it is a comfort to anybody.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room said, from the door, and would not come into the lane, that the two of them were told not to put anything at the top of a note, and the reason is the second one is right.

1484. **The road goes quiet and there was never an arrangement by which anybody was told, and the finding is structural.** The closure is not an act anybody performs and it is not a document. **The only record of it in nine counties is the man of about thirty-four's own sheet with no head over it, on which he wrote in words that a road on the seventh line had been taken off the works under an abbey at the second hour of the night on the twenty-ninth of a month, on the word of a man with a limp, in a room with no bench in it, and that it was not a direction and had no head and was not in any book. He put no number on it, and the chapter gives his reason in his own reasoning and not in the narrator's, and it is the same reason as at the gate on the eighteenth: a man who has spent a month watching one figure get made out of another figure does not put a number on a thing that has been decided in a room by a man who cannot certify it. That was the fourth thing that had gone down that lane in his coat in about six weeks and it is not one of the three numbers and the chapter says so.** The sheet did not stop being kept and did not go into the room's book.

1485. **The woman of about forty finds out in a market, four days later, from a third party, and what she says is not a complaint.** **Nell Craddock, 40, came up the lane in Chapter 0624 on the twenty-eighth of Hearthmonth with a cart of quicklime and a sale and two of her own hands on the shafts, and nobody sent her, and nobody asked her anything, and that is the last time anybody in nine counties asked her anything.** She asked **Do you want it** at a counter and he did not answer, and she did not repeat it. **Four days later, on the third of Goatmonth, on a market morning, at about the second hour, she found out from a man of about thirty-one from the road office putting down a basket.** He had not looked at her and said it to the middle distance, in about nine words, and did not know whose road she kept, and said so when she asked, and the whole of what he could say was that the schedule carries a line and a number and a district and not names. **Nobody in nine counties had the means of telling her and the only person in the room with the information was a man with no reason to think she cared, and the reason there was no arrangement is item 1483 and not carelessness.** She said three things, and no one thanked her and nobody told her she was right. **One, that she wants that said first, before anything else, because she knows what is coming: nobody has been cruel to her and she wants it on the record. Two, that the answer to whether the count is to be kept is keep it, and that she is going to keep it, and that the boy is to go on having it, and that neither of those things is hers to command and she has just commanded it. Three, and it is the sentence the block is built on: *you have all been very careful, and there was nobody in nine counties who could have been uncareful, and that is not the same as anybody being careless and it is not a defence either and I am not offering anybody one; I am not saying you were cruel, I am saying it happened, it happened on a Sunday night in a room and it happened to a road and it happened to a boy of about eleven who has a count in his head that nobody has ever written down, and I have not got a word for the other thing, and I have been looking for one since Monday and I have not found it, and I am not going to make one up, and I would like it understood that I have not found one because there is not one and not because I am slow.*** **Aren Kest was about forty paces off in the market street from the second hour, and she had known where he was the whole time and had not looked at him and had not come over, and about nine people in that market did not know that and about four did.**

1486. **The cost, and it is not that anybody is hurt, and the volume does not say so in a way anybody can use.** In Chapter 0629, on the twenty-fourth of Goatmonth, twenty-five days after the closure, which is three weeks and four days and not three weeks, **the man of about thirty-four with a sheet asked aloud whether anybody in the room had been on the seventh line since the twenty-ninth of a month, and nobody had, and he then said that nobody was hurt, nothing had gone late, nothing had rotted and no wages had gone anywhere, because she is paid in kind and the kind is a season's slack — and the copyist of about twenty-six refused the good news in front of about nine people, and her reason is the volume's rule turned against itself and it is new.** **That is not a good report, that is the absence of a report, and in that room the two have never been the same. If a road produces no returns then a road can be shut for four years and nothing arrives, and a road that is doing perfectly well produces nothing either, and the two of them look identical in nine counties. And every single thing in that room is built so that a thing which looks identical in nine counties does not get set beside another thing. I made that rule, I have kept it for four years and a half, two of the sheets on it are a lie, and the rule has just cost a road twenty-five days of anybody knowing anything, and it will go on costing, and the whole of the cost of that is a woman of about forty who was four days of carting away and had nobody to ask.** The man of about thirty-one from the road office read the blank column as good news and was told he could not know that, and that in about four years somebody will shut a road that produces no returns on a blank in a schedule and put it in a report as a thing that was tried and did not cost anything, **and the road will not be there and the report will be true.** The badge-man then said the sentence the block needs: **nobody being hurt is the answer a man gives when he has not found the cost. It is not a finding. It is a gap with a comfortable shape in it.** And the copyist said the only cost she could find in twenty-five days is that it cost the woman the only thing she has, which is not being asked, **and that it is not a figure and it is not on anybody's page, and that she has been looking for a better one because a better one would have gone in a column.** Nobody thanked anybody.

1487. **It has happened three times, and the third time somebody acted on it, and nobody has asked him whether he wanted it.** In the same room on the same morning the badge-man set out the whole of it: **a culvert at the gate of this market town in a month past, where a man with a limp was the only person who could see a middle course that had gone and said it out loud twice to two people and neither of them was the road office; a crossing under an abbey on the twentieth of a month, where he was the only person there at the second hour of the night; and a room over a saddler's on the twenty-ninth, where this time he acted on it. Those are the three, in thirty-one years of the badge-man's watching, and in the first two the man was right, nobody was harmed, and nothing came of it at all.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote **three** on his own sheet with no head over it and it went down the lane in his coat, **in words and not as a figure, and said so when he was asked why, because it is not a measurement, and if he had written three in a column beside something else the room would have had a number to use by Friday.**

1488. **The recovery beat, and it is the volume's ordinary work and the volume's catastrophe in the same masonry.** In Chapter 0623, four days off, on the twenty-sixth of a month, a new sill in three courses was laid in lime at the crossing where the water had come over, and **the middle course went in perfectly and the man of about fifty who laid it sat down on the parapet and would not lay the fourth, and gave the reason to about four men at a crossing and not to the protagonist: the bottom course is bedded on the channel and cannot go anywhere, the top course is bedded on the one under it and carries the weight of the water every hour, and the one in the middle has nothing on it that would fall and nothing under it that would hold it. It is a good course. It is the best laid of the three. It is the one that will go. And nobody will ever ask me about a middle course, because a middle course is not a thing anybody thinks about: a bottom course is the ground, a top course is the water, and it is the one in between that has to be its own argument, and it has not got one.** He told a boy of about seventeen and not the protagonist, and he told the cart not to write the day down and there is no return in nine counties in which a man may write down how many stones he laid, and he has asked three times in his life and been told twice that there is no head to rule it on and once that there is no rule because nobody has ever thought of one. **Aren Kest held the string for three hours and said nothing at all, which is the whole of what he did and he did it on purpose, because a new sill in three courses is not work for a man with a bad leg on ground wet to the ankle and everybody at that crossing knew it including him.** The wages went into five hands on a sound stone, one of them a boy's and straight into a pocket, and nobody said it was well done. **The three courses are the culvert at the town's gate and the new sill and the seventh line, and the volume has now put the same masonry in all three places and has said so nowhere in any document.**

1489. **Hester Vane, the one name this block spent, and why.** **Hester Vane, 43, is the woman of about forty-three who at the ferry stone gate on the eighteenth of Hearthmonth said her line is the fifth, that her road will stand aside, and set the condition that the count be taken first by the people who walk it, on the ground, before her line is settled.** She appears once in this block, in Chapter 0629, and **she is named for the first time in nine counties by herself**, and the chapter says so on the page. **Her road's own people have taken the count. She came four days on foot to say that it was taken and that nothing came of it, and she says that in a room is the second worst place in nine counties to say it and a gate is the first, and she refused three times to give the count in a room and gave it to nobody, and said that what her road keeps is the crossings that are open and the weeks the road is shut, and that that has never once been a number of days in anybody's mouth.** **She said out loud, to the room and not to the copyist and not to the woman of about fifty, that nobody is to say her road was shut, because it was not, it stood aside, and that those are not the same and the page cannot tell them apart.** The woman of about fifty said in the same room and within about four seconds that it is not a thing that has been done before, and Hester Vane said to her that she is not asking anybody to cite hers. **Nobody said the two roads are the same road and nobody asked, and the fact that nobody asked was noted afterwards by about four people and not by about five, and the fifth-line road and the small road are not the same road and are nowhere on the page joined.** She is a person Block 0004 needs by name, which is why the block spent one on her.

1490. **The register's ninth entry, and it is a question and there is no finding under it.** In Chapter 0630 the copyist of about twenty-six asked **What goes in** and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room said say it and do not soften it, and the ninth entry is the question **whether a room with a door on a lane and a number and a deed of thirteen words may hold a thing that was decided in a room with no head over it, and if it may not, where that thing goes.** She answered both halves herself: **it may not, and the answer to the second half is a drawer about eleven inches deep, and it is not a place, it is a place so that a thing is not lost, and a room can hold a question and a room can hold a thing somebody has refused to sign, and what it cannot hold is a decision, because a decision that lands here has nowhere to be challenged, and a decision that cannot be challenged is not a decision, it is a thing that happened to nine people in a cold room at the second hour of a Sunday night. It goes in as a question and it stays a question and it is the ninth, and no finding goes under the ninth, and I would like the man who wrote it and the man who decided it both to be in the room when somebody tries to make it a finding.** **The register stands at nine entries, all of them questions, and none of them a finding. The day-book stands at nine. The press stands open with no date. The fourth head of the Crown's form is ruled and empty and about nine months empty and nobody has filled it and nobody may rule a fifth.** The badge-man said he would be in the room, and nobody thanked him.

1491. **The answer he gives to the question nobody asked, and the year turns on the morning he gives it.** In the lane at about the seventh hour on the first of Thawmonth YR 321, to about four people who could hear and about five who could not, **and nobody had asked and nobody in nine counties had ever asked: *I wanted it.*** He said it flatly and said what wanting it is not, and refused the comfort of it: **it is not a defence, and I would rather be a man who wanted a thing and knew that about nine households on a road are carrying what six were carrying, than a man who was asked whether he wanted it and said no and did it anyway.** THE FIGURE IS NINE AND NOT FOUR, established at `chapter-0606.md`, `chapter-0609.md` and `chapter-0625.md`, and Chapter 0630 printed four until a review of this block caught it; a successor must not print four and must not add the two nines together. The copyist of about twenty-six said from the doorway that it is not in the register and he said he would not want it to be, and said why: **if it went in there it would be a finding about a man and the register is for questions, and a man who has been asked afterwards and has answered afterwards has not been asked. A thing I say in a lane on a Thursday to four people who can hear me is not being asked. It is the other thing.** Nobody thanked him. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room came out of the door about twenty minutes after the stair and went past him and neither of them said anything, and that is the whole of what happened between them in a month, and nothing between them is described in a document.** **The year turned on that morning and nobody in the lane turned with it; the first thing written with the new year against it was a date in the corner of a roll book in the same hand, and the keeper of the room said out loud that she has watched two years turn and neither changed anything the room holds, and that no date on any paper in that room makes a road four days of carting away better or worse.** The block ends on a man answering a question nobody asked, in a lane about four hundred paces long, and nothing went into the register and nothing went down the lane in a coat.

1492. **The page, the blanks, and the second time round, and the ambiguity is untouched and was made harder on purpose.** **The six spaces stand exactly where they stood: three with a mark and three with nothing in them, and of the three blanks TWO are refusals and ONE is a line the page has not reached, and the page cannot tell which is which and no chapter of this volume may resolve it and none of these ten did.** **The page went round nine counties a second time in this block and came back with the same three marks and the same three blanks, and the man of about thirty-four with a sheet said in Chapter 0630 what he said in Chapter 0613 and it is the volume's instrument and not a new figure: on the third time round there will be a fourth blank, and it will be the first space on the sheet, and the fourth blank will be a man who has read it and would not, and there will be nothing whatever on that page to tell anybody so, and he has a piece of paper with no head over it in a satchel that says which one it was, and it goes down the lane in his coat at the seventh hour, and he is not going to be asked to prove it, and nobody asked him to prove it.** The copyist said the part that is new and that she wanted to be argued with: **a blank in a page is a thing that gets argued with, and she wanted something to be argued with for the first time in about two months and has not got a place to put it.** The man of about forty-seven said the thing that is the pressure on Block 0004 and 0005 and is the worst sentence in the block: **he is right four times and the fifth time there will not be a gap, and the thing that goes first will be a road with a number on it and a name against the number and a keeper, and he has not got a name for which one and he says he will not guess and he is not going to, because if he names a road the next page has a name at the top of it before it goes out and he will not be the man who hands nine counties a list.**

1493. **The money instrument, and the collision set, with the extractors and the free set printed, because a collision report without them is not a measurement.** **The prior set was rebuilt over the six hundred and twenty chapters that stood on disk before this block with THREE extractors** — one keyed on the item list's footing (*is ⟨words⟩ pence*), one on the restated enumeration (the numeral list immediately preceding the footing), and one on the mark conversion (*is ⟨n⟩ marks and ⟨m⟩ over*) — **and the three are, in that order: 470 instances / 91 distinct, 75 instances / 72 distinct, 131 instances / 120 distinct. The union is 676 instances and 208 distinct, and the set size is 208.** The four barred numerals thirty-six, fifty-two, sixty-one and sixty-six are at **0** in every total, over-figure and printed price in this block, **and the cost of that prohibition is that the free set those four leave is 207 out of the 208**, which is printed here because a claim of zero collisions without the free set beside it is no result. **The ten columns are 209, 221, 233, 241, 252, 268, 279, 296, 318 and 341 pence, ten distinct, and every one is clear of the 208-element set, and every one is at or above 202 and none of them is one of Batch 0001's 214 / 232 / 198 / 245 / 249 / 182 / 217 / 260 / 225 / 213 or Batch 0002's 202 / 215 / 229 / 234 / 243 / 251 / 262 / 271 / 283 / 294.** Each column is 9 to 11 items priced 1 to 41 pence, **each column's printed total equals the sum of its item list and equals its restated enumeration and equals its mark conversion, and all ten were checked three ways, and all thirty coda figure lists were checked item by item and every printed numeral equals its list.** **Collision with the 208-element prior set: 0 over ten totals. The 203-element set printed at 1472 was NOT rebuilt by this phase and is not re-asserted; 208 is what three extractors give over 620 chapters and the difference is the extractors and the chapter count.**

1494. **PARTIALLY SUPERSEDED BY 1495. What Batch 0004 inherits, measured on the files as they stand after this block's last edit, and the two bases named separately because they are not the same.** **BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file counted on its own and the ten added. Block 0003 WAS 26,731 AS THIS ITEM WAS WRITTEN AND IS 26,844 after this block's review repair, per chapter 2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,582 / 2,804 / 2,810, and a concatenated `cat` of the same ten files returns 26,844 and no chapter file is missing its trailing newline, so the per-file and concatenated bases agree.** **BASIS TWO, the scene body: everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which is the file total less the cost line less the money column paragraph, and which THEREFORE INCLUDES THE TITLE LINE AND THE DATELINE, so a body is not a scene body. The bodies ARE 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,011 / 2,188 / 2,207, minimum 1,756 at `chapter-0627.md` and maximum 2,970 at `chapter-0625.md`, and were 2,142 / 2,416 / 1,779 / 1,978 / 2,957 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 1,996 / 2,188 / 2,204, against Batch 0001's 1,362 to 1,871, Batch 0002's 1,698 to 2,939 and Volume 12's 1,161 to 2,095. Do not subtract the title line from a body; that gives a series wrong by exactly the dateline length in every chapter.** **Codas ARE 482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603, so 476 to 616, and the share of a chapter is a mean of 20.03 per cent over a range of 16.08 to 22.4, and the 16.08 is `chapter-0625.md` and is the right direction because the share falls as the body lengthens. This item first printed 482 / 500 / 458 / 476 / 548 / 519 / 507 / 565 / 616 / 603, so 458 to 616 at a mean of 19.9 over 15.6 to 22.4, and the two codas that moved are 0623 and 0625 and they moved because the review repair added absence clauses to their person lists.** This block therefore **held the body floor and let the coda run longer than Batch 0002's 408 to 484**, and share is a measured consequence of body length and is reported as such and not as a virtue. `about` IS **603 at 22.46** per 1,000, case-insensitive whole-word, against 602 at 22.52 as this item was written. **The thank family is 40 and all forty were read in context and every one is a negation or is a plain statement that a person was not thanked or was not thanked for something, and there is no exception.** Curly doubles **284 and 284** with 0 straight doubles, **0** curly apostrophes and 45 straight apostrophes, **0** em dashes and 0 en dashes, 20 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons. **`Aren Kest` is in every one of the ten and is 13 / 3 / 2 / 3 / 10 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 4, and the low figures are the chapters in which he is at a crossing four days off or is standing in a lane about four hundred paces short of the boards, and a name in a chapter a man is not in is a false claim about a person.** `Mara Vey` at 0 and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room is never named. **Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more, the ten new against the six hundred and twenty before and against themselves: 0 and 0, recounted after the review repair and unchanged by it. THE RUN CLAIM IN THIS ITEM WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE AND THE CORRECTION IS THE POINT. The longest CHAIN of consecutive shared eight-word windows against the six hundred and twenty before is 36 windows, which is forty-three words, at `chapter-0624.md` in the cost coda's own person list, and it is a CORPUS measure and not a file-pair run: it chains across fourteen of the six hundred and twenty prior files and no single one of them carries it, `chapter-0624.md` against `chapter-0604.md` is a longest contiguous word-run of 24 on the same whitespace token, and the largest chain any one prior file carries is 19 windows, which is twenty-five words, against `chapter-0609.md`. The rest of the field is 29 at `chapter-0625.md` against `chapter-0620.md`, a single partner, on the same man recalling the same reason for the same act; 25 at `chapter-0629.md` against `chapter-0619.md`, also a single partner, the same woman using the same standing phrase about the same two categories; then 25 at 0625, 24 at 0623, 23 at 0628 and 23 at 0625. The volume record before this block was 42 on the chain basis and this block's longest is 36, and the paragraph-level instrument, which is the one that can see a lifted sentence, returns 0.** Volumes 01 to 12 recount to **1,681,701 in six hundred chapters and do not move**, Batch 0001 to **20,333**, Batch 0002 to **24,843**, Batch 0003 to **26,844**, **Volume 13 TO 72,020 in thirty chapters and the manuscript TO 1,753,721 in six hundred and thirty**, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,844 reproduces it. **The collision set, the name sweep and the eight filler families were all re-run on the ten new files: a capitalised two-word sweep returns `Aren Kest`, `Garvin Slade`, `Hester Vane`, `Nell Craddock` and two line-wrap artefacts and nothing else, and the twenty-five barred terms are at 0 across all ten.**

1495. **The review of Volume 13 Batch 0003, run by a phase that wrote none of the block, thirteen findings and one the review itself got wrong, and the fourteen repairs, and the two instruments this block had to be taught twice. This item is the authority for Batch 0003 and 1494 is read with it.** A review of `novel: save writer work batch-0003` raised thirteen findings, three of them high, marked six things checked and not defects, and certified the twenty-five barred terms at 0. **The first three mattered for one reason and not for three: each contradicted the block's own central arithmetic AND each had already been copied into `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, so the next writer would have inherited all three as settled fact.** Nothing in this repair changed the plot, a dateline, a beat, a motive or the planned ending, and no chapter was restarted.

**One, THE WORKS' CAPACITY WAS PRINTED AS BOTH SEVEN AND SIX, and canon is seven.** `chapter-0607.md`, `chapter-0621.md` and `chapter-0622.md` all say the works will take seven and has taken seven, and `chapter-0622.md` already holds the reconciliation in its own words: *There is one thing the works will not take and the thing it will not take has to come off.* Against that, `chapter-0625.md` said **will take six and there are seven on it**, `chapter-0627.md` said **it will take six, and it took six before anybody in this county was born** in the same rhetorical construction and with the same *before anybody in this county* pivot that negates 0622, and `chapter-0630.md` put both versions in one unmediated scene with Slade saying **it will take six** at the third hour and the keeper of the room saying **The seven is still seven** in the last speech of the block. **A two-level reading existed and no chapter ever made it. IT IS MADE NOW: the works will take seven, seven roads go through it and seven were on it, and the seventh is the one thing on it the works will not take, because it has no figure and no name and nobody whose job it is; taking it off leaves six, which is inside what the works will take, and the six marked to be held hold.** Repaired on that footing in five places and no plot moved: 0621's *carrying more than it will take* now says what the more than is; 0625's speech says *a set of works that will take seven and there are seven on it, and there is one thing on it the works will not take*; 0625's consequence says *six is inside what it will take*; 0627 says *it will take seven, and it took seven before anybody in this county was born*; 0630's Slade says *it will take seven*; and the keeper of the room's **The seven is still seven** is now **The seventh is still off the works**, which is a claim about the status of a line and not a second claim about a capacity.

**Two, CHAPTER 0628 COULD NOT HAVE HAPPENED AS WRITTEN, and this was the block's emotional climax.** The closure is the second hour of the night on Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth, day 1924, and Chapter 0628 is Goatmonth the third, day 1928, four days later. The nearest preceding Friday is Hearthmonth the twenty-seventh, day 1922, **two days before the closure**, and four lines put Nell Craddock's knowledge, her two visits up the slope, and the copyist's thinking there, and a fifth put the clerk's interval at *two days and a bit* for four. **The concealment beat, twice telling the woman at the top of the slope she had heard nothing, only works if she already held something, and the only thing she can hold without anybody having told her anything is a belief, and a belief is enough to make what she said a lie and that is what the cost coda's lie told twice names.** Repaired on that footing: the clerk says **Four days**; the slope is **twice up there since Monday**, which is Hearthmonth the thirtieth, the morning after; her account of it is **I had not been told a thing and I said no on the strength of a woman of about sixty asking me twice**; her second distinction is **I have known since Monday that something had come off those works, and I have not been told**; the copyist has been thinking about the consequence **since Sunday night**; and the word she has been looking for she has been looking for **since Monday**. Four days of carting is kept, the beat survives with its whole weight, and no date in the chapter is now earlier than the closure.

**Three, CHAPTER 0629'S DAY COUNT WAS SHORT BY FOUR.** Hearthmonth the twenty-ninth is day 1924 and Goatmonth the twenty-fourth is day 1949 and the interval is twenty-five days. The chapter printed **twenty-one days** in its second sentence and **three weeks** in five places and **nine days ago** in the badge-man's account of the third of the three things that have happened. All eight now read **twenty-five days** — in the narration, in the speech, in the cost line, in the coda's line, and in the coda's own time list, which still foots at fourteen figures. **Three weeks and one day was the figure this block's own chapter summary carried and is also wrong; the correct gloss is three weeks and four days.**

**Four, `chapter-0630.md` said four households and the figure is nine.** Established three times before Batch 0003: *called Nell Craddock by about nine households* at 0606, *about nine houses at the top end and about nine at the bottom* at 0609, *about nine houses at one end and about nine at the other* at 0625. Now **about nine households on a road**.

**Five, `chapter-0626.md` said the machinery was ready nine hours after it was refused.** The refusal is at two in the morning on the night of the twenty-ninth and the machinery is ready at seven the same morning, which is **about five hours**; the same speech said two people had told him **in two days** when the second of them spoke at that counter that morning, now **inside one day**. The coda's time list carries **about five hours** in place of about nine hours and still foots at eleven.

**Six, FOUR STATE SENTENCES PRINTED VOLUME 13 AS 71,897 AND EACH CONTRADICTED ITS OWN ARITHMETIC,** because 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,731 is 71,907. Corrected on the first pass of this repair and then superseded again by the recount below.

**Seven, `state/open-threads.md` 473 put day 1998 at the seventeenth of Mudmonth,** where 1986 is the first of Mudmonth YR 321 and 1998 is the **thirteenth**, which is what `batch-0004/PROMPT.md` and `state/current.md` already said, and the same sentence then named 1986 and 2016 as the month starts inside Block 0004 and said of one of them that it was outside the block. Block 0004 has **one** month start inside it, at 1986, and 2016 is outside it. **The calendar itself is sound. Every weekday and day-of-month in Batch 0003 derives correctly off day 930, the ten datelines map to 1915 / 1916 / 1921 / 1923 / 1924 / 1925 / 1926 / 1928 / 1949 / 1956 with the claimed weekdays and market flags, and Block 0004's range, its single month start and its market mornings all derive correctly. What was wrong was a derived figure copied into a summary and a self-cancelling set, not the chain.**

**Eight, THE THIRTY-SIX-WORD RUN WAS ATTRIBUTED TO A FILE PAIR IT DOES NOT BELONG TO, and this is the finding most worth a successor's minute.** The figure reproduces and the wording does not. On a whitespace-only normaliser, sliding an eight-word window one token at a time, **the longest chain of consecutive shared windows against the six hundred and twenty prior files is 36 windows, which is forty-three words, and it is at `chapter-0624.md` in the cost coda's own person list — but it chains across fourteen of the six hundred and twenty files and no single one of them carries it. `chapter-0624.md` against `chapter-0604.md` is a longest contiguous word-run of 24, on a whitespace token with punctuation retained, stripped, or case preserved, and the largest chain any one prior file carries is 19 windows, which is twenty-five words, against `chapter-0609.md`.** The item's own phrase, *eight consecutive whitespace-delimited words*, describes an eight-word WINDOW and that is where the confusion started. **A successor chasing 36 against 0604 will not find it, and the class is general: a chain measured against a corpus and a run measured against a file are two measurements and this project has printed the first in the second's clothes.** The 29 and the 25 that this block does name are both single-partner and are sound.

**Nine, `chapter-0622.md`'s badge-man opened on two spans with no connective,** *Thirty-one years* followed by *I have put four and a half years of other people's figures into rooms*. `chapter-0626.md` uses the same opening correctly, which is how the carried fragment was identified. Now **And for four and a half of them I have been putting other men's figures into that room**: both spans kept, the connective supplied, and the sentence no longer asserts that he spent thirty-one years inside a standing the room acquired four and a half years ago.

**Ten, `chapter-0624.md`'s second clause had no verb,** *I am telling you I heard it and not that I have come to be told*, and the volume's own move is *I am not saying X, I am saying Y.* Now **I am saying I heard it and I am not saying I have come here to be told**.

**Eleven, AN UNRECORDED BODY ECHO, 21 WORDS, 0626 AGAINST 0628.** The man of about thirty-four's speech in both chapters carries *and the other five I could not put anywhere, and I have never once thought about the five before, and I*, with the clause after it swapped: in 0626 he adds *I was not thinking about her this morning at seven o'clock* and in 0628 *I am thinking about one of them now*. **It sits under this block's twenty-two word line, which is why the sweep did not name it, and it is the same man making the same argument with the same figure four days apart, so it is an echo and not a lift. It is now recorded here, it survives the repair above because 0626 was not re-voiced and 0628's echo stands in a line the repair did not touch, and the paragraph-level instrument still returns 0 and 0.**

**Twelve, `state/current.md` item thirteen read as exhaustive where it was a selection.** It listed Aren Kest as being in the lane in 0624, 0627, 0629 and 0630. He is in the lane in 0626 as well, walking past the weigh-house at the fourth hour about four hundred paces short of the counter, and he is about forty paces off in a market street in 0628. All six are now named and the item says it is the whole of the block and not a selection.

**Thirteen, THREE COST CODAS NAMED PERSONS WHO ARE NOT IN THE CHAPTER WITHOUT SAYING SO, and that is the house convention and not a slip.** `chapter-0623.md` listed the copyist, the man of about forty-seven with a page and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room; `chapter-0625.md` listed the woman of about forty who runs a lime kiln; `chapter-0628.md` listed the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room. 0625 already ended its own list *a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a room and was not there*, so the convention is to mark the absence in the list. **The repair applies the convention and does not replace it, and the counts of eleven, fourteen and fourteen figures of persons do not move.** A fourteenth instance of the same class was found by reading and not by the review: **0625's coda closed *the fourteenth of them was not in the room and has not been told and will find out in a market*, and the person who will find it out in a market is the eleventh and not the fourteenth.** Corrected; the fourteenth now says only that she was not in that room.

**Fourteen, AND THIS ONE THE REVIEW CERTIFIED AS CLEAN AND IT WAS NOT, which is why it is recorded at the length of the other thirteen.** `chapter-0625.md`'s money column totalled **two hundred and fifty-two pence**, and that spelling contains the barred numeral `fifty-two` inside the number itself. This is the defect `state/continuity.md` 1336 repaired in an earlier volume by re-ordering a column that totalled one hundred and fifty-two, and it is the defect `workspace/volume-13/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` forbids in *every total, over-figure and printed price*. The review listed all twenty-five barred terms at 0 across the ten files and a whitespace-token whole-word sweep finds `fifty-two` at 2. **THE RULE THIS PROJECT PRINTS AND THEN BREAKS: FOR A BARRED FIGURE, CHANGE THE COLUMN AND NEVER THE PRINTED TOTAL.** So the column was re-ordered by moving one item price from two pence to three, the printed total followed the items to **two hundred and fifty-three pence**, the mark conversion is five marks and thirteen over, the ten prices are still ten distinct prices between 1 and 41 with no price of thirty-six, and two hundred and fifty-three is not in the six hundred and twenty chapters before this block. **Six closed-volume files also print a total whose spelling contains a barred numeral — 0117, 0133, 0138, 0141 and 0143 in Volume 03 and 0401 in Volume 09 — and they are recorded here and not repaired, because a closed volume is not repaired on a hunch and because removing a word from a correct column to keep a ledger tidy is editing and not auditing.** The twenty-five barred terms are at 0 across the ten new files after this repair, recounted, and that zero is the second time this block has been wrong about it.

**The recount after the repair, every basis named, and it is 1494 with the wrong figures beside the right ones.** **BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file on its own and the ten added. Block 0003 is 26,844, per chapter 2,652 / 2,918 / 2,260 / 2,456 / 3,539 / 2,560 / 2,263 / 2,582 / 2,804 / 2,810, and a concatenated `cat` of the same ten files returns 26,844. BASIS TWO, the scene body, which includes the title line and the dateline and is therefore not a scene body: 2,170 / 2,418 / 1,779 / 1,980 / 2,970 / 2,041 / 1,756 / 2,011 / 2,188 / 2,207, minimum 1,756 at `chapter-0627.md` and maximum 2,970 at `chapter-0625.md`, and the volume's floor of 1,161 is not approached. Codas 482 / 500 / 481 / 476 / 569 / 519 / 507 / 571 / 616 / 603, so 476 to 616, at a mean share of 20.03 per cent over 16.08 to 22.4. `about` 603 at 22.46 per 1,000. Thank family 40, all forty negations or plain statements that a person was not thanked, recounted and unchanged. Curly doubles 284 and 284, 0 straight doubles, 0 curly apostrophes, 45 straight apostrophes, 0 em dashes, 0 en dashes, 20 question marks, 0 exclamation marks, 0 semicolons. `Aren Kest` 13 / 3 / 2 / 3 / 10 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 1 / 4 and in every one of the ten, and the series is unchanged by this repair: the low figures are still the chapters in which he is at a crossing four days off or standing in a lane about four hundred paces short of the boards, and a name in a chapter a man is not in is a false claim about a person. `Mara Vey` 0. `Hester Vane` 5, `Nell Craddock` 2, `Garvin Slade` 10. Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more: 0 and 0. Longest chain of consecutive shared eight-word windows against the six hundred and twenty prior files: 36, at 0624, a corpus chain across fourteen files, with 29 and 25 the two named single-partner recurrences. Money: 209 / 221 / 233 / 241 / 253 / 268 / 279 / 296 / 318 / 341, ten distinct, each footing three ways, colliding zero times with the 208-element set and the four barred numerals at 0. Volumes 01 to 12 recount to 1,681,701 in six hundred chapters and do not move, Batch 0001 to 20,333, Batch 0002 to 24,843, Batch 0003 to 26,844, Volume 13 to 72,020 in thirty, the manuscript to 1,753,721 in six hundred and thirty, and 1,681,701 + 20,333 + 24,843 + 26,844 reproduces it.**

**And the four questions a successor should ask of the next block, three of which this block asked of itself and got wrong.** **One, can a figure on a page be read against the page? Three of the fourteen defects were a claim about a date or a number read against the dateline that produced it, and no sweep on this project sees one of them. Two, is a measurement printed with its normaliser AND its scope? Two of the fourteen were a measurement printed without one and then given a partner it did not have, and the project's own history has now had four instruments return their own seed length or two long runs and call them the maximum. Three, does a certificate get checked the way a sentence does? One of the fourteen was certified clean by the very review that found thirteen others, and the barred numeral was inside a money total that had been footed three ways and checked against a 208-element collision set. Four, and this is the standing one: the four and a half years of the room's standing, the absence of any means of telling anybody, the register at nine questions with no finding under any of them, the seventh line still off the works, the six marked to be held still holding for a season, and the fact that the year turned on the last morning of this block and nobody in that lane turned with it are all unchanged by anything in this item.**

## Volume 13, Batch 0004 — Chapters 631 to 640, "What the Room Owes a Road", items 1496 to 1503, and the highest number in this file is now 1503

**Appended by the phase that wrote Volume 13 Batch 0004. Items 1479 to 1495 stand under their own numbers. This section names the antagonist nowhere, prints no third of the barred kin-term, prints the word that names the founding paper nowhere, prints no System panel and no stage word, and contains no chapter of new prose.**

1496. **The frame, derived and not copied, and the ten days.** The single anchor is **day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday**, on the carried thirty-day month, and the day-of-month half and the weekday half were derived separately from the number and never from one another. **Block 0004 is days 1957 to 1998 inclusive, forty-two days, that is the second of Thawmonth YR 321 to the thirteenth of Mudmonth YR 321, and every day of it is YR 321 because the year turned on day 1956, which was the last day of Batch 0003.** It has **exactly one first of a month inside it, 1986**, the first of Mudmonth YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning, and Chapter 0640 is on it and the badge-man says the hearing stands in the street on it. **Block 0005 is days 1999 to 2040 inclusive, forty-two days.** The ten chapter days are **1958, 1961, 1965, 1970, 1972, 1973, 1976, 1977, 1978 and 1986**, and their datelines are, in order: **Thawmonth the third, a Saturday and a market morning, the second hour in the market street and the third hour in a room over a weigh-house; Thawmonth the sixth, a Tuesday and not a market morning, the fourth hour at the counter boards with the room shut behind them; Thawmonth the tenth, a Saturday and a market morning, the second hour with the door open, two pieces of paper read out loud; Thawmonth the fifteenth, a Thursday and a market morning, nine days given out loud; Thawmonth the seventeenth, a Saturday and a market morning, a man of about fifty-two who goes home; Thawmonth the eighteenth, a Sunday and not a market morning, a bridge a day and a half off; Thawmonth the twenty-first, a Wednesday and not a market morning, the fifth hour at the boards; Thawmonth the twenty-second, a Thursday and a market morning, three clauses written out nine times in nine hands; Thawmonth the twenty-third, a Friday and not a market morning, the ninth of nine days; and Mudmonth the first, YR 321, a Saturday and a market morning and the first of a month.** **Six of the ten are market mornings and four are not, and the four that are not are a Tuesday, a Sunday, a Wednesday and a Friday, so the room over the weigh-house was shut on all four and no chapter of this block puts anything in that room on any of them, and each of those four chapters says on the page that the room is shut and will not be opened and that the argument is therefore in the open.** `outline/volume-13.md` §17's printed table was not used for either half and it is wrong in five rows.

1497. **The notice, and how a room that had spent four and a half years proving it could not hold a decision was asked to hold a rule instead, and what it did.** The badge-man had no head with which to call a meeting and said so out loud in the market street at about the second hour on a Saturday market morning, to nobody in particular, and put no name at the top of anything and said he would not. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room said in front of about nine people that the room is shut on every day but a market morning and that she will not have it opened for nine people on a Tuesday because nine people asked nicely, and that the register is at nine questions with no finding under any of them and that the ninth has been standing unanswered for about a month and she is not going to be handed a second question before the first is dealt with.** The copyist of about twenty-six's condition, given before anybody sat down: **she will keep the three series and will not merge them and will not derive one out of another and will not write a rule in a book of questions, and she will read out in this room with the door open anything written and sent to this room, and it will go nowhere else.** On the second morning of the argument the copyist said the thing the whole block turns on and said it as a condition on the artefact itself: **nine people will each write the three clauses in their own hand and there is not going to be a second copy of anything and she is not going to be the one who holds it, and the reason is that a decision that lands here has nowhere to be challenged and a rule that landed here would be a thing nine people had each got to themselves and that nobody could bring in here and hold up, so it goes in nine hands.** Nobody said anything on the boards about it. The register stood at nine and no finding went under any of them and nothing was written in it in this block.

1498. **The three clauses, in their finished form, and the nine days they were argued over, which are day 1970 to day 1978 and are Chapter 0634 to Chapter 0639.** **Clause one: no road is shut until the man who proposes to shut it has been to the end of that road and has said it out loud, in words that road uses, in a place where somebody who works that road can hear him, and on a day that road is being used.** The last four words are the man of about thirty-four with a sheet's and he gave the reason that a clause that can be complied with on a day the road is shut is a clause that will be complied with. **Clause two: no road is shut without a witness standing on that road at the time, and the witness is named by the people who work that road and not by the man proposing to shut it, and if that witness cannot be found afterwards then the road is open.** **Clause three: the burden of showing that a road may be shut lies with the man who says it may be shut, and if that man cannot be named and found afterwards then nothing has been shown and the road is open.** The nine days were given by the badge-man on the fifteenth of Thawmonth with the reason for the figure, which was that the longest thing anybody in nine counties had ever agreed to was about nine days and it did not hold. **Nobody thanked him and the copyist wrote the figure on a sheet and did not put it in a book and the man of about thirty-four wrote it on his own sheet with no head over it and did not make a tally of it, on the grounds that a count of days is not a measurement of anything anybody had done.** The room's own summary of what a rule is and where it goes is at `chapter-0639.md`: **a rule is not a decision, and that is the whole difference, and it is worth a lane about four hundred paces long. A decision has nowhere to be challenged. Nine of you have each got a piece of paper and every one of you can be asked about it by anybody at any time, including the ninth of you, including the copyist, including the man with the badge. And that is why nine is a good number and it is not because nine is a good number.**

1499. **The imperfection, named on the eighth of the nine days by a person who is not the protagonist, and it is the answer the outline asks for and it is also the block's last page.** A woman of about thirty-six who keeps a road in the county where a list of names was burned: **this rule covers roads that can be written to. Every one of those three clauses requires somebody to be found. The notice requires somebody to be reached at the end of a road, the witness requires somebody to be named by the people who work that road, and the burden requires a man who can be found afterwards. A road that cannot be written to has nobody at the end of it and nobody working it and no man in nine counties who could be found. And a rule about finding people is not a rule about roads. It is a rule about roads that have people on them at the end. And in my county there was a list of names and it was burned, and I have never in my life been able to work out whether the men who burned it hated anybody, and what I have come to after about twenty years is that they did not hate anybody and it was a sheet. A sheet with good manners on it is still a sheet.** Nobody argued with her and four people in that room agreed and five did not and none of them said so. **The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote it out in about nine words and it went down the lane in a coat in a satchel, and the man of about forty-seven asked for it to be written down and said he wanted it in the world and not in a book, and nobody thanked him.**

1500. **The road that went quiet, and it is not got back, and no chapter of this volume may say which road it was.** A man of about fifty-two who keeps a road and **will not name it**, and gave the reason in a lane on the second morning: the moment one of them has the name of it they have a road with a name against it and everything that happens to it gets argued about on a piece of paper instead of on the ground where it happens. **He put his chair back under the table himself on the third of the nine days, said he had four days of road in front of him, and said he would be back on the ninth day. He was not there on the seventh and the copyist said it out loud and would not make anybody alarmed. He was not there on the ninth.** On the eighth of the nine days a carter put a load down at the low end of the boards and said to the middle distance that nothing had come from the end of it, nothing in and nothing out, not a boy and not a note and not a basket of anything, since the middle of last week. **About nine people said something at once. The badge-man asked which road once and did not ask twice and gave the reason that a man who asks twice is a man who has begun to want an answer, and said afterwards that he had wanted an answer since about the second hour and had not been able to do one single thing with it and that the wanting was the part he had to be careful of and not the asking.** The woman of about fifty, who keeps a road nineteen years, said one sentence about that and did not expand it: **you want to know which road it is because you would like to be able to send somebody, and that is not the badge talking.** **They tried for about two hours on the ninth day and could not find out.** The road office man offered a rider inside four days to any of seven roads and the copyist refused twice, and her reason is the same both times and is the block's structural finding: **you know seven and you know that one of them has gone quiet and there is nothing in nine counties that joins those two facts and there is not going to be one and I am not saying it to be cold, I am saying it because I am the person who writes things down in this room and if anybody in this lane wants to know what I am going to write down today it is nine clauses on nine pieces of paper and one carter and that is all.** **And she said why she did not write his road down, twice, and said she would not have written it down anyway: if she had put the name of a road in a book because a man standing on wet boards said a word to her, then the only thing anybody in nine counties would have about that road would be a line, and a line is a thing you can put beside another line. She did not take the standing back and she said she was not going to take it back and nobody told her she was wrong.** **The badge-man told her twice that she was right and said the second time that he has spent thirty-one years telling rooms that there is no head in nine counties and that a rule needs one, and has just said to about nine people that the standing which cost a road its address is the reason a man is not in a lane on the ninth of nine days, and that both are true, and that he has been waiting about thirty-one years to pay a bill in that shape and it arrived on a Friday in the open and nobody was cruel to anybody and a road went quiet anyway.** The man of about thirty-four with a sheet wrote, on his own sheet with no head over it and in words and not as a figure, that a man of about fifty-two had said he would be back on the ninth day and had not been there on the seventh and had put his chair back himself, and said that the only place that sentence can go is into a satchel and down four hundred paces of lane. **AT THE CLOSE OF THE BLOCK, eight days after the ninth day, nothing has come from that road, nobody in nine counties knows which road it is, no clause of the rule reaches it, Chapter 0640 says so, and it says that no chapter after that one is going to say which road it was.** It is not the small road and the small road's letter is on the table in the room, and no chapter of this block names it or came near it.

1501. **The two roads that sent nobody, both in writing, both on the page, and the small road appears only as a handwriting.** **The coast of returning roads wrote that it will not send anybody, that the only person among them who could be spared for the length of an argument is the one who holds the post, and that a post does not get left; that anything written and sent to the post will be read on the day it came and will not be answered, and that the coast would rather say that now in its own hand than be asked in about a month for an answer it could not give; that it thought the notice clause right and that it would not be able to do anything with any of the three; and it ended with a sentence about a gate and about what a gate is for.
